## 0.1 The absolute constraint, restated as an operating rule

**[A]** The brief imposes an absolute constraint, and the corpus independently imposes the same one. Both are adopted here without qualification.

> **[S-F: Q19]** — *"An ENTITY-STRUCTURE DECISION PAPER, for the founder/CEO, putting the options to qualified Indian corporate and tax counsel. **Not an answer — the corpus forbids inventing one** — but the artifact that converts **A-B1 / LDR-U08** from `UNRESOLVED` into `DECIDABLE`."*
>
> **[S-D: LDR-U08]** — *"Section 8 company / trust / society / other / two-entity structure — **the corpus states the options and refuses to choose.**"*
>
> **[S: App. A §3.5]** — the National Team *"cannot … **finalise legal structure**"*; the Founder/CEO cannot approve alone *"**final legal/tax conclusions without professionals**."*
>
> **[S-A: Annexure A]** — *"Any legal, tax, insurance or privacy conclusion asserted? **No.** All appear as questions for professionals with **[V]**, or as cited external facts with **[X]**."*

**[P] Rule G-1 (No Structure Selection).** This instrument may not state or imply that Sewak Sathi Sanstha should be registered as, converted into, or operated under any particular legal form. Where an option is described as attractive, the description is a **source quotation** or a **labelled analysis**, never a recommendation of this instrument. **The output chain is fixed: OPTION SET → CONSEQUENCE ANALYSIS → PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION → HUMAN DECISION.**

**[P] Rule G-2 (No Cell Without An Anchor).** Every compatibility, consequence or trade-off statement in Parts III–XXII is either **(i)** a quotation or paraphrase of a corpus statement with its source ID, **(ii)** an analysis labelled **[A]** derived only from such statements, **(iii)** an external fact labelled **[X]** carried forward from the corpus with its own `[V]` marker intact, or **(iv)** `UNKNOWN FROM CURRENT CORPUS`. **No fourth category exists. A cell with no anchor reads `UNKNOWN`.**

**[P] Rule G-3 (Option Labels Are Not Recommendations).** **[S: App. A §3.2]** contains a column headed *"Recommendation status."* Its entries — *"Option for early internal pilot only after validation"*, *"Strong candidate for business network — requires professional structuring"*, *"May be suitable for social-impact arm — requires legal/tax advice"*, *"Recommended for professional exploration; not approved until designed"* — are **reproduced verbatim in Part III as source text**. They are **pre-decisional exploration statuses written before any of the four pillars had been costed, liability-tested or brand-tested**, and the same row that says *"strong candidate"* also says *"requires professional structuring."* **Rule G-1 applies to them. They are not decisions and this instrument does not adopt them.**

**[P] Rule G-4 (Conflicts Are Recorded, Not Reconciled).** Inherited from **[S-F: Rule F-28]**. Where two source statements cannot both be satisfied, both are printed with their IDs and the conflict is entered in the **GC-series** at §I.5. **No conflict is closed here.**

**[P] Rule G-5 (The A-N16 Standing Block).** The bank-mandate blocker is **preserved in full at Part IX §IX.7** and is restated in the completeness audit. **Unless and until the source corpus demonstrates that A-N16 / A-B9 / BLK-A-03 / SR-03 has been closed by an authorised human decision, this instrument treats it as open.** No bank mandate, signatory set, payment approval route or account architecture is invented here.

**[P] Rule G-6 (No Invented Quantities).** Inherited from **[S-F: completeness audit]**. **No rupee value, percentage, ratio, threshold, interval, headcount, premium, professional fee, minimum-signatory count, retention period, application-of-income figure, paid-up capital, compliance hour or membership number is created by this instrument.** The only quantities reproduced are the corpus's own, each with its label: `NOT FINAL`, `PROPOSED`, `ILLUSTRATIVE`, `UNKNOWN — MEASURE IN PILOT`, or `[X]` external citation requiring validation.

**[P] Rule G-7 (`UNKNOWN` Is A Permitted Answer, And A Useful One).** Where the corpus is silent, the entry reads `UNKNOWN — REQUIRES <discipline> COUNSEL` or `UNKNOWN FROM CURRENT CORPUS`. **A decision paper that fills every cell is a decision paper that has invented a structure.**

**[P] Rule G-8 (No Permission To Operate Informally).** Part XXII separates what cannot proceed from what *"can proceed only as an informal/non-organisational experiment if counsel permits."* **The second category is a description of a possibility that counsel must evaluate; it is not authorisation, not advice and not consent.** **[S-C: HV-6]** is the source of the question, not the answer.

**[P] Rule G-9 (This Instrument Mints Only `G-`, `EP-`, `GPD-`, `GC-`, `UE-`, `FC-`, `CS-`, `CB-`, `HY-`, `WC-`, `WCQ-`, `BK-`, `GVQ-`, `SUC-`, `LB-`, `INS-`, `TX-`, `DT-`, `IPE-`, `DS-`, `PV-`, `DM-`, `MD-`, `PQ-` Series).** Each labels **this document's** rows, rules and findings only, and **never replaces a source ID.** Where a v1.3-G row restates a source item, the source ID governs and appears first.

## 0.2 Evidence and status labelling (carried forward unchanged)

**[S]** The five v1.2 status labels are used throughout exactly as defined at **[S: App. A §Document Control]**:

| Label | Meaning | How used here |
|---|---|---|
| `APPROVED ARCHITECTURE` | A decision already present in the source | Reproduced; **not re-examined** |
| `PROPOSED / REQUIRES FINAL APPROVAL` | A recommended design where the source is incomplete | Reproduced as a proposal; **never treated as decided** (**[S-F: Rule F-9]**) |
| `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION` | A matter involving law, tax, employment, finance, safeguarding, safety, insurance, privacy or regulated operations | Reproduced with the validating discipline named |
| `UNRESOLVED` | No answer exists in the corpus | **Preserved. Never closed here** |
| `FUTURE IMPLEMENTATION` | Deferred by the source | Reproduced with its deferral basis |

**[S]** Evidence tags, as used across v1.3-A…v1.3-F and adopted unchanged:

| Tag | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **[S]** | Directly stated in a source instrument |
| **[A]** | Analysis of source statements — no new fact asserted |
| **[P]** | Proposal of this or a source instrument — `PROPOSED`, no operative force |
| **[C]** | Derived/computed from source statements by stated reasoning |
| **[I]** | Inference — plausible, labelled, not evidence |
| **[V]** | `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION` |
| **[X]** | External fact cited **by the corpus**, carried forward with its own `[V]` intact. **No legal conclusion is drawn from any [X] in this document** |
| **[M]** | Assumption or illustrative value, labelled at point of use |
| **[H]** | Hypothesis requiring test |
| **[SC]** | Scenario — not a forecast |

**[P] Rule G-10 (No `[X]` Promotion).** Every `[X]` item in this instrument — DPDP timelines and penalties, the 12A application-of-income and political-activity conditions, 80G cash-donation limits, FCRA track-record and dedicated-account requirements, BNSS 2023 ss.31/40/43 — is reproduced **as the corpus reproduced it**: an external proposition requiring confirmation by a qualified professional **for the actual entity, the actual activity and the actual jurisdiction**. **[S-A: Annexure C preamble]**: *"No legal conclusion is drawn from any of them in this document."* **Nothing here asserts that any registration produces any benefit, or that any activity is exempt.**

## 0.4 Where this instrument sits in the corpus's own lifecycle

**[A]** **[S-F: §0.2]** defines the lifecycle: `UNRESOLVED DECISION → PROFESSIONAL / LEGAL VALIDATION → BUILD / IMPLEMENT → PILOT TEST → EVIDENCE REVIEW → PASS / MODIFY / BLOCK → EXPANSION ELIGIBILITY`.

```
LDR-U08 LEGAL STRUCTURE UNDER KRYTOS
  |
  |   [S-F: Q19, SR-01]  "the single next artifact"
  v
+---------------------------------------------------------------------+
|  v1.3-G  —  THIS INSTRUMENT                                          |
|  Moves LDR-U08 from  UNRESOLVED  to  DECIDABLE.                      |
|  It does NOT move it to DECIDED, and it cannot:                      |
|  a decision paper is not a decision.                                 |
+---------------------------------------------------------------------+
  |
  |   authorised human decision-maker(s) + qualified Indian professionals
  v
DECIDED  ->  PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION (V-01, V-04, V-05, V-10 …)
  ->  BUILD (BLK-C-01 entity and governing documents; BLK-C-06 bank mandate)
  ->  GATE P0  ->  PILOT
```

**[A]** The distinction matters and is the whole subject of **[S-F: Part XVIII]**: **producing this paper is a State 1 act.** It is the *correct next* State 1 act — **[S-F: SR-01]** names it as the next action — but filing it instead of using it makes it the seventeenth row of **[S-F: §XVIII.2]**. **Its value is entirely conditional on it being put in front of counsel and of an authorised human decision-maker.**

# MANDATORY ONE-PAGE EXECUTIVE ANSWER

> ## The primary question, answered from the corpus and from nothing else.

**Question:** *What entity/legal-structure options could potentially support Sewak Sathi Sanstha's four-pillar architecture, and what must qualified Indian professionals and authorised human decision-makers determine before selecting one?*

| # | Answer |
|---|---|
| **1** | **The corpus already names an option set, and it is not a single list — it is three overlapping lists that this instrument merges without renumbering.** **[S: App. A §3.2]** gives four: *internal Krytos division · separate company/subsidiary · nonprofit entity / trust / society / Section 8 type structure · hybrid structure*. **[S-ST: R4]** gives three: *(i) single entity now, restructure later · (ii) two entities from the start — a not-for-profit service entity and a for-profit venture entity, with a brand licence, related-party agreements and transfer-pricing discipline · (iii) a holding structure with the mission held separately from both*. **[S-F: Q19]** and **[S-E: N-E-24]** give five labels: *Section 8 company / trust / society / other / two-entity structure*. **Part III merges these into OPT-A…OPT-J.** |
| **2** | **Three of the ten categories the brief asks about are absent from the corpus entirely.** `LLP`, `partnership` and `proprietorship` return **zero occurrences** across all seven instruments and both v1.2 text layers (§I.3). **"Ordinary private company" is present only as the undifferentiated word "company"** in **[S: App. A §3.2 row 2]**. **OPT-D, OPT-E, OPT-F and OPT-G are therefore surfaced by this instrument for counsel to consider or exclude, and every compatibility cell for them reads `UNKNOWN FROM CURRENT CORPUS` unless a general corpus statement applies.** Their absence is a finding, not an oversight to be papered over. |
| **3** | **The corpus has already decided that no single option may be assumed suitable.** **[S: App. A §3.2]**'s own risk column says of the nonprofit row: *"Restrictions on profit distribution and founder upside; **may not fit commercial waterfall without separate structure**"*; of the company row: *"**Social service and donations may need separate treatment**; governance must remain aligned with mission"*; of the internal-division row: *"**May mix charitable, commercial and protection risk with broader Krytos**; public donations and social-impact funds need careful accounting"*; of the hybrid row: *"**More complex accounting, governance and conflict-of-interest controls**."* **[S-ST: R4]** states the underlying reason: *"service (donation/CSR-eligible, tax-exempt-eligible, politically restricted **[X]**), business (taxable, contractual, market-facing) and protection (liability-heavy, insurance-dependent) have **conflicting legal natures. A single entity will serve none of them well.**"* |
| **4** | **GC-01 is the sharpest unrecorded conflict in the corpus, and it is structural.** The eight-priority waterfall is `APPROVED ARCHITECTURE` and a **reserved matter** — **[S: Ch.18]**, **[S-D: LDR-C16]** *"APPROVED — THE CONTROL"*, **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]**, **[S-D: LDR-C26]** — and it contains **priority 4 "Founder compensation / approved cap"** and **priority 8 "Residual surplus / founder upside."** **[S: App. A §3.2]**'s nonprofit row says a nonprofit form carries *"restrictions on profit distribution and founder upside"* and *"may not fit commercial waterfall without separate structure."* **No instrument in v1.2 or v1.3-A…F joins these two statements.** Either a chosen form accommodates priorities 4 and 8, or a reserved matter must be decided by an apex whose own authority is `LDR-U01 UNRESOLVED`. **This is a human decision, not an analytical one.** |
| **5** | **The parent term of the relation is unspecified.** `LDR-U08` is *"legal structure **under Krytos**"* **[S-D]**, and `OPT-I` is a parent/operating-entity structure — but **Krytos's own legal form, jurisdiction, governing documents, objects and ownership are nowhere stated in the corpus.** Krytos is *"parent/company context and ownership ecosystem"* **[S: Ch.1]** holding projects LifeXP, Code Forge, App Forge, Trade Lab **[S: App. A §3.1]**; **[S-A: A-V9]** lists *"Krytos governing documents"* as a question for governance counsel; **[S-A: A-14 S5]** records that *"the entity's own succession law and **Krytos's governing documents** take effect in parallel **[V]**"*; **[S-A: §11.2]** records that *"the entity's control follows **Krytos's own succession, which is outside this architecture entirely**."* **UE-24 ⟨new⟩ records this as a material information gap: OPT-A…OPT-J cannot be evaluated against a parent whose form is unknown.** |
| **6** | **Eleven of twenty-eight dependency chains root at `LDR-U08` or `LDR-U01`, and `LDR-U08` is step 1 of the thirteen-step master critical path.** **[S-F: Part VI, Part VII]**. Chains 4–7 originate at `LDR-U08`: entity → economic measurement `P-01…P-15`; entity → classification → priority-1 payment; entity → data fiduciary → privacy notice and breach notification; entity → insurance → any protection activity. **[S-F: IV.4 rank 1]**: it blocks *"A-B1, A-N14, A-N15, A-N16, LDR-U09, the privacy notice, insurance, contracts, employment, banking"* and transitively *"priority-1 payment, therefore the entire economic model, therefore the pilot's ability to pay anyone, therefore P-01…P-15 as a set."* |
| **7** | **What must be determined before selection — the nine professional gates.** **[S-F: V-01]** corporate/entity (form, objects clauses permitting **all four pillars**, dissolution and restructuring) · **[V-04]** tax (12A application-of-income and political-activity conditions, 80G, FCRA timing — **"12A/80G/FCRA conditions attach to the entity chosen under V-01"**) · **[V-05]** labour (worker classification per option) · **[V-10]** governance/company-secretarial **and** banking (mandate continuity, minimum signatories, custody instruments for reserves and the restricted social-impact fund) · **[V-06]** insurance (**L1–L15** — cover is written on a legal person) · **[V-07]** privacy (which entity is the data fiduciary; Significant Data Fiduciary status *"depends on scale and on the unresolved entity structure"* **[S-D: §50.6]**) · **[V-11]** IP/brand (A-N12, A-N13, name availability, trademark classes) · **[V-08]** safeguarding · **[V-09]** criminal/regulatory (intervention boundary). **Parts XIX and XX give the engagement briefs.** |
| **8** | **What must be determined before selection — the ten human decisions.** `LDR-U08` structure · `LDR-U09` worker classification · `A-N16` bank mandate and signatories · `A-N14` data-fiduciary identity and named officer · `A-N15` custody of reserves and the social-impact fund · `A-N12`/`A-N13` brand and IP ownership · `LDR-U01` CEO versus National Council authority · `A-N19` apex dispute resolution · `LDR-U19` apex succession mechanism · dissolution and restructuring posture under **[S: Const. Art.19]**. **Part XXI gives the minimum decision set that converts each from `UNRESOLVED` to `DECIDABLE` to `DECIDED`.** |
| **9** | **Three of those ten have no disinterested decider today.** **[S-F: Rule F-10]**: *"the body that would decide them is itself partly constituted by them … The only escape is that all three sit with the founder/CEO under [S: Ch.4] ownership authority, which is APPROVED architecture. **That is not a resolution of LDR-U01** — it is the fact that, today, one person can decide all three."* **[S-F: XXIV.2]** adds `LDR-U16` founder compensation as *"a decision with no disinterested decider"* (**[S-D: Rule D-92]** Founder Recusal Register applies). **This instrument does not resolve the concentration; it records that the entity decision is the one decision that can lawfully be taken by that person today, and that taking it changes what the other nine require.** |
| **10** | **A-N16 remains a blocker and is not closed by anything in this paper.** **[S-A: §22 N16]** `UNRESOLVED` → **[S-A: A-B9]** BLOCKER → **[S-F: BLK-A-03, SR-03, P0-C]**. Consequence, verbatim: *"**Priority 1 fails on one person's absence. The waterfall's first obligation becomes unpayable.**"* Decision window: *"Now → **before the first external payment**."* **§IX.7 preserves the block.** |
| **11** | **What the pilot cannot do while the entity question is open.** **[S-F: XXV.7]** — *"**Any payment to a worker** — A-B1 entity and classification. An unlawful payment creates liability, not data"*; any protection or first-response activity; any activity involving a minor or vulnerable adult; any systematic personal-data collection; any published claim. **[S-F: P0-A]** requires as a PASS condition that *"**the entity exists**"* with a *"certificate of registration; objects clause"* — and lists as a FAIL condition *"**no legal person**."* **[S-F: XIV.3 Finding 2]**: *"the architecture's first and most absolute financial obligation is one the organisation cannot lawfully perform. **This is the single hardest fact in this register.**"* |
| **12** | **What may nevertheless proceed.** **[S-F: LDR-U08 "Does NOT block"]**: *"**Nothing.** Writing, thinking, designing and training may continue."* **[S-E: Rule E-60 / BR-9]**: one team, in one locality, using the name locally under **recorded interim permission**, with no national channel, campaign, domain, account portfolio or partnership. **[S-B: §40.2]**: the pilot needs **no technology**. **[S-F: VIII.2]**: P0 does not require a resolved CEO-vs-Council question, a resolved brand/IP question, a named founder successor, any numeric threshold, a leaderboard, composition monitoring or software. **Part XXII separates these into five classes — and gives no permission.** |
| **13** | **Final verdict — # B — DECISION-READY WITH MATERIAL GAPS.** Part XXIV |
| **14** | **Why B and not A.** Four gaps are material and none is closable by analysis: **GC-01** the unreconciled waterfall/nonprofit conflict; **UE-24** Krytos's own legal form unknown; **§I.3** three option categories absent from the corpus; **`LDR-U17`** the economic blueprint was never attached, so **no structure's compliance load can be weighed against any capacity to bear it**. **A decision can be approached — indeed [S-F: SR-01] mandates approaching it — but it cannot yet be *fully* specified.** |
| **15** | **Why B and not C or D.** **Not C:** the source information is not missing or contradictory in a way that prevents framing — the corpus names the options, the consequences, the validators, the blockers, the dependency chains and the decision windows with unusual precision, and **[S-F]** has already consolidated them. **Not D:** the architecture is sufficiently specified that the entity question can be *stated*, which is precisely what Parts I–XXII do. **The gaps are inputs to a decision, not obstacles to defining one.** |
| **16** | **What would move B → A.** Four inputs, none of them architectural: **(1)** Krytos's governing documents and legal form, obtained from the founder; **(2)** a human decision on GC-01 — whether priorities 4 and 8 of the waterfall are to be preserved in the chosen form, or referred as a reserved matter; **(3)** counsel's confirmation of which of OPT-D…OPT-G are legally available for any of the four functions, so that the option set can be closed rather than left open; **(4)** the economic blueprint or an explicit statement that the pilot will produce it (**`LDR-U17`**), so that compliance load can be weighed. |
| **17** | **What this instrument refuses to do.** Select a structure · assert that any option is suitable because it exists · assert any tax benefit, exemption, eligibility or coverage · invent an owner, a fiduciary, a signatory set, a mandate, a classification, a licence, a dissolution clause, a governance authority or a successor · close `LDR-U01`, `A-N19`, `LDR-U19` or `LDR-U16` · create a weighted score (**the corpus authorises none**) · declare a winner · permit informal operation · invent a number |
| **18** | **The one line this paper exists to support.** **[S-F: XXV.8]** — *"**Next artifact: the entity-structure decision paper. Nothing else should be built until it exists.**"* **It now exists. It is not the decision.** |

# PART I — SOURCE RECONSTRUCTION

## I.1 Method

**[P]** Every entity-related reference in **v1.2 (both text layers), the Strategic Stress Test, v1.3-A, v1.3-B, v1.3-C, v1.3-D, v1.3-E and v1.3-F** was located by direct term search against the files, then read in context, then recorded. **Nothing below is recalled; every row carries the instrument and the section.**

**[A]** Search terms used, exactly as the brief specifies — 41 terms: `entity`, `legal person`, `registration`, `ownership`, `founder`, `CEO`, `National Council`, `National Team`, `fiduciary`, `bank`, `banking mandate`, `reserve`, `social fund`, `organisation account`, `venture account`, `worker`, `employee`, `contractor`, `volunteer`, `compensation`, `salary`, `remuneration`, `tax`, `12A`, `80G`, `FCRA`, `charitable`, `trust`, `society`, `company`, `LLP`, `partnership`, `proprietorship`, `IP`, `trademark`, `copyright`, `brand`, `dissolution`, `succession`, `governance`, `audit`, `liability`, `insurance`, `safeguarding`. Extended by the terms the corpus actually uses for the same concepts: `legal structure`, `legal entity`, `objects clause`, `signatory`, `mandate`, `data fiduciary`, `custody`, `reserved matter`, `Krytos`, `subsidiary`, `hybrid`, `two-entity`, `holding`, `federation`, `unincorporated`, `statutory dues`, `classification`, `GST`, `TDS`, `statutory audit`, `CSR`, `domain`, `mark`, `licence`.

## I.2 The entity provenance table — EP-01 … EP-48

**[A]** Columns exactly as the brief requires: **Source ID / Section → Exact Issue → Entity Consequence.** Where an item appears in more than one instrument, **all source IDs are retained** (**[S-F: Rule F-4]**).

Krytos = *"Parent/company context and ownership ecosystem"*; Sewak Sathi Sanstha = *"Proposed/public operating name for the Krytos-owned organisation; **final legal/name validation required**"* | **The operating name is not a legal person.** Every layer below Krytos is an organisational unit, not an entity. Whether any layer may hold money, records or contracts is undetermined |

### I.2.2 v1.1 source sections preserved in Appendix A **[S: App. A §n]**

| EP | Source ID / Section | Exact issue | Entity consequence |
|---|---|---|---|
| **EP-31** | **[S: App. A §1 Executive Summary]** | *"Its purpose is **not limited to charity**; it is a mission-driven operating network … while also building sustainable economic capacity through legitimate products and services"*; *"**Mission includes people, communities, animals, environment** and legitimate causes"*; *"The organisation is **not merely a donation-funded charity**; legitimate business is part of the design"* | The **charitable characterisation is expressly incomplete.** Whether a charitable form can carry a design that disclaims charity-only status is the central V-01/V-04 question — Part V |
| **EP-32** | **[S: App. A §1.3]** Non-negotiable source-preservation rules | *"**The organisation is not converted into a conventional NGO-only model; the business component remains central**"* | **A structural constraint on the option set:** any option that forces the architecture into an NGO-only model violates a source-preservation rule. Recorded, not resolved |
| **EP-33** | **[S: App. A §3.1]** Position inside Krytos | *"Sewak Sathi Sanstha sits inside the broader Krytos ecosystem together with other Krytos projects such as **LifeXP, Code Forge, App Forge and Trade Lab**. The organisation is therefore conceived as **an operating organisation and product ecosystem component owned by Krytos, not as an independent body that automatically supersedes the founder/CEO**"* | The organisation is conceived as a **component of a multi-project parent.** Any separation option must account for the sibling projects and for shared brand, IP and systems. **Krytos's own form is unstated — UE-16** |
| **EP-34** | **[S: App. A §3.2]** Legal structure status | *"**REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION: The exact legal structure has not been finalised.** Before taking **public money, employing people, contracting at scale, operating regulated services, handling sensitive personal data or deploying protection/first-response functions**, qualified professionals must validate the legal entity, tax registration, labour/employment model, contracts, accounting design, safeguarding obligations, insurance and regulatory permissions"* | **This is the source's own statement of the six triggers that make the entity decision unavoidable.** All six are contemplated by the architecture |
| **EP-35** | **[S: App. A §3.2]** "Possible legal architecture" table | Four rows, each with *Advantages / Risks-unresolved issues / Recommendation status*: **(1) Internal Krytos division** — *"Fast initial control; aligned with founder ownership; simpler brand integration"* / *"**May mix charitable, commercial and protection risk with broader Krytos; public donations and social-impact funds need careful accounting**"* / *"Option for early internal pilot only after validation"*. **(2) Separate company/subsidiary** — *"Cleaner contracts, accounts and liability separation; suitable for commercial ventures"* / *"**Social service and donations may need separate treatment; governance must remain aligned with mission**"* / *"Strong candidate for business network — requires professional structuring"*. **(3) Nonprofit entity / trust / society / Section 8 type structure** — *"Better mission credibility and eligibility for certain grants/donations"* / *"**Restrictions on profit distribution and founder upside; may not fit commercial waterfall without separate structure**"* / *"May be suitable for social-impact arm — requires legal/tax advice"*. **(4) Hybrid structure** — *"Separates commercial ventures from social programmes while preserving mission funding links"* / *"**More complex accounting, governance and conflict-of-interest controls**"* / *"Recommended for professional exploration; not approved until designed"* | **The corpus's own option universe, reproduced verbatim in Part III.** **Rule G-3 governs the "Recommendation status" column.** The risk column is the source of most `POTENTIALLY RESTRICTED` and `REQUIRES STRUCTURAL SEPARATION` entries in Part IV |
| **EP-36** | **[S: App. A §3.4]** Authority distinctions | Ownership — *"**Krytos / Founder-CEO as determined by final legal structure**"*; Governance — *"Proposed National Council … Does not own the organisation over the CEO **unless a future legal charter explicitly says so**"*; Financial — *"**No single person should receive money, approve expenses, record transactions and audit them**"* | Every authority type is defined **relative to a structure that will determine it.** The governance authority requires a *legal charter* — the instrument that does not exist |
| **EP-37** | **[S: App. A §3.5]** Proposed CEO–Council–National Team model | `PROPOSED / REQUIRES FINAL APPROVAL`: *"**The source leaves the final relationship between CEO, National Council and National Team unresolved.**"* Founder/CEO cannot approve alone: *"Illegal activity, unsafe protection action, hidden financial transfers, unilateral audit suppression, **final legal/tax conclusions without professionals**."* National Council cannot approve alone: *"**Ownership transfer**, override of CEO outside charter, direct operational micromanagement, spending outside approved budget."* National Team cannot: *"Change constitutional principles, **finalise legal structure**, bypass Council audit, alter pay policy without approval"* | **The architecture already prohibits every internal body from finalising the legal structure or concluding legal/tax questions alone.** This is the source basis for Rule G-1 |
| **EP-38** | **[S: App. A §14.3]** Waterfall definitions | **Statutory obligations** = *"Taxes, deductions, labour or other legally required amounts **applicable to the legal structure and worker classification**"*; **Venture revenue account** = *"Designated account/ledger where customer revenue for an approved venture is recorded before allocation"*; **Worker compensation** = *"Payment to workers/members for verified work **under the approved legal and financial model**"*; **Residual surplus / founder upside** = *"Remaining surplus after priority obligations and caps **as defined by approved structure**"* | **Four of the waterfall's own defined terms are defined by reference to the structure.** Two of them (statutory obligations, founder upside) cannot be quantified or even characterised until it is chosen |
| **EP-39** | **[S: App. A §14.4]** | *"ILLUSTRATIVE WORKING EXAMPLE — NOT FINAL POLICY: … 37.5% worker compensation, 17% direct delivery costs, 12% organisation service fee … **Final percentages, caps, multipliers and calculations require founder approval and professional validation**"* | `LDR-U18`. **No percentage in this instrument.** Rule G-6 |
| **EP-40** | **[S: App. A §20.2]** Account and ledger architecture | Six financial areas with purpose and controls: **Team accounts/ledgers** (*"Record team-level expenses, reimbursements and local funds"* — approval limits, receipts, weekly reconciliation) · **Venture revenue accounts** (*"Separate customer revenue by approved venture"* — invoice/order matching, delivery proof, waterfall allocation) · **Organisation account** (*"Receive approved organisation allocations and pay approved central costs"* — national finance oversight and audit) · **Reserve funds** (*"Continuity, emergencies and wage-protection buffer"* — *"National Funds Manager manages; release requires approval matrix"*) · **Social-impact funds** (*"Restricted mission programmes funded from surplus conditions"* — *"**Segregated ledger**, approved budgets, evidence and audit"*) · **Petty cash where allowed** (*"Cap, receipts, dual count, timely replenishment/reconciliation"*) | **The architecture specifies six account/ledger classes and no legal holder for any of them.** Part IX maps each to the entity question. **[S-B: §3.2]**: the Mohalla Team is *"**not a legal entity, not a cost centre with its own bank account unless the unresolved structure says so [V]**"* |
| **EP-41** | **[S: App. A §20.3–20.4]** | Segregation of duties: *"Person A may RECEIVE money / Person B may APPROVE expense / Person C may RECORD transaction / Person D may REVIEW-AUDIT"*; in small teams *"roles may be combined temporarily **only with compensating controls**: lower limits, dual acknowledgement, city/state review, receipts and bank proof, random audit, prompt role split as volume grows."* Funds Manager **cannot**: *"Unilaterally approve their own expenses; change compensation percentages, priority order or leader multiplier; **release reserve or social-impact funds without required approvals**; hide transactions or maintain **off-book cash**; audit their own work as final reviewer"* | Dual control and the prohibition on off-book cash are **APPROVED architecture** and are the internal-control half of the banking question. **The mandate half (`A-N16`) is `UNRESOLVED`** |
| **EP-42** | **[S: App. A §21.2]** Social-Impact Fund specification | Funding source *"Approved allocation from venture surplus when waterfall conditions are met; **donations restricted to social impact where lawful may also be possible after validation**"*; Restricted use *"only on approved programme purposes; not ordinary salaries, political/religious activity, personal favours or venture bailouts"*; Disbursement *"Payments follow finance controls, receipts and **dual approval where appropriate**"*; Audit *"Auditors can trace source, approval, disbursement, evidence and outcome"*; *"These restrictions are preserved **unless the founder formally amends them after professional validation**"* | A restricted fund with donor-restriction traceability is a **fiduciary object.** Its custodian is `UNRESOLVED` (**A-N15**); its instrument does not exist (**[S-A: §22 N15]**: *"Restricted purpose survives in policy but in no instrument"*) |
| **EP-43** | **[S: App. A §30.1]** Launch phases | Phase 0 → Phase 5 as at EP-19, with Phase 5 exit *"Stable platform, **professional compliance**, funding/reserve model, national audit capability"* | Compliance is a **scaling obligation**, not a launch one — but Phase 0 is |
| **EP-44** | **[S: App. A §31]** Launch Checklist (v1.1 detail behind **[S: Ch.31]**) | Rows with their **Dependency** column verbatim: *Governance — "Approve CEO–Council–National Team charter" — Not started — CEO + proposed Council — **Dependency: Legal structure options** — Evidence: Signed governance charter — **Blocker*** · *Legal — "**Select legal entity / hybrid structure**" — Not started — CEO + legal adviser — Professional review — **Legal opinion and registration plan** — Blocker* · *Legal — "Contract templates for customers and members" — **Dependency: Entity structure** — Blocker* · *Tax — "Tax/accounting treatment of revenue and funds" — **Dependency: Entity choice** — Tax memo / accounting policy — Blocker* · *Employment — "Worker/member classification and compensation legality" — **Dependency: Compensation model** — Employment/labour advice — Blocker* · *Finance — "Bank/account/ledger architecture" — **Dependency: Entity/tax review** — Account plan and controls — Blocker* · *Finance — "Weekly waterfall policy approval" — **Dependency: Legal/tax validation** — Blocker* · *Finance — "Reserve release approval matrix" — Proposed — Blocker* · *Business — "Venture approval framework" — **Dependency: Legal/contracts** — Blocker* · *Data privacy — "Privacy notice and retention policy" — Blocker* · *Audit — "Audit plan for pilot" — Blocker* · *Partnerships — "Partnership due-diligence template" — **Non-blocker for pilot*** | **The source's own dependency column makes the entity the predecessor of the governance charter, the contract templates, the tax treatment, the bank/account/ledger architecture, the waterfall policy and the venture framework.** This is the strongest single piece of source evidence for **[S-F: CP-2]**'s maximum blocking effect. **Note the one exception: partnership due-diligence is expressly `Non-blocker for pilot`** |
| **EP-45** | **[S: App. A §37]** Final Decision Register (v1.1 detail behind **[S: Ch.33]**) | Same items as EP-28, with the added *"Final approval required?"* column: **Legal structure under Krytos — Unresolved — "Professional review of internal division, subsidiary, nonprofit/social arm or hybrid structure" — "Yes; professional validation"**; **Worker legal classification — Unresolved — "Determine employee/contractor/volunteer/stipend/business participant categories" — "Yes; professional validation"**; **Insurance coverage — Unresolved — "Professional insurance review before field/business scale" — "Yes; professional validation"**; **Public name — Proposed/public name — "validate name availability and registration"** | Confirms that the **five** worker categories in `LDR-U09` are the source's own list, and that **registration** is contemplated for the public name |
| **EP-46** | **[S: App. F]** Evidence Standards | *"Evidence must prove work without invading privacy or creating fake certainty"*; per-record required / not-acceptable / privacy-retention columns | Evidence standards are the audit substrate for any statutory audit or donor report. **Retention periods `UNRESOLVED` (A-N26)** |
| **EP-47** | **[S: App. A §12 / §10]** | *"Internal training is **not automatically legally sufficient** for first aid, security, labour, child safeguarding, medical, counselling, legal or regulated activities"* | Certification and regulated-activity permissions attach to a person or entity that can hold them. **`UNKNOWN` today** |
| **EP-48** | **[S: App. A §Document Control]** | *"Document status: … it is **not legal advice, tax advice, financial advice, an employment contract, a safeguarding policy, an insurance opinion or a regulatory approval**."* Launch condition: *"The organisation **should not represent itself as legally launch-ready** until professional validation gates in the launch roadmap are completed"* | **The source forbids the representation this paper must not make.** Carried into **[S-E: MC-8]** and Rule E-59 |

### I.2.3 Strategic Stress Test **[S-ST]**

| EP | Source ID / Section | Exact issue | Entity consequence |
|---|---|---|---|
| **EP-49** | **[S-ST: R4]** — *"Whether one legal entity can hold all four pillars"* | *Original:* *"legal structure under Krytos is UNRESOLVED; options listed include internal division, subsidiary, nonprofit/social arm, hybrid **[S: Ch.33]**."* *Concern:* *"service (donation/CSR-eligible, tax-exempt-eligible, politically restricted **[X]**), business (taxable, contractual, market-facing) and protection (liability-heavy, insurance-dependent) have **conflicting legal natures. A single entity will serve none of them well**."* *Options:* *"(i) **single entity now, restructure later** — cheap now, **expensive or impossible later once contracts and reputation attach to one name**; (ii) **two entities from the start** — a not-for-profit service entity and a for-profit venture entity — with a **brand licence, related-party agreements and strict transfer-pricing discipline [V]**; (iii) **a holding structure with the mission held separately from both**."* *New risk of separating:* *"administrative burden, **related-party scrutiny**, and a possible public perception of 'charity and business being mixed' whichever way it is done; **donor confusion**."* *Decision owner:* *"Founder/CEO with corporate, tax and regulatory counsel **[V]**. **This is the Phase 0 decision on which most others depend, and it should be made first.**"* | **The corpus's most explicit multi-entity analysis, and the source of OPT-H and OPT-I.** Note that R4 is a **RECONSIDERATION** item — it reopens a question the source left open, and it does not answer it |
| **EP-50** | **[S-ST: §26 item 1]** | *"**Decide the legal structure (R4)** — entity form, tax position, worker classification, service/business separation. **Why it cannot wait:** Everything downstream (payment, insurance, contracts, tax exemption, FCRA, political-neutrality obligations) depends on it; it is UNRESOLVED **[S: Ch.33]** and it is Phase 0 **[S: Ch.30]**. Owner: Founder/CEO + counsel **[V]**. Effort: H"* | The stress test **independently** ranks the entity decision first among fourteen pre-launch changes |

| **EP-95** | **[S-B: §14.3, §16]** | Four paid/volunteer models A–D; **Model D** (*"mixed, separable"*) is *"what the architecture describes"*; *"**Model B is not fundable at team level**"*; boundary rules include *"**the same person may hold both kinds of work, but the hours are recorded separately**"* | **The volunteer/worker boundary is an economic design and a legal question simultaneously** — **HV-1**, **EV-3**, **A-V3** |
| **EP-96** | **[S-B: §21]** Reserve system | Five funds tabulated with source definition, level held and releaser; *"Other approved reserves — **Unresolved / Unresolved**"*; *"(Proposed) First-season float — **Does not exist in the source**"*; *"**Two facts govern everything below:** (i) the reserve release rules are **NOT FINAL [S: Ch.33]** … (ii) **custody is National**, with v1.3-A N15 and N16 both **UNRESOLVED**. This section therefore models *behaviour*, not balances"*; **§21.4** — *"Can one team's reserves be transferred to another? **No — and the source is clear rather than merely silent**"* | **Reserve custody is the entity question in its most concrete form.** **B-15** requires `[V]` *"for custody, banking, tax and accounting treatment — **all contingent on v1.3-A N15/N16**"* |

| **EP-102** | **[S-B: Annexure C]** EV-1…EV-10 | **EV-1 Accountant/auditor** — gross-revenue basis; accrued-compensation treatment; **float characterisation — advance, loan, grant or deferred income**; seeded-reserve accounting; direct-cost attribution; materials allocation treatment. **EV-2 Tax counsel** — *"Tax treatment of each revenue category; **GST/indirect tax on services**; 12A/80G conditions and **the effect of any commercial activity**; donation deductibility and cash limits; FCRA eligibility and timing; **tax on member payments**."* **EV-3 Employment/labour** — *"classification; **lawfulness of pro-rata partial payment (B-06)**; minimum-wage and timely-payment obligations; **whether accrued unpaid compensation creates a wage debt**; volunteer status and its limits; **trainee-time eligibility**."* **EV-4 Corporate/entity counsel** — *"**Authority to advance funds, hold reserves, operate bank mandates; entity form (E-C1); whether a Mohalla team can hold any balance at all**."* **EV-5 Insurance broker** — *"whether any cover exists for venture delivery, professional indemnity on client work, member injury, third-party injury, cyber, and **trustee liability**."* **EV-6 Competition/commercial counsel** — *"**price floors (B-11)**; contract enforceability, interest and consumer law (B-13); **sponsorship and naming rights**; exclusion of liability for venture work."* **EV-10 Banking/payments** — *"**mandate continuity and minimum signatories (v1.3-A N16); payment-processing costs at weekly cadence; collection instruments for SME customers**"* | **EV-4 is the only corpus line that asks directly whether a local team may hold a balance at all.** Part IX |
| **EP-103** | **[S-B: §46.2]** Verdict | **D — NOT YET ECONOMICALLY VIABLE**, with a defined path to B. *"**The source contains no subsidy instrument to evaluate**"* | **The economic viability of any structure cannot be assessed, because viability itself is unassessed.** Part XXIV relies on this |

**[A]** The brief requires that the search not rely on memory. The result of the term search, including terms that return **nothing**, is recorded here because a negative finding about the corpus is itself decision-relevant.

| `legal person` | **NO — the phrase does not appear** | — | **[S-F: chain 4]** uses it functionally: *"there is no **legal person** to incur cost, hold an account, employ anyone or be audited"* — the concept is present, the term is v1.3-F's |

**[A] The three negative findings that matter most:**

1. **`LLP`, `partnership firm` and `proprietorship` are absent.** Three of the ten categories the brief requires to be investigated were never contemplated by any instrument. **This is not evidence that they are unsuitable — it is evidence that the corpus did not consider them.** Whether they are **legally available for any of the four functions** is itself a `V-01` question. **OPT-E, OPT-F, OPT-G carry `UNKNOWN FROM CURRENT CORPUS` in every cell that has no general corpus anchor.**
2. **`copyright` is absent while `IP ownership` is a BLOCKER-adjacent unresolved item.** The corpus asks who owns *"the architecture, SOPs, curricula, software, data models and brand assets"* **[S-A: N13]** without ever naming the rights that subsist in them. **Part XVII records this as a question for IP counsel, not as an omission to be filled here.**
3. **`TDS`, payroll-statute names, and CSR-recipient eligibility are absent.** Part XIV therefore raises them as **⟨new — v1.3-G⟩** questions, explicitly marked as questions the corpus did not ask, with no answer supplied.

## I.4 What the corpus already agrees on — the entity-relevant subset

**[A]** **[S-F: §I.4 Rule F-5]** lists ten load-bearing agreements that appear independently in three or more instruments and are contradicted nowhere. **Four bear directly on the entity question and are not re-opened here:**

| Agreement | Appears in | Entity relevance |
|---|---|---|
| **The waterfall priority order is `APPROVED` and is a reserved matter** | **[S: Ch.18]**, **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]**, **[S-D: LDR-C16, LDR-C26, INV-2]**, **[S-B]** throughout | **The structure must accommodate an `APPROVED` money sequence it did not choose — and may not change it by interim authority. GC-01** |
| **AI recommends; humans decide; AI cannot make consequential legal decisions** | **[S: Const. Art.12]**, **[S-A: A-21, A-22]**, **[S-D: L-26, AI-1…AI-8]**, **[S-E: Rule E-68, E-133]** | **No AI system may select, validate or approve the entity.** **[S: App. A §3.5]** already prohibits the National Team from finalising it |
| **No number is invented; every threshold is `PROPOSED` or `UNKNOWN — MEASURE IN PILOT`** | All six | Rule G-6 |
| **One shared pilot: one mohalla team, no technology** | **[S-B: §40]**, **[S-C: §57]**, **[S-D: Rule D-130]**, **[S-E: Rule E-222]** | **The entity that must exist before the pilot is the entity for one team — not for a national network.** Part XXII |

**[A]** To these, v1.3-G adds three propositions that appear independently in **four or more** instruments and are contradicted nowhere. They are recorded as agreements, not as decisions:

| Agreement ⟨new — v1.3-G⟩ | Appears in |
|---|---|
| **The entity decision is sequenced first, by the source's own hand.** Phase 0 precedes Phase 1 **[S: Ch.30]**; the launch checklist makes *"Select legal entity / hybrid structure"* a `Blocker` and the dependency of five other `Blocker` rows **[S: App. A §31]**; the stress test ranks it first of fourteen pre-launch changes **[S-ST: §26 item 1]** and calls it *"the Phase 0 decision on which most others depend, and **it should be made first**"* **[R4]**; v1.3-F makes it STEP 1 of the master critical path and SR-01's next action **[S-F: VII.2, XXII, XXV.4 Action 1]** | **4 instruments** |
| **No internal body may finalise the structure or conclude a legal/tax question alone.** **[S: App. A §3.5]** — National Team *"cannot … finalise legal structure"*; Founder/CEO cannot approve alone *"final legal/tax conclusions without professionals"*; **[S: Ch.33 / App. A §37]** — *"Yes; professional validation"*; **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]** — entity structure is a reserved matter; **[S-F: Q19]** — *"the corpus forbids inventing one"* | **4 instruments** |
| **The absence of a legal person is itself a public-facing fact that must be disclosed, never concealed.** **[S-ST: §18.3 item 4]** legal-status honesty; **[S-E: Rule E-59]** no concealment on request; **[S-E: MC-8]** publish entity status accurately *including what is pending*; **[S-E: §20.4 L4]** structural trust; **[S: App. A §Document Control]** *"should not represent itself as legally launch-ready"* | **4 instruments** |

Two of the eight priorities are **priority 4 "Founder compensation / approved cap"** and **priority 8 "Residual surplus / founder upside."** Separately, **[S: App. A §3.2 row 3]** states that a *"Nonprofit entity / trust / society / Section 8 type structure"* carries *"**Restrictions on profit distribution and founder upside; may not fit commercial waterfall without separate structure**."* **[S-D: §50.5]** records that whether the waterfall conflicts with an application-of-income requirement is *"`CANNOT BE ASSESSED`."* **No instrument joins the reserved-matter status of the waterfall to the nonprofit-incompatibility note, and no instrument states what happens if a chosen form cannot accommodate priorities 4 and 8** | **[S: Ch.18]** vs **[S: App. A §3.2]** vs **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]** | **MATERIAL — the largest conflict found by this instrument.** Consequence: **the entity decision and the waterfall are coupled.** If a chosen form cannot carry priority 4 or 8, then either (a) the form is not chosen, (b) the activity generating the surplus is placed in a different person (**OPT-H / OPT-I**), or (c) a **reserved matter** is decided by an apex whose own authority is `LDR-U01 UNRESOLVED`. **This paper does not choose among (a), (b) and (c). Art.3]** already puts it |

**[P] Rule G-11 (Conflicts Are Inputs To The Decision, Not Obstacles To It).** GC-01, GC-02 and GC-05 change **what must be decided**, not **whether it can be decided.** Each is therefore carried into Part XXI's minimum decision set as an explicit sub-question, and into Part XIX as an explicit question for counsel. **None is closed here.**

# PART II — CURRENT UNRESOLVED ENTITY REGISTER

## II.1 Method

**[P]** One register, containing **every entity-relevant unresolved item** located across v1.2, the stress test and v1.3-A…v1.3-F, with the nine fields the brief requires. **Source IDs are retained verbatim and appear first** (**[S-F: Rule F-4]**). Where two instruments give the same item different IDs, **both are printed** — this is why `A-N26` appears with `LDR-U10` and `N-E-26`, and `A-N19` with `LDR-U12`.

**[P] Rule G-12 (Status Is Copied, Not Reassessed).** The *status* column reproduces the corpus's own label. **No item is promoted from `UNRESOLVED` to `PROPOSED`, or from `PROPOSED` to `DECIDED`, anywhere in this Part** (**[S-F: Rule F-9]**).

**[P] Rule G-13 ("Does NOT block" Is A Source Statement Or An Analysis, Never A Permission).** Where the corpus states what an item does not block, that text is quoted. Where it does not, the entry reads `NOT STATED IN CORPUS` — **and no activity is authorised by this instrument.**

## II.3 What Part II establishes

**[A]** Thirty-two items, of which:

| Class | Count | Items |
|---|---|---|
| **`UNRESOLVED` in a source instrument** | 15 | UE-01, UE-02, UE-03, UE-05, UE-06, UE-07, UE-08, UE-09, UE-10, UE-11, UE-13, UE-14, UE-15, UE-17, UE-29 |
| **`PROPOSED` — not adopted** | 6 | UE-04, UE-16, UE-18, UE-19, UE-22, UE-31 (partly) |
| **`REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION` with no answer** | 2 | UE-21, UE-31 |
| **`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`** | 4 | UE-14, UE-15, UE-17, UE-20 |
| **⟨new — v1.3-G⟩, i.e. entity-consequential but not separately registered by any source instrument** | 9 | UE-24, UE-25, UE-26, UE-27, UE-28, UE-29, UE-30, UE-31, UE-32 |
| **Of which: no disinterested decider exists today** | 2 | UE-14 (**[S-F: XXV.5]**), UE-20 (**[S-F: XXIV.2]**) |
| **Of which: an interim procedure exists and is sufficient for a one-team pilot** | 2 | UE-14 (**[S-D: Rule D-113]**), UE-05 (**[S-E: BR-9]** — but see **GC-06**) |

**[A] The structural finding of Part II:** **twenty-nine of the thirty-two items depend on UE-01.** The three that do not are **UE-24** (which UE-01 depends on), **UE-23** (deliberately undecided, and independent), and **UE-04** (an adoption-order decision). **The register is therefore not a list of thirty-two problems; it is one problem with thirty-one consequences and one input.**

**[P] Rule G-14 (The Register Is An Input To Counsel, Not A Substitute For Counsel).** Every row's *professional validation* column names a **role or category**, never a person or firm (**[S-F: Rule F-7]**). **Nothing in Part II is an instruction to a professional; it is a schedule of questions for authorised human decision-makers to commission.**

# PART III — STRUCTURE OPTION UNIVERSE

## III.1 How the option universe was assembled

**[A]** The brief requires ten categories A–J. **The corpus supplies overlapping but non-identical lists, and this Part merges them without discarding any.**

| Corpus list | Source | Options named |
|---|---|---|
| **v1.1 §3.2 "Possible legal architecture"** — the only tabulated option set in the primary source | **[S: App. A §3.2]** | Internal Krytos division · Separate company/subsidiary · **Nonprofit entity / trust / society / Section 8 type structure** · Hybrid structure |
| **Stress-test reconsideration R4** | **[S-ST: R4]** | (i) single entity now, restructure later · (ii) **two entities from the start — a not-for-profit service entity and a for-profit venture entity — with a brand licence, related-party agreements and strict transfer-pricing discipline [V]** · (iii) **a holding structure with the mission held separately from both** |
| **Stress-test validation register** | **[S-ST: Annexure B Corporate/entity ◆]** | *"entity form and structure (**single vs. two-entity vs. holding**)"* |
| **v1.3-E national-identity register** | **[S-E: N-E-24]** | **Section 8 company / trust / society / other** |
| **v1.3-F executive answer** | **[S-F: Q19]** | **Section 8 company, trust, society, other, or a two-entity structure** |
| **v1.3-D register** | **[S-D: LDR-U08]** | *"Section 8 company / trust / society / other / two-entity structure"* |
| **v1.3-A founder-concentration analysis** | **[S-A: §11.3 item 1]** | *"internal division, subsidiary, nonprofit/social arm, hybrid, or **two-entity separation**"* |
| **v1.3-B economic register** | **[S-B: E-C1]** | *"Review division / subsidiary / nonprofit / hybrid / two-entity"* |
| **v1.2 decision register** | **[S: Ch.33; App. A §37]** | *"Review **internal division, subsidiary, nonprofit/social arm or hybrid** structure"* |
| **Future-scale option** | **[S-ST: §5 Scenario D, §23.1 S5]**; **[S-A: A-L7]** | **Federation** — legally distinct state entities bound by a common constitution, standards body and brand licence |
| **Non-entity option, raised as a question** | **[S-C: HV-6]** | *"Whether an **unincorporated network** may issue credentials, hold records, publish a directory, or accept guardian consent"* |

**[P] Rule G-15 (Every Corpus Option Survives The Merge).** No option named by any instrument is dropped, merged away or downgraded. **Where two instruments use different words for what may or may not be the same thing — "nonprofit entity", "nonprofit/social arm", "social arm", "trust", "society", "Section 8 type structure", "Section 8 company" — the words are kept side by side and the question of whether they denote the same legal object is referred to counsel.** This paper does not assume that *"nonprofit entity"* and *"Section 8 company"* are the same, nor that *"trust"* denotes a public charitable trust rather than a private one.

**[P] Rule G-16 (Absent Categories Are Surfaced, Not Endorsed).** **LLP, partnership and proprietorship appear nowhere in the corpus** (§I.3). They are included as **OPT-E, OPT-F, OPT-G** because the brief requires them and because counsel may consider them — **but each carries an explicit `NOT PRESENT IN CORPUS` marker, and every cell for them in Part IV defaults to `UNKNOWN FROM CURRENT CORPUS` unless a general corpus statement applies.** Their inclusion here is **not** a suggestion that they are suitable.

Art.3]** · **[S-E: Rule E-58 / brand tier T2 PARENT]** |
| **Named in the corpus as an option?** | **YES.** This is the **only option the corpus describes as the present conceptual state** — the organisation is *already* conceived as a Krytos-owned operating component. **What is unresolved is whether that conception is given legal form, and if so what form** |
| **Critical dependency** | **UE-24: Krytos's own legal form, jurisdiction, governing documents, objects and ownership are nowhere stated.** `LDR-U08` is literally *"legal structure **under Krytos**"* — the parent term of the relation is unspecified |
| **Corpus-recorded consequences of this option** | **[S-A: §11.2]**: *"**The entity's control follows Krytos's own succession, which is outside this architecture entirely**"*; *"**Whoever owns Krytos owns the operating system of the institution; a licensor could withdraw it**"*; *"the legal data fiduciary is **presumably** the entity, i.e. **ultimately Krytos**."* **[S-A: A-14 §13.2 S5]**: *"the entity's own succession law and **Krytos's governing documents take effect in parallel [V]**."* **[S: App. A §3.2 row 1]** risk: *"**May mix charitable, commercial and protection risk with broader Krytos**; public donations and social-impact funds need careful accounting."* **[S-E: Rule E-59]**: *"An ownership structure that can only survive not being asked about is not a governance structure"* |
| **Related variant named by the corpus** | **[S: App. A §3.2 row 1]** *"**Internal Krytos division**"* — Advantages *"Fast initial control; aligned with founder ownership; simpler brand integration"*; Risks *"May mix charitable, commercial and protection risk with broader Krytos; public donations and social-impact funds need careful accounting"*; Recommendation status *"**Option for early internal pilot only after validation**"* → **GC-02** (its relationship to **[S-F: P0-A]** is unresolved) |
| **Status** | `OPTION — NOT SELECTED`. **Rule G-1** |

### OPT-J — Other structure identified by counsel

**[A]** The corpus expressly provides for this category: **[S-F: Q19]** and **[S-E: N-E-24]** both end their option lists with **"other."** **[S-A: A-V1]** requires counsel to advise on *"entity form under Krytos"* without restricting the answer to a named list. **The following are recorded so that counsel's answer can be located against something; none is proposed by this instrument.**

| Sub-option | Corpus provenance | Note |
|---|---|---|
| **J-1 Unincorporated association / network** | **[S-C: HV-6]** — *"Whether an **unincorporated network** may issue credentials, hold records, publish a directory, or accept guardian consent"* | **A question, not a permission.** Rule G-8; **Part XXII class 2** |
| **J-2 Internal Krytos division** | **[S: App. A §3.2 row 1]** | Carried under **OPT-I** as a named variant; **GC-02** |
| **J-3 Federation of legally distinct state entities** | **[S-ST: §5 Scenario D; §23.1 S5]**; **[S-A: A-L7]**; **[S-B: §31.4]**; **[S-C: Annexure D]** | **LATER class.** **UE-30.** *"dilutes single-owner control … reduces founder flexibility — a deliberate trade"* |
| **J-4 Separate IP / brand-holding entity** | Not named as an option. **Derived** from **[S-E: N-E-17]** (*"held in trust"* as an ownership alternative), **[S-A: N13]**, **[S-ST: R4 (iii)]** (*"the **mission** held separately from both"*) | **⟨new — v1.3-G⟩.** Recorded because **UE-06** is a property question about assets that may be held apart from any operating entity. **Whether such a structure is available or advisable is a V-11/V-01 question** |
| **J-5 Separate custodian of reserves and/or the social-impact fund** | Not named as an entity option. **Derived** from **[S-A: §22 N15]** — *"Determine **holding entity**, instrument and change-of-control protections"* | **⟨new — v1.3-G⟩.** The source's own wording *"holding entity"* contemplates that the custodian may be a **different person** from the operator. **UE-09, UE-10** |
| **J-6 Section 8 company with a wholly-owned commercial subsidiary, or the reverse** | Not named. **Derived** from **[S-ST: R4 (ii)/(iii)]** and **[S: App. A §3.2 row 2]** | **⟨new — v1.3-G⟩.** A specific arrangement within OPT-H/OPT-I whose availability and consequences are **V-01/V-04** questions |
| **J-7 Any form counsel identifies that this instrument has not listed** | **[S-F: Q19]** *"other"*; **[S-E: N-E-24]** *"other"*; **[S-D: LDR-U08]** *"other"* | **The category is deliberately open.** **This paper's option list is not a closed set, and Part IV's matrix must be re-run for any option counsel adds** |

**[P] Rule G-17 (The Option List Is Open).** OPT-A…OPT-J is **a schedule of options for counsel to complete, not a menu to choose from.** Counsel may add forms, exclude forms as legally unavailable, or find that two listed options are the same object. **Part IV, Part XX and Part XXI must then be re-run.** Nothing in this instrument forecloses that.

## III.3 The source's own risk column, reproduced verbatim and in full

**[S]** **[S: App. A §3.2]** is the only place in 179 pages where the corpus weighs options against each other. **It is reproduced here in its entirety, unaltered, because it is the evidentiary basis for most `POTENTIALLY RESTRICTED` and `REQUIRES STRUCTURAL SEPARATION` entries in Part IV.**

| Possible legal architecture | Advantages *(verbatim)* | Risks / unresolved issues *(verbatim)* | Recommendation status *(verbatim — see Rule G-3)* |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Internal Krytos division** | Fast initial control; aligned with founder ownership; simpler brand integration. | **May mix charitable, commercial and protection risk with broader Krytos; public donations and social-impact funds need careful accounting.** | Option for early internal pilot only after validation. |
| **Separate company/subsidiary** | Cleaner contracts, accounts and liability separation; suitable for commercial ventures. | **Social service and donations may need separate treatment; governance must remain aligned with mission.** | Strong candidate for business network — requires professional structuring. |
| **Nonprofit entity / trust / society / Section 8 type structure** | Better mission credibility and eligibility for certain grants/donations. | **Restrictions on profit distribution and founder upside; may not fit commercial waterfall without separate structure.** | May be suitable for social-impact arm — requires legal/tax advice. |
| **Hybrid structure** | Separates commercial ventures from social programmes while preserving mission funding links. | **More complex accounting, governance and conflict-of-interest controls.** | Recommended for professional exploration; not approved until designed. |

**[A] Four observations, each an analysis of the table and none a recommendation:**

1. **Every row's risk column is a statement about the four pillars, not about the form.** *"May mix charitable, commercial and protection risk"*; *"social service and donations may need separate treatment"*; *"may not fit commercial waterfall"*; *"more complex accounting, governance and conflict-of-interest controls."* **The corpus evaluates structure by pillar fit — which is exactly what Part IV does.**
2. **No row's advantage column mentions cost, speed of formation, compliance burden or tax.** The only advantage-type claims are *control*, *brand integration*, *liability separation*, *cleaner contracts and accounts*, *mission credibility*, *grant/donation eligibility*, and *separation while preserving funding links*. **[A]** Cost and burden are conspicuously absent — and **[S-B: OC-22]** records that `C_comp` *"depends entirely on entity form [V]"* with **no amount anywhere.**
3. **The "Recommendation status" column is graded, and the grades are exploration grades.** *"only after validation"* · *"requires professional structuring"* · *"requires legal/tax advice"* · *"**not approved until designed**."* **All four end in a validation condition.** Rule G-3 applies.
4. **The table predates every v1.3 instrument.** It was written before the protection liability framework (A-10), before the costed unit model (v1.3-B), before the participation spectrum (v1.3-C), before the reserved-matters and succession architecture (A-14), before the brand/IP registers (v1.3-E) and before the consolidation register (v1.3-F). **[A]** Its risk column is therefore **incomplete by construction** — it does not mention insurance, data fiduciary status, bank mandates, worker classification, IP licensing, dissolution mechanics or the founder-succession problem. **Part IV supplies those dimensions; it does not replace the table.**

---

# PART IV — FOUR-PILLAR STRUCTURE COMPATIBILITY MATRIX

## IV.1 The five permitted values, and the rule that governs their use

**[P]** The brief fixes five permitted values. **No other value is used anywhere in this Part.**

| Code | Value | When it may be used |
|---|---|---|
| **PC** | **POTENTIALLY COMPATIBLE** | A corpus statement identifies this option as suited, or better suited, to this criterion — **without this instrument concluding that it is suitable** |
| **PR** | **POTENTIALLY RESTRICTED** | A corpus statement identifies a restriction, condition, added burden or risk attaching to this option for this criterion |
| **SS** | **REQUIRES STRUCTURAL SEPARATION** | A corpus statement says, in terms, that this criterion cannot be carried by this option without a separate structure — or that mixing creates a stated risk |
| **PV** | **REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION** | The corpus attaches a `[V]` to this criterion **independently of the option**, or the option-specific answer turns on a validation the corpus has not obtained |
| **UK** | **UNKNOWN FROM CURRENT CORPUS** | No corpus statement addresses this option/criterion pairing. **`UK` is not "unsuitable" and not "suitable." It is absence of evidence** |

**[P] Rule G-18 (The Matrix Records Source Statements, Not Legal Capability).** A **PC** cell never means "this form can lawfully do this." It means *"the corpus identifies an advantage here."* A **PR** cell never means "this form cannot do this." It means *"the corpus identifies a restriction or risk here."* **Whether any form may lawfully do anything is a counsel question, and every cell in this matrix is downstream of Part XIX.**

**[P] Rule G-19 (OPT-E, OPT-F, OPT-G Default Rule).** These three forms are **absent from the corpus** (§I.3, Rule G-16). Their cells read **`UK`** for every criterion where the corpus differentiates between forms, and **`PV`** for every criterion where the corpus attaches a validation requirement **independently of form**. **No cell for these three options reads PC, PR or SS anywhere in this matrix, because no corpus statement exists that could support one.**

**[P] Rule G-20 (An SS Cell Carries The Mandatory Sentence).** Wherever a cell reads **SS**, the following sentence is recorded and must be carried into any decision paper, counsel brief or human approval document that relies on this matrix:

> **STRUCTURAL SEPARATION MAY BE REQUIRED — COUNSEL TO DETERMINE.**

### IV.3.1 Cell counts, stated plainly

**[C]** Derived by counting the 330 cells above. **The distribution is the finding.**

| Code | Cells | Share of 330 |
|---|---|---|
| **PV — requires professional validation** | **253** | 76.7% |
| **UK — unknown from current corpus** | **27** | 8.2% |
| **PC — potentially compatible** | **22** | 6.7% |
| **PR — potentially restricted** | **22** | 6.7% |
| **SS — requires structural separation** | **6** | 1.8% |

**[A] Three observations on the distribution, none of which is a recommendation:**

1. **More than three quarters of the matrix is `PV`.** That is not a defect of the matrix; it is a faithful image of the corpus. **[S-F: Part III]** counts **77 validation items in 17 categories**, **[S-F: XIV.3 Finding 3]** records that *"every number the pilot needs is a number the pilot must produce"*, and **[S-A: Annexure A]** confirms *"Any legal, tax, insurance or privacy conclusion asserted? **No.**"* **A matrix that read mostly PC and PR would be a matrix that had invented legal conclusions.**
2. **All 27 `UK` cells belong to OPT-E, OPT-F and OPT-G** — nine each, being exactly the nine criteria on which **[S: App. A §3.2]** differentiates between forms (FC-01, FC-03, FC-04, FC-07, FC-08, FC-09, FC-14, FC-15, FC-23). Every other criterion carries a **form-independent** `[V]` in the corpus and therefore reads `PV` for all ten options. **This is the quantified form of §I.3's negative finding: three of the ten categories the brief requires cannot be evaluated from the corpus at all.**
3. **All 6 `SS` cells sit on the service/commerce boundary** — FC-01, FC-04, FC-09 and FC-24 — **and in two option groups**: OPT-A/B/C for the three commercial criteria, OPT-D and OPT-I for the social criteria. **The corpus locates the separation requirement precisely where [S-ST: R4] (*"conflicting legal natures"*) and [S-B: §38.2 B-27] (*the wall between the two sides*) both locate it.**

| **FC-23 A/B/C = PR** | **[S: App. A §3.2 row 3]** Risks + **[S: Ch.18 priority 8]** | *"Restrictions on **profit distribution and founder upside**"* — and priority 8 is *"Residual surplus / **founder upside**."* **[A]** The corpus contemplates **no equity investor anywhere**: `equity`, `share capital`, `paid-up capital` and `venture capital` (in the financing sense) return **zero occurrences**; `shareholder` appears **once**, in **[S-ST: F-11 item 7 (iv)]** (*"a federated structure where the mission is held by a body that **no single shareholder controls**"*). **Whether any form can carry an upside claim, and for whom, is a V-01/V-04 question** |
| **FC-24 A/B/C = PC** | **[S: App. A §3.2 row 3]** Advantages | *"eligibility for **certain grants/donations**"* — subject to **[X]** 80G cash-donation limits and **[X]** FCRA track record, both `[V]` |
| **FC-24 D = SS** | **[S: App. A §3.2 row 2]** Risks | *"Social service and **donations may need separate treatment**"* → **STRUCTURAL SEPARATION MAY BE REQUIRED — COUNSEL TO DETERMINE** |
| **FC-24 H = PC**, **FC-25 H = PC** | **[S: App. A §3.2 row 4]**; **[S-ST: R4 (ii)]** | *"preserving **mission funding links**"*; *"with a brand licence, **related-party agreements** and strict transfer-pricing discipline **[V]**"* |
| **FC-24 I = PR**, **FC-25 I = PR** | **[S: App. A §3.2 row 1]** Risks | *"**public donations** and social-impact funds need careful accounting"* |
| **FC-25 A/B/C = PC** | **[S: App. A §3.2 row 3]** Advantages | *"eligibility for **certain grants**/donations"* |
| **FC-28 I = PR** | **[S: App. A §3.2 row 1]** Risks | *"May mix charitable, commercial and **protection risk** with broader Krytos"* |

## IV.5 What the matrix does not show, stated explicitly

**[A]** Five limits, recorded so that no reader over-relies on the grid:

| Limit | Basis |
|---|---|
| **1. It is not a scoring instrument.** | **No weighting is authorised by the corpus.** **[S-F: Part XX]**: *"The corpus authorises no weighting."* Part XX therefore also produces no score |
| **2. It does not rank the options.** | Rule G-1. **A count of `PC` cells is not a measure of suitability** — the `PC` cells are unevenly distributed across criteria of radically different weight, and the corpus supplies no weight |
| **3. It does not assess affordability.** | **[S-B: OC-22]** `C_comp` *"depends entirely on entity form [V]"* with **no amount**; **[S-B: §8.6]** the centre is *"funded last and spent first"*; **`LDR-U17`** the economic blueprint was **never attached**; **[S-B: §46.2]** verdict **D — NOT YET ECONOMICALLY VIABLE**. **No option's compliance load can be weighed against any capacity to bear it** |
| **4. It does not assess timing or reversibility.** | **[S-ST: R4 (i)]** warns that deferring separation is *"expensive or **impossible later** once contracts and reputation attach to one name."* **Reversibility is a counsel question and is treated at Part VII and Part XX, not scored here** |
| **5. It is a snapshot of a corpus in State 1.** | **[S-F: Part XVIII]**: *"this register is itself a State 1 artefact about State 1 artefacts."* **The matrix will require re-running if counsel adds or excludes an option (Rule G-17), if UE-24 is disclosed, or if GC-01 is decided** |

**[P] Rule G-21 (The Matrix Is An Input, Not An Output).** This matrix exists to be **handed to counsel with Part XIX's briefs**, so that each `PV` and `UK` cell can be replaced by a professional answer, and each `PC`, `PR` and `SS` cell can be confirmed or corrected. **It is not a decision aid for use without that step.**
