# PART V — CHARITABLE / SOCIAL-PURPOSE SIDE ANALYSIS

## V.1 What the corpus says the social-purpose side actually is

**[A]** Before any structure can be assessed against it, the object must be stated from source. **The corpus describes a social-purpose side that is deliberately *not* a charity, and deliberately *not* only a charity.**

| Element | Source text | Entity consequence |
|---|---|---|
| **The mission's scope** | **[S: Ch.2]**: *"Help **people, communities, animals, environments** and legitimate causes while building **sustainable national capacity**."* **[S: App. A §1]**: *"Mission includes people, communities, animals, environment and legitimate causes requiring assistance"* | **At least four distinguishable beneficiary classes plus a capacity-building purpose.** Whether each is a permissible object of a given form, and whether *"building sustainable national capacity"* is a charitable purpose at all, are **V-01 / V-04** questions |
| **The express disclaimer** | **[S: Ch.2]**: *"**Not charity-only** — The organisation includes legitimate business and AI-enabled economic activity."* **[S: App. A §1]**: *"Its purpose is **not limited to charity**"*; *"The organisation is **not merely a donation-funded charity**; legitimate business is part of the design."* **[S: App. A §1.3]**: *"**The organisation is not converted into a conventional NGO-only model; the business component remains central**"* | **A source-preservation rule constrains the option set.** Any structure that forces an NGO-only model violates **[S: App. A §1.3]**. **This is not a preference; it is recorded as non-negotiable** |
| **The nine service categories** | **[S: Ch.15]** via **[S-B: §6.4]** — categories 1–3 time-costed, 4–5 mixed, 6–7 cash-intensive; **[S: Ch.3]** lists *"Education, food, health-support, cleaning, plantation, clothing, donations, community infrastructure, emergency, animal/environment support"* | Each carries its own permission surface — **[S-A: A-10 L11]**: *"What **permissions, notifications or registrations** are required for: **food distribution, health camps, ambulance-like transport, disaster response, animal handling, use of public space, and collection of donations or materials**?"* **[S-ST: Annexure B Sector permissions]** adds *"**no diagnosis/treatment without licence**"* |
| **The funding position** | **[S-B: §33.3]**: *"**Service continues when revenue falls only to the extent that service can be delivered in *time* rather than *money*.**"*; *"**The one obligation that may never stop — protecting a person from immediate harm [S: Ch.16] — is therefore unfunded by design**"*; **[S-B: §33.4]**: *"**Without B-24, a donor cannot fund service even if they wish to, because there is no instrument to receive the gift**"* | **The absence of a legal person is what makes a gift unreceivable.** This is the sharpest charitable-side consequence in the corpus |
| **The restricted fund** | **[S: Ch.20; App. A §21.2]** — restricted programme list: *"education, scholarships/learning support, adult training, emergency participant support, community infrastructure, safeguarding capability and certain health/nutrition support"*; exclusions: *"ordinary operating salaries, venture bailouts and **political or religious activity**"*; *"These restrictions are preserved **unless the founder formally amends them after professional validation**"* | A restricted fund requires a **restricted instrument** (**UE-10**). Its restrictions are policy today and would need to become enforceable obligations |
| **The neutrality condition** | **[S-A: A-01 N1–N9]** organisational prohibitions; **[S-A: A-05]** Equal Treatment and Non-Alignment Principle; **[S-A: §3.1 A.3]** — *"Whether **income-tax exemption for a charitable entity is conditional on absence of political activity** or support to parties/candidates, and the consequence of breach"* → *"Published practitioner guidance indicates such a condition exists and that **registration can be jeopardised [X]** — must be confirmed for the actual entity and circumstances"* | **Neutrality is simultaneously an organisational policy and, potentially, a condition of tax standing.** **[S-A: A-B4]** makes the Neutrality Code a BLOCKER for *"any public activity, distribution, partnership or media engagement"* |
| **The dignity condition** | **[S-A: A-19]** Beneficiary Privacy & Dignity Code; **[S-B: §38]** What Must Be Free; **[S-B: §39]** NM-1…NM-12 never-monetised list; **[S-B: §38.2 B-27 rule 1]** *"A beneficiary is never a sales prospect"* | **The social-purpose side carries prohibitions that a commercial entity would not ordinarily carry.** Whether they can be made enforceable is **UE-31** |
| **The protection adjacency** | **[S-ST: §29]**: Sewak Sathi may act as *"a documented, neutral, **non-authority-bearing interface**"* and must never *"**broker access to government schemes for a fee**"* | **A boundary constraint on the objects clause and on the commercial pillar simultaneously** |

## V.2 Objects-clause requirements, as the corpus states them

**[A]** *"Objects clause"* is the most repeated entity-document requirement in the corpus. **Four independent statements:**

| Source | Text |
|---|---|
| **[S-A: A-V1]** | Corporate/entity counsel must advise on *"Entity form under Krytos; political-activity restrictions attaching to that form; **objects clauses**; protective provisions; dissolution and restructuring"* |
| **[S-ST: Annexure B Corporate/entity ◆]** | *"**objects clauses permitting all four pillars**; **feasibility of altering objects later**"* |
| **[S-E: LV-E-1]** | *"Entity structure, **objects clause** and lawful public description — Determines **what may be said about status**, and 12A / 80G / FCRA applicability"* |
| **[S-F: BLK-B-01, BLK-C-01, P0-A, PAK-01]** | Validate *"Entity form, **objects clauses permitting all four pillars**, dissolution/restructuring"*; build *"Registration, **objects clause**, articles or deed"*; P0-A evidence *"Certificate of registration; **objects clause**"*; PAK-01 artefact *"Certificate of registration and **objects clause permitting all four pillars**"* |

**[C] Derived requirement set.** From those four statements and from §V.1, an objects clause would have to accommodate, **at minimum**:

| # | Purpose the objects would have to reach | Corpus anchor |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Relief and assistance to people and communities | **[S: Ch.2, Ch.15]** |
| 2 | **Animal welfare** | **[S: Ch.2]** *"animals"*; **[S: Ch.15]** *"animal/environment support"*; **[S-ST: Annexure B]** *"animal-welfare legality"* |
| 3 | **Environmental** purposes — cleaning, plantation | **[S: Ch.2]** *"environments"*; **[S: Ch.15]** |
| 4 | **Education and learning support**, including adult training and digital/AI literacy | **[S: Ch.20]**; **[S: App. A §13.2]** *"Education/training — Digital skills, AI literacy, local business training"* |
| 5 | **Food distribution and health-support** (not diagnosis or treatment) | **[S: Ch.15]**; **[S-ST: Annexure B]** *"no diagnosis/treatment without licence"*; **[S-A: L11]** |
| 6 | **Emergency and disaster response**, coordinated with statutory bodies | **[S: Ch.16]**; **[S-A: L11]**; **[S-ST: Annexure B]** |
| 7 | **Community infrastructure** | **[S: Ch.20]** |
| 8 | **Safeguarding capability** | **[S: Ch.20]**; **[S-A: A-B6]** |
| 9 | **Protection / first response as a defensive, de-escalatory, authority-linked function** — with the nine DO-NOTs | **[S: Ch.16; Const. Art.9]**; **[S-A: A-07, A-08]** |
| 10 | **Community networking, need identification and local information flow** | **[S: Ch.3 pillar 2]** |
| 11 | **Legitimate commercial activity** — software, websites, apps, AI automation, digital services, design, editing, analytics, education/training services, local business technology, physical products and services | **[S: Ch.17; App. A §13.2]**; **[S: App. A §1.3]** *"the business component remains central"* |
| 12 | **Capacity-building — "building sustainable national capacity"** | **[S: Ch.2]** |
| 13 | **Receiving and applying donations, grants and restricted gifts** | **[S: Ch.20]**; **[S: App. D SOP-07]** |
| 14 | **Holding and applying reserves and a restricted social-impact fund** | **[S: Ch.18 priorities 6, 7; App. A §20.2, §21]** |
| 15 | **Owning or licensing a name, mark, architecture, SOPs, curricula, software and data models** | **A-N12, A-N13**; **[S-E: N-E-17…N-E-19]** |
| 16 | **Processing personal data across four classification levels, including sealed safeguarding records** | **[S-A: A-17…A-20]** |
| 17 | **Engaging volunteers and paying workers, contractors and specialists** | **[S-C: §6]**; **LDR-U09** |
| 18 | **Forming partnerships and MOUs with municipalities, panchayats, schools, PHCs, hospitals, NGOs, faith-body *service* committees and CSR bodies** | **[S-E: Part 25]**; **[S: Ch.29]** |
| 19 | **Operating across states** | **[S: Ch.4, Ch.6]**; **[S-ST: §5 item 3]** |
| 20 | **Dissolving, merging, restructuring and transferring assets** | **[S: Const. Art.19]** |

**[A]** **[S-ST: Annexure B]** requires counsel to advise on the *"**feasibility of altering objects later**"* — because **[S-ST: R4 (i)]** warns that a single entity chosen now may be *"expensive or impossible"* to restructure *"once contracts and reputation attach to one name."* **Whether items 11, 14 and 15 in particular can coexist with items 1–10 in one objects clause is the central V-01 question, and this paper does not answer it.**

**[P] Rule G-22 (No Registration Benefit Is Asserted).** Nothing in Part V states or implies that registering in any form **produces** exemption, deduction, eligibility, permission or credibility. **[S: App. A §3.2]**'s phrase *"eligibility for **certain** grants/donations"* is reproduced as a source claim with its own `[X]`/`[V]` markers intact. **Rule G-10 governs.**

**[P] Rule G-23 (The Charter-Only Character Is Not A Substitute For An Entity).** **[S-C: H-05]** requires a charter with nine elements, and **[S-C: HD-01]** proposes the nine-level spectrum. **[A]** A charter is an internal instrument; **whether it can be the *only* instrument — i.e. whether an unincorporated network may operate on a charter alone — is precisely [S-C: HV-6]'s unanswered question.** Rule G-8 applies.

# PART VI — COMMERCIAL / AI BUSINESS SIDE ANALYSIS

## VI.1 The commercial side, as the corpus defines it

**[S]** **[S: App. A §13.1]**: *"The AI Business Network is a **serious economic division**. It is not simply members using ChatGPT, and it is not a cover for unrecorded work. It exists to **create legitimate customer value, generate revenue, compensate verified work, build reserves, strengthen local skills and contribute to social programmes** when surplus conditions are met."*

**[S]** **[S: Const. Art.11 Business]** `APPROVED ARCHITECTURE`: *"**Legitimate business is a core function.** Ventures require approval, real customer value, recorded revenue, quality control and closure rules."*

**[A]** The commercial side is therefore **not an optional adjunct**: it is `APPROVED` architecture, a *"core function"*, the **only revenue line in the entire model** (**[S-F: XIV.1]**: *"there is no revenue line for service, for governance, for training, or — as v1.3-E established — for communication"*), and — per **[S: App. A §1.3]** — *"the business component remains central."*

## VI.2 The commercial chain, node by node, with entity consequences

**[P]** The brief specifies the chain. **Each node is mapped to its corpus anchor and to the entity question it raises.** No node is asserted to be lawful or unlawful.

| # | Chain node | Corpus anchor | **Entity consequence** | Unresolved item |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Customer** | **[S: App. A §13.4]** *"Customers may be acquired through local relationships, referrals, public portfolios, approved marketing, partnerships and direct outreach"*; **[S-B: §18]** acquisition funnel, *"**CAC** mostly in hours"*; **[S-E: Rule E-175]** no growth campaign, **Rule E-25** emotion is never a budget line | **Who is the customer's counterparty?** A customer deals with a person, a team or an entity — and **[S-B: §3.2]** says the team is *"not a legal entity."* Consumer-protection duties, warranty and complaint routes attach to **somebody** | **UE-01, UE-26, UE-32**; **[S-B: EV-6]** *"consumer law"*; **[S-E: LV-E-24]** *"Consumer protection and service warranties for venture work"* |
| **2** | **Contract** | **[S: App. A §13.4]** *"Every customer engagement should define **scope, price or pricing basis, timeline, acceptance criteria, revision limits, data access, IP ownership, confidentiality, payment terms and complaint route**"*; **[S: App. E]** *"Customer contract — Business owner within template; Finance/Legal if non-standard"*; **[S: Ch.31]** *"Contract templates for customers and members — **Dependency: Entity structure** — Blocker"* | **Capacity to contract is the entity question in its purest form.** The launch checklist makes entity structure the **express dependency** of the contract templates. **IP ownership is already a required contract term** | **UE-01, UE-26**; **A-N13**; **[S-B: B-13]** collection terms |
| **3** | **Revenue** | **[S: Ch.18 steps 1–2]** *"Customer revenue → **Venture revenue account** — recorded before allocation"*; **[S: App. A §14.3]** *"Venture revenue account — Designated account/ledger where customer revenue for an approved venture is recorded before allocation"*; **[S-B: E-07]** gross-revenue basis *"receipts or invoiced — **UNRESOLVED**, the source explicitly leaves it open"* | **Whose account?** **[S-B: §3.2]**: the venture revenue account's *"**ownership and custody are unresolved** (v1.3-A N14, N15)."* The accounting basis is unresolved and is a **tax and labour** question as well as an accounting one | **UE-03, UE-09**; **[S-B: E-07, EV-1]**; **UE-27** |
| **4** | **Delivery** | **[S: App. A §13.4]** *"Work is assigned based on verified skills and availability; **quality is reviewed before delivery**; revenue is recorded at source"*; **[S: App. A §13.5]** scale/close rules; **[S-B: §17]** capacity constraint | **Who is liable for defective delivery?** **[S-A: A-10 L5]** asks what professional indemnity is required *"where members give advice (legal, tax, medical-adjacent, technical, engineering, safeguarding)"*; **[S-B: EV-6]** asks about *"exclusion of liability for venture work"* | **UE-13**; **[S-A: L5]**; **[S-D: LV-24]** |
| **5** | **Worker compensation** (priority 1) | **[S: Ch.18 priority 1]**; **[S: Ch.19]** 13-step weekly cycle; **[S-D: INV-2]** *"never suspended by a dispute, a process, a gate or a score"*; **[S-B: B-05]** accrued-compensation ledger, **B-06** pro-rata scarcity rule | **The single hardest fact in the corpus.** **[S-F: XIV.3 Finding 2]**: *"Priority 1 is a legal obligation the organisation **cannot currently discharge** … Until they close, the architecture's first and most absolute financial obligation is one the organisation **cannot lawfully perform**."* Paying it requires an entity (**UE-01**), a classification (**UE-02**) and a mandate (**UE-03**) | **UE-01, UE-02, UE-03**; **A-B1, A-B9** |
| **6** | **Statutory obligations** (priority 2) | **[S: Ch.18 priority 2]** *"Where applicable under legal/tax/employment structure"*; **[S: App. A §14.3]** *"Taxes, deductions, labour or other legally required amounts **applicable to the legal structure and worker classification**"*; **[S-B: PC-10]** `C_stat = f(C_wc, classification)` | **Not computable.** **[S-B: PC-10]**: *"Classification UNRESOLVED → **amount not computable [V]**."* **[S-B: §8.3]** defines **S-2 Legal solvency** as `V_gross ≥ R_1 + R_2` — a line that cannot be evaluated | **UE-01, UE-02**; **Part XIV** |
| **7** | **Costs** (priority 3 direct; plus the `C_L1` existence cost) | **[S: Ch.18 priority 3]**; **[S-B: §4.1–§4.4]** `C_var`, `C_L1`, `C_stat`, `C_ins`, `C_comp`, `C_city`; **[S-B: §21.2]** *"the one cost that a new team reliably has and **no fund is permitted to cover is `C_L1` — the cost of existing**"* | **`C_comp` (compliance / statutory filing) "depends entirely on entity form [V]" [S-B: OC-22]** — with **no amount anywhere.** The entity decision **creates** a cost line whose magnitude the corpus cannot state | **UE-01**; **[S-B: OC-22, BLK-D-07]**; **UE-12** |
| **8** | **Reserves** (priority 6) | **[S: Ch.18 priority 6]**; **[S: App. A §20.2]** *"National Funds Manager manages; release requires approval matrix"*; **[S-B: §8.5, B-03, B-15]**; **[S: App. D SOP-10]** | **Custody is `UNRESOLVED` (A-N15)** and **[S-F: chain 27]** warns: *"**a restricted fund held in an unrestricted instrument is not a reserve.**"* Seeding requires a lawful source — *"founder capital, Krytos, or a validated donation"* **[S-B: B-03]** — each with its own `[V]` | **UE-03, UE-09, UE-12** |
| **9** | **Organisation service fee** (priority 5) | **[S: Ch.18 priority 5]** *"Sustains organisational infrastructure after higher-priority items"*; **[S-B: §8.6]** *"**The centre is funded last and spent first**"* | **The fee flows to "the organisation" — which has no legal existence.** Who receives it, invoices for it, and accounts for it is `UNKNOWN`. **The 12% illustrative figure is `NOT FINAL`** (**LDR-U18**; Rule G-6) | **UE-01, UE-12**; **GC-01** |
| **10** | **Social impact** (priority 7) | **[S: Ch.18 priority 7]** *"Only when surplus conditions are met; restricted use"*; **[S: Ch.20]**; **[S-B: §21.1, §33.2]** | **A restricted fund with no instrument.** And **[S-F: XIV.2 row 8]**: priority 7 is *"**NO — priority 7 is reached only if 1–6 are satisfied**"* | **UE-10, UE-11** |
| *(11)* | *(Residual surplus / founder upside — priority 8)* | **[S: Ch.18 priority 8]**; **[S: App. A §14.3]** *"Remaining surplus after priority obligations and caps **as defined by approved structure**"* | **Defined by the structure.** **GC-01** bites here: **[S: App. A §3.2 row 3]** records *"restrictions on **profit distribution and founder upside**"* for the nonprofit family | **UE-20**; **GC-01** |

**[A]** **The chain's structural finding:** **nodes 5, 6, 8, 9 and 10 each name a recipient, a payer or a custodian, and none of them exists.** The chain is `APPROVED` architecture operating without a legal subject at any node. **[S-F: chain 4]** states it in one line: *"there is no legal person to incur cost, hold an account, employ anyone or be audited; **the pilot's economic measurements have no subject**."*

## VI.3 The venture categories, with entity consequence per category

**[S]** **[S: App. A §13.2]** lists eleven potential venture categories **with the source's own approval note.** The notes are reproduced verbatim; the entity consequence column is **[A]** analysis derived only from those notes and from other corpus statements.

| Category | Source approval note *(verbatim)* | **Entity consequence [A]** | Validation |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Software development** — custom tools, internal systems, scripts, simple SaaS | *"Requires technical quality, **customer contract** and **data/security review**"* | A contracting party; a data processor role; IP in deliverables; warranty | V-01, V-06, V-07 |
| **Websites** | *"Low-risk if **data/privacy and delivery scope** are clear"* | Contract; data processing; IP | V-06, V-07 |
| **Apps** | *"Higher-risk; requires **technical, legal and support review**"* | Contract; product liability; data; IP; ongoing support obligation | V-01, V-06, V-07 |
| **AI automation** — chatbots, document workflows, summarisation, operations automation | *"**Must disclose limitations; no unlawful data processing or autonomous consequential decisions**"* | **[S: Const. Art.12]** `APPROVED`; **A-N36/A-N37** AI risk tiers and auditability; data fiduciary (**UE-07**); **AI-generated content disclosure** **[S-E: LV-E-25]** | V-07, V-16, V-01 |
| **Digital services** — data entry, online listings, CRM setup, e-commerce support | *"**Customer data controls required**"* | Processor contracts (**A-N30**); cross-border processing **[S-A: A-V6]** | V-07, V-06 |
| **Design** — branding, posters, packaging, social creatives | *"**IP ownership and content rights must be clear**"* | **The source itself makes IP ownership a condition of the category.** Who owns the output — the organisation, the member, the customer? **A-N13, UE-06** | V-11 ◆ |
| **Editing** — video, copy, reports, educational material | *"**Fact-check and consent controls**"* | Consent, image rights, defamation exposure **[S-E: LV-E-22]** | V-07, V-16 |
| **Analytics** — dashboards, reports, business insights | *"**Data source legality and confidentiality controls**"* | Lawful basis; confidentiality; **aggregate/small-cell suppression** **[S-D: Rule D-108]** | V-07 |
| **Education / training** — digital skills, AI literacy, local business training | *"**Trainer competence and safeguarding when minors are present**"* | **A-B6** certified safeguarding; **credential issuance** (**CS-21**); trainer status (**[S-D: Rule D-47]** trainer time is paid work → **UE-02**) | V-08 ◆, V-05 |
| **Local business technology** — POS support, inventory sheets, WhatsApp automation where lawful | *"**Do not access customer accounts without permissions**"* | Authorisation, data access, processor terms | V-07, V-06 |
| **Physical products / services** | *"**Health, safety, licensing, tax and liability review required**"* | **The heaviest permission surface in the list.** Sector permissions (**CS-15**), product liability, GST/indirect tax, statutory dues | V-02 ◆, V-04, V-06, V-09 |

**[A]** **[S: Ch.33]** records venture categories as `POTENTIAL` — *"Approve categories one by one using risk/value framework."* **[S-B: §19]** supplies that framework and **[S-B: E-C7]** carries the decision. **No category is approved, and this paper approves none.**

Art.11]** makes legitimate business `APPROVED` architecture and **[S: App. A §1.3]** makes it central. **But no venture category is approved ([S: Ch.33 `POTENTIAL`]), no contract template exists ([S: Ch.31 `Not started`]), and no revenue has been earned.** The commercial side is a **requirement of the architecture with no legal subject.**

# PART VII — HYBRID STRUCTURE / MULTI-ENTITY ANALYSIS

## VII.1 Why this Part exists separately

**[A]** Three independent source statements make multi-entity arrangements a distinct object of analysis rather than a variant of Part III:

1. **[S-ST: R4]** — *"service …, business … and protection … have **conflicting legal natures. A single entity will serve none of them well.**"*
2. **[S: App. A §3.2 row 3]** — a nonprofit form *"**may not fit commercial waterfall without separate structure**."*
3. **[S: App. A §3.2 row 4]** — the hybrid's stated advantage is *"**Separates commercial ventures from social programmes while preserving mission funding links**"*, and its stated risk is *"**More complex accounting, governance and conflict-of-interest controls**."*

**[P] Rule G-25 (Separation Is A Consequence To Be Determined, Not A Recommendation).** Wherever this Part records that separation may be required, the mandatory sentence applies:

> **STRUCTURAL SEPARATION MAY BE REQUIRED — COUNSEL TO DETERMINE.**

## VII.2 The five conceptual architectures

**[A]** Each is described **only** by what it would have to solve and what new questions it would create. **None is proposed, preferred or costed.**

### HY-01 — Single entity carrying all four pillars

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| **Corpus provenance** | **[S-ST: R4 (i)]** *"**single entity now, restructure later** — cheap now, **expensive or impossible later once contracts and reputation attach to one name**"*; **[S-ST: Annexure B]** *"**single** vs. two-entity vs. holding"*; **[S: App. A §3.2 rows 1–3]** each describe a single person |
| **What it would have to solve** | (a) an objects clause reaching all twenty purposes at §V.2 — **[S-ST: Annexure B]** *"objects clauses **permitting all four pillars**"*; (b) **GC-01** — priorities 4 and 8 of an `APPROVED` waterfall inside a form whose source-recorded risk is *"restrictions on profit distribution and founder upside"*; (c) accounting separation of restricted from unrestricted funds **inside one set of books** — **[S: App. A §20.2]** already requires segregated ledgers; (d) the application-of-income question **[X]** with commercial receipts in the same income pool; (e) **one liability pool** for service, commercial and protection activity |
| **New questions it creates** | Whether mixing *"charitable, commercial and protection risk"* is acceptable (**[S: App. A §3.2 row 1]**'s own words); whether a single insurance programme can be written across all three natures (**L1, L3, L4, L5, L7**); whether donor confidence survives commercial activity in the same person (**[S-C: HV-9]**); whether **reversibility** exists — **[S-ST: R4]** says restructuring later may be *"impossible"* |
| **Validation preconditions** | V-01, V-03, V-04, V-05, V-06, V-07 — **all of Part XIX** |

### HY-02 — Social-purpose entity + commercial operating entity (**the corpus's "two-entity" option**)

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| **Corpus provenance** | **[S-ST: R4 (ii)]** *"**two entities from the start** — a **not-for-profit service entity** and a **for-profit venture entity** — with a **brand licence, related-party agreements and strict transfer-pricing discipline [V]**"*; **[S-F: Q19]** *"a **two-entity structure**"*; **[S-A: §11.3 item 1]** *"two-entity separation"*; **[S-D: LDR-U08]**; **[S-E: N-E-24]** |
| **What it would have to solve** | (a) **which pillar goes where** — the corpus assigns *service* and *business* but says nothing explicit about **protection/first response** or the **community network**; (b) a **brand licence** between the two — which requires **A-N12** to be decided first (**UE-05**); (c) **related-party agreements** and **transfer-pricing discipline** `[V]`; (d) **money movement** between the two — the waterfall's priority 5 organisation fee and priority 7 social-impact contribution would cross an entity boundary; (e) **two governance systems**, or one shared board; (f) **two data-fiduciary determinations** or one; (g) **worker classification across both** — a person may work for both |
| **New questions it creates** | **[S-ST: R4 new risk]**, verbatim: *"administrative burden, **related-party scrutiny**, and a possible **public perception of 'charity and business being mixed'** whichever way it is done; **donor confusion**."* Plus: **conflict-of-interest controls** (**[S: App. A §3.2 row 4]**); **which entity employs whom**; **which entity is insured for which activity**; **which entity is the counterparty to a customer and which to a donor**; **what happens if one fails and the other survives**; **whether the founder's ownership of both creates a self-dealing surface** (**[S-D: Rule D-92]** limb (iv) *"directed expenditure outside the waterfall"*) |
| **Validation preconditions** | All of HY-01's, **plus** V-04 on related-party and transfer pricing, V-11 on the brand licence, V-10 on dual governance, V-06 on cross-entity insurance, V-03 on consolidated and separate accounting |
| **Note on the protection pillar** | **[A]** **The corpus never states where protection/first response belongs in a two-entity split.** It is *"liability-heavy, insurance-dependent"* **[S-ST: R4]** and neither charitable nor commercial in nature. **This is a gap the brief's option list does not close and counsel must be asked to close — `UNKNOWN FROM CURRENT CORPUS`** |

### HY-03 — Parent / holding entity + operating entity (or entities)

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| **Corpus provenance** | **[S-ST: R4 (iii)]** *"a **holding structure with the mission held separately from both**"*; **[S-ST: Annexure B]** *"holding"*; **[S: Ch.1]** and **[S: App. A §3.1]** — the organisation is *already* conceived as *"an operating organisation and product ecosystem component **owned by Krytos**"*; **[S: App. A §3.2 row 2]** *"Separate company/**subsidiary**"* |
| **What it would have to solve** | (a) **UE-24** — what Krytos *is*; (b) how *"the mission"* is **held** by a holding body and by what instrument; (c) whether **[S-A: §11.2]**'s finding is accepted or cured — *"**Whoever owns Krytos owns the operating system of the institution; a licensor could withdraw it**"*; (d) whether **[S-A: §11.2]**'s other finding is accepted — *"**The entity's control follows Krytos's own succession, which is outside this architecture entirely**"*; (e) **Rule E-58/E-59** — the parent is named accurately in legal and financial contexts and subordinated in service contexts, which requires the parent to be **nameable** |
| **New questions it creates** | **[S: App. A §3.2 row 1]**: *"**May mix charitable, commercial and protection risk with broader Krytos**"* — i.e. the sibling projects LifeXP, Code Forge, App Forge, Trade Lab **[S: App. A §3.1]** share the parent's risk pool; whether the parent may fund the centre (**UE-12**: *"founder capital / **Krytos** / validated donation / accrued-and-waived fee"*); whether Krytos's own governing documents override anything Sewak Sathi adopts (**[S-A: A-14 S5]**: *"**Krytos's governing documents take effect in parallel [V]**"*); **E-C-15** — the brand owned by a person while the identity must not be a person |
| **Validation preconditions** | V-01, V-10 (including *"**Krytos governing documents**"* per **A-V9**), V-11, V-04 on intra-group transactions, V-07 on which entity is fiduciary — **[S-A: §11.2]**: *"presumably the entity, i.e. **ultimately Krytos**"* |

### HY-04 — Separate custodian of reserves and/or the social-impact fund

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| **Corpus provenance** | **Derived from [S-A: §22 N15]**, whose stated action is *"Determine **holding entity**, instrument and **change-of-control protections**"* — the phrase *"holding entity"* contemplates that the custodian may be a person other than the operator. Also **[S-E: N-E-17]**'s ownership alternative *"**held in trust**"* |
| **What it would have to solve** | (a) **[S-A: §11.2]**: *"Restricted purpose survives in policy but **not in any instrument**; a successor could redefine 'restricted'"*; (b) **[S-F: chain 27]**: *"a restricted fund held in an unrestricted instrument is not a reserve"*; (c) **A-N16** mandate continuity for the custodian as well as the operator; (d) **change-of-control protections** — the corpus's own words |
| **New questions it creates** | Who appoints and removes the custodian; whether the custodian is a fiduciary in law; whether release authority (**[S: App. E]** *"Reserve release — National approval matrix"*) can be exercised by an operator over money held by another; whether **SOP-10**'s abuse flags (*"personal request, venture bailout, missing approvals"*) are enforceable against a third-party custodian; **donor-restriction traceability** across two persons |
| **Validation preconditions** | V-10 banking/finance ◆ (*"custody instruments for reserves and the restricted social-impact fund"* — **A-V10** verbatim), V-01, V-03 restricted-fund accounting, V-04 |
| **Status** | **⟨new — v1.3-G⟩** as a distinct architecture. The corpus names the *requirement* (holding entity, instrument, change-of-control protections) but never analyses a separate custodian as a structure |

### HY-05 — Separate IP / brand-holding entity

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| **Corpus provenance** | **Derived from [S-A: §22 N13]** (*"Determine ownership and **any licence to the operating entity**"*), **[S-E: N-E-17]** (*"held in trust"*; *"licensed"*), **[S-A: §11.2]** (*"**a licensor could withdraw it**"*), **[S-ST: F-11 item 7 (ii)]** (*"transfer of the brand"* as a suspensive-veto matter) |
| **What it would have to solve** | (a) **A-N13** — the architecture, SOPs, curricula, software and data models have no owner; (b) **A-N12** — teams hold no documented right to the name; (c) **[S-E: N-E-19]** — domain and digital-asset ownership, with **Rule E-188** prohibiting personal registration; (d) the risk that *"the institution's operating system could be **withdrawn by a licensor**"* |
| **New questions it creates** | Licence terms, scope, duration, royalty (if any), termination, and **what happens on termination**; whether the operating entity can be **sued, funded or dissolved** while the IP sits elsewhere; **transfer-pricing** on any licence fee; whether a licence from a founder-controlled holder to an operating entity is a **related-party transaction** requiring disclosure; **post-exit restrictions** on departing teams (**A-N12** action column) |
| **Validation preconditions** | V-11 ◆, V-01, V-04 on intra-group licensing, V-10 on who controls the holder |
| **Status** | **⟨new — v1.3-G⟩.** Not named as an option by any instrument |

### HY-06 — Federation of legally distinct state entities

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| **Corpus provenance** | **[S-ST: §5 Scenario D]**; **[S-ST: §23.1 S5]**; **[S-A: A-L7]**; **[S-B: §31.4]**; **[S-C: Annexure D]**; **[S-ST: F-11 item 7 (iv)]** |
| **Corpus text** | *"**federation**: legally distinct state entities bound by a **common constitution, standards body and brand licence** — the model used by large mass-membership institutions worldwide. **Federation also solves inter-state legal heterogeneity [V] and dilutes single-owner control** (which mitigates F-11 but **reduces founder flexibility — a deliberate trade**)"*; at S5: *"**Independent trustees, statutory-scale audit, public-interest reporting, open standards**"*; **[S-ST: F-11 (iv)]**: *"a federated structure where **the mission is held by a body that no single shareholder controls**"* |
| **Classification** | **LATER.** **[S-A: A-L7]**: *"Full federation or state-entity structure — **A scale question**"*; **[S-B: §31.4]** places S5 at *"1M+ members / 10M+ participants"* |
| **Why it is recorded here anyway** | **[S-ST: R4 (i)]**: deferring separation may be *"expensive or **impossible later** once contracts and reputation attach to one name."* **[A]** The first-entity decision should be taken **knowing whether it forecloses federation** — which is a question for counsel, not for this paper |
| **Validation preconditions** | V-01, V-10, V-11 (the brand licence is the federation's binding instrument), V-02 per state, V-04 |

## VII.3 The ten validation preconditions of any multi-entity structure

**[P]** Compiled from **[S-ST: Annexure B]**, **[S-ST: R4]**, **[S-A: A-V1, A-V2, A-V8, A-V9, A-V10]**, **[S-B: EV-1…EV-6]** and **[S-F: V-01…V-17]**. **Each is a question for counsel; none is answered.**

| # | Precondition | Source |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Ownership and control of each entity** — who holds it, on what instrument, with what voting or protective rights | **[S-A: A-V1]** *"protective provisions"*; **[S-ST: F-11 (ii)]** *"suspensive veto (not ownership)"*; **[S-D: LV-8]** *"board composition"* |
| **2** | **Governance of each entity and across entities** — one board or several; how a reserved matter is decided when it affects both | **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]**; **UE-14, UE-19, UE-25** |
| **3** | **Tax characterisation of each entity and of every transaction between them** | **[S-B: EV-2]**; **[S-ST: R4 (ii)]** *"strict transfer-pricing discipline [V]"* |
| **4** | **Related-party transaction discipline** — identification, approval, pricing, disclosure, and recusal | **[S-ST: Annexure B]** *"related-party and transfer-pricing discipline if service and business are separated"*; **[S-D: Rule D-92]**; **[S-A: A-14 S6]** conflict of interest |
| **5** | **Money movement between entities** — may the waterfall's priority 5 fee and priority 7 contribution cross an entity boundary, and as what? | **[S: Ch.18]**; **[S-B: §21.4]** *"money cannot move laterally"*; **UE-09, UE-10** |
| **6** | **IP and brand licensing between entities** — terms, scope, termination, and what happens on termination | **[S-ST: Annexure B]** *"brand licensing between entities"*; **[S-A: N12, N13]**; **[S-E: LV-E-4, LV-E-5]** |
| **7** | **Banking across entities** — mandates, signatories, and whether the A-N16 continuity rule must hold in each | **A-N16**; **[S-A: A-V10]**; **[S-B: EV-10]**; **UE-03** |
| **8** | **Employment across entities** — may one person work for both, and for whom are they classified? | **[S-C: HV-1]**; **[S-B: EV-3]**; **UE-02** |
| **9** | **Data-fiduciary determination per entity and per data category** | **[S-A: §22 N14]** action column, verbatim: *"**Assign per entity and per data category**"* |
| **10** | **Audit and consolidation** — separate accounts, consolidated view, statutory audit for each, and internal-audit independence across both | **[S-ST: Annexure B]** *"statutory audit arrangements; internal-audit independence"*; **[S-F: V-03, V-13]**; **UE-27** |

## VII.4 The conflicts multi-entity structures would have to be tested against

**[A]** Ten architectural constraints that any separation must survive. **Each is a source statement; none is a veto this instrument exercises.**

| # | Constraint | Source | Test for a separated structure |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **The waterfall priority order is `APPROVED` and a reserved matter** | **[S: Ch.18]**; **[S-D: LDR-C16, LDR-C26]**; **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]** | Can priorities 1–8 be honoured **in sequence** when the money crosses an entity boundary at priority 5 and again at priority 7? **GC-01** |
| **2** | **Worker compensation is non-suspendable** | **[S-D: INV-2]**; **[S-A: A-14 §13.6]** | Does separation create any state in which priority 1 depends on a transfer between two persons? |
| **3** | **The social-impact fund may not fund operating salaries or bail out ventures** | **[S: Ch.20]**; **[S-B: §21.2]** | Does a cross-entity transfer from the commercial side to the social side look like a bailout, or from the social side to the commercial side like a salary subsidy? |
| **4** | **Money cannot move laterally between teams** | **[S-B: §21.4]** | Does separation create a lateral route that the single-entity model forbids? |
| **5** | **Four segregated finance hands; no single person receives, approves, records and audits** | **[S: Ch.18; App. A §20.3]**; **[S-D: C-D-06, Rule D-103]** | Are there four hands **per entity**, and does a shared officer break the segregation? |
| **6** | **A beneficiary is never a sales prospect; free and paid must be separable** | **[S-B: §38.2 B-27]**; **[S-A: A-19]**; **[S-A: A-20 §19.5]** prohibits commercial use of sensitive data | Does separation **help** (a structural wall) or **hurt** (a shared data estate across the wall)? |
| **7** | **No uniform, no insignia, no worn mark; brand visibility is deliberately low** | **[S-A: A-06 V1–V3]**; **[S-E: Rule E-65, E-C-05]** | Does a shared brand licence between two entities increase public adjacency risk? |
| **8** | **Parent subordination in service contexts; accurate entity naming in legal contexts** | **[S-E: Rule E-58, E-59]**; brand tiers **T1 master / T2 parent / T3 local / T4 venture** | Which of two or three entities is T1, and does the public see a structure it cannot understand? **[S-E: §12]**: *"No human being holds eleven concepts"* **[S-ST: §18.1]** |
| **9** | **Political and religious non-alignment** | **[S-A: A-01 N1–N9, A-05]**; **[X]** 12A political-activity condition | If one entity is exempt and the other is not, whose activity jeopardises whose status? **[S-A: §3.1 A.3 row 3]** *"Whether donation/deduction eligibility for donors is affected by **any political adjacency**"* |
| **10** | **The founder's ownership is `APPROVED` and concentration is the corpus's largest institutional risk** | **[S: Const. Art.3]**; **[S-A: A-12]**; **[S-ST: F-11]** *"**the institution's legal purpose, brand, reserves and social-impact fund are all downstream of one person's ownership of the parent**"*; *"if that person **sells Krytos**, … nothing in the architecture prevents redirection of the institution"* | Does separation **dilute** concentration (as federation is said to) or **concentrate** it further in whoever holds the licensor? **[S-ST: §5 Scenario D]**: federation *"dilutes single-owner control … **reduces founder flexibility — a deliberate trade**"* |

**[P] Rule G-26 (A Separation That Breaks An `APPROVED` Constraint Is Not A Design, It Is An Amendment).** If any candidate multi-entity architecture cannot satisfy constraints 1–10, the correct output is **not** a quiet adjustment. It is either a different architecture, or **a reserved matter referred to the apex under [S-A: A-14 §13.3] and [S-D: Rule D-113]**, or an amendment approved under **[S: Const. Art.18]**. **This paper takes none of those routes.**

## VII.5 What Part VII does not do

**[A]** It does not: state how many entities are needed · name any entity · assign any pillar to any entity · price any structure · assess any tax outcome · determine whether separation is lawful, advisable or affordable · resolve **GC-01** · close **A-N12**, **A-N13**, **A-N15** or **UE-24** · or convert **[S-ST: R4]** from a `RECONSIDERATION` item into a decision.

**[P]** **The single output of Part VII is a schedule of questions.** **[S-ST: R4]** already states the decision owner: *"**Founder/CEO with corporate, tax and regulatory counsel [V]. This is the Phase 0 decision on which most others depend, and it should be made first.**"*

---

# PART VIII — WORKER / VOLUNTEER / CONTRACTOR CLASSIFICATION

## VIII.1 The unresolved decision, in the source's own words

**[S]** **[S: Ch.33]**: *"**Worker classification — `UNRESOLVED`** — Determine **employee/contractor/volunteer/stipend/business participant** categories — Yes; professional validation."*
**[S]** **[S: App. A §37]**: *"**Worker legal classification — Unresolved** — Determine employee/contractor/volunteer/stipend/business participant categories — Yes; professional validation."*
**[S]** **[S-D: LDR-U09]**: *"**Employee / contract labour / volunteer / stipend / business participant** — each carries **different statutory consequences** — Not stated — Founder/CEO with Legal and Finance — Yes — labour (A-V3), tax (A-V2) — **A-B1 BLOCKER**; B-06, B-09; [S-D: L-15]; [S-C: HV-1]; payroll — Does NOT block: **Volunteer-only activity with no payment at all** — Now → before the first payment — Consequence: **A member is paid in a way that creates an unrecorded statutory liability**."*
**[S]** **[S-ST: Annexure B Employment ◆]**: *"classification of members as **employee / contract labour / volunteer / stipend / business participant**; **minimum-wage and payment-cycle legality**; **statutory social-security and deduction obligations**; **appointment letters and contracts in member languages**; legality of the grievance and disciplinary procedure; **the volunteer-vs-worker boundary at each spectrum level (§9)**."*
**[S]** **[S-C: HD-C3]**: *"Determines whether **L3–L5 participation is volunteering, employment or contracting** — and therefore **whether §6 is lawful as designed**."*

**[A]** **Five categories are the source's own. This Part analyses ten role types because the brief requires ten and because the corpus contains role types that the five categories do not obviously name** — founder, officer, director/trustee/governing member, manager and paid specialist. **Mapping the ten role types onto the five source categories is itself a counsel question (WCQ-01) and is not performed here.**

**[P] Rule G-27 (No Role Is Classified Here).** No cell in this Part assigns a legal classification to any role. Every classification cell reads either a corpus statement or `UNRESOLVED` / `[V]` / `UNKNOWN`. **A classification assigned by an architecture document is the exact failure mode [S-F: LDR-U09] names: "a member is paid in a way that creates an unrecorded statutory liability."**

## VIII.3 The classification facts the corpus does establish

**[A]** Ten findings, each sourced. **None is a classification.**

| # | Finding | Source |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **The architecture already contains a status vocabulary that is not a legal vocabulary.** **[S: Ch.19 template as cited in [S-ST: §9.2]]** distinguishes *"volunteer, paid, mixed, stipend/contract/employee"* in the **vacancy record** | **[S-C: §6 preamble]** |
| **2** | **Levels 0–4 are designed to be legally thin, and L5 is the architecture's spine.** L0–L2 require *"no identity documents"* and are *"deletable on request"*; L3 requires *"identity verification + task-specific training"*; **L4 is *"the one pre-L5 level that can lawfully be paid, and it is paid as a supplier, not as a member"***; **L5** runs the full lifecycle *"application → screening → identity verification → interview → training → probation → role"* | **[S-C: §6.1–§6.6]** |
| **3** | **Volunteers carry the same obligations as paid members while receiving nothing.** *"§15 shows volunteers carry the **same** verification, training, conduct, safety, privacy and evidence obligations as paid members while receiving nothing"* | **[S-B: §34.1 row 5]** |
| **4** | **Model D (mixed, separable) is what the architecture describes**, and it requires that *"**the same person may hold both kinds of work, but the hours are recorded separately**"* | **[S-B: §16.1–§16.3]** |
| **5** | **Model B (paid employment for service) is not fundable at team level.** *"There is no revenue from which a general payroll could be paid"* — priority 1 attaches to **venture** receipts only | **[S-B: §16.2, §8.4]** |
| **6** | **Some roles may never be unpaid.** **[S-B: §34.2 D-8]** and **E-10**: *"**The four segregated finance hands and the safety role carry legal exposure** — Name the roles; fund them explicitly"*; **E-06**: *"Is governance work volunteer or compensated, and from what source? **`UNRESOLVED`**"* | **[S-B: §43.2]** |
| **7** | **Three roles the corpus insists are paid work:** trainers (**Rule D-47**), mentors (**L-12**), and certified supervisors (*"externally priced … cannot be volunteered away"*). And one it insists is not: **service to a beneficiary** — *"reward for **work performed** is compensation; reward for **service rendered to a beneficiary** is prohibited"* | **[S-D: C-D-19]** |
| **8** | **Role-attached political restrictions are an employment-law question that cannot be asked until classification is known.** *"Whether restrictions attached to organisational **roles** (A-02) are lawful in the relevant employment/volunteer classification — **Employment-law question; classification itself is UNRESOLVED**"* | **[S-A: §3.1 A.3 row 8; A-V3]** |
| **9** | **Liability is indeterminate because classification is indeterminate.** *"What is the organisation's potential liability for a member's acts — **vicarious, principal, or otherwise** — and how does that change with member classification? … **Classification is UNRESOLVED, so liability is currently indeterminate**"* | **[S-A: A-10 L7]** |
| **10** | **The payment cadence, the accounting basis and the scarcity rule are all classification-dependent.** `E-07` receipts-vs-invoiced *"determines whether workers are paid in the month they worked"*; `E-09` cadence below `B_healthy`; **B-06** pro-rata — *"lawfulness of pro-rata partial payment"*; **B-05** — *"whether accrued unpaid compensation creates a **wage debt**"* | **[S-B: §43.2, EV-3]** |

| **WCQ-20** | Who is the **employer or principal** for a person working simultaneously for a Mohalla team, a City shared-service function and (in a separated structure) two entities? | **[S-B: §26.4, B-21]**; **HY-02 precondition 8** |

| **WCQ-22** | Which roles **may never be unpaid**, and does naming them create an obligation to fund them before the pilot? | **[S-B: E-10, §34.2 D-8]**; **[S-B: E-06]** |
| **WCQ-23** | Is a **"business participant"** — the source's fifth category — a recognisable relationship at all, and what would it mean for a member who shares in venture surplus? | **[S: Ch.33]**; **GC-01** (priority 8 upside) |
| **WCQ-24** | Does a **stipend** differ from a wage for these purposes, and may it be paid from priority 1 or only from priority 3? | **[S: Ch.33]**; **[S: Ch.18]** |

**[P] Rule G-28 (Classification Precedes Payment, Not The Reverse).** **[S-F: chain 5]**: *"Entity structure → worker classification (LDR-U09) → priority-1 payment … **priority 1 is the waterfall's first and non-suspendable obligation**."* **No payment may be made, and no recruitment conversation may imply one, before WCQ-01…WCQ-26 have written answers.** **[S-B: E-02]**: the compensation formula *"must exist in writing **before first recruitment**"*; **[S-B: §46.1]**: *"The first failure arrives in **week 3**, not month 3, and it is an **expectation failure** rather than a cash failure."*

# PART IX — BANKING AND MONEY CONTROL

## IX.1 The architecture the corpus already specifies — and the holder it does not name

**[S]** **[S: App. A §20.2]** specifies **six financial areas** with a purpose and controls for each. **[S: Ch.31]** makes *"Bank/account/ledger architecture"* a `Blocker` whose **dependency is "Entity/tax review."** **[A]** The table below reproduces the source and adds the one column the source cannot fill.

| Financial area | Source purpose *(verbatim)* | Source controls *(verbatim)* | **Legal holder** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Team accounts / ledgers** | *"Record team-level expenses, reimbursements and local funds"* | *"Approval limits, receipts, weekly reconciliation"* | **`UNKNOWN`.** **[S-B: §3.2]**: the team is *"**not a legal entity, not a cost centre with its own bank account unless the unresolved structure says so [V]**"*; **[S-B: EV-4]**: *"whether a Mohalla team can hold **any balance at all**"* |
| **Venture revenue accounts** | *"Separate customer revenue by approved venture"* | *"Invoice/order matching, delivery proof, waterfall allocation"* | **`UNRESOLVED`.** **[S-B: §3.2]**: *"whose **ownership and custody are unresolved** (v1.3-A N14, N15)"* |
| **Organisation account** | *"Receive approved organisation allocations and pay approved central costs"* | *"National finance oversight and audit"* | **`UNKNOWN`.** The recipient of priority 5 has no legal existence (**UE-01**) |
| **Reserve funds** | *"Continuity, emergencies and wage-protection buffer"* | *"**National Funds Manager manages; release requires approval matrix**"* | **`UNRESOLVED` (A-N15).** *"Restricted purpose survives in policy but **in no instrument**"* |
| **Social-impact funds** | *"Restricted mission programmes funded from surplus conditions"* | *"**Segregated ledger**, approved budgets, evidence and audit"* | **`UNRESOLVED` (A-N15)** |
| **Petty cash where allowed** | *"Small immediate expenses"* | *"Cap, receipts, **dual count**, timely replenishment/reconciliation"* | **`UNKNOWN`** — and *"where allowed"* is the source's own conditional |

**[S]** **[S: App. D SOP-09 Weekly Payment]** escalates on *"**Unreconciled revenue or legal uncertainty**"*; **SOP-10 Reserve Release** escalates on *"**Personal request, venture bailout, missing approvals**"* and is owned by **National Funds**; **SOP-08 Expense Approval** escalates on *"Self-approval, missing receipt, suspicious supplier."*

## IX.2 The seven money-control questions the brief requires — BK-01 … BK-07

| ID | Question | What the corpus fixes | **What is `UNKNOWN` / `[V]` / `UNRESOLVED`** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **BK-01** | **Who can open accounts?** | Nothing. No account exists. **[S: Ch.31]** makes the account architecture a `Blocker` dependent on *"Entity/tax review"* | **`UNKNOWN — REQUIRES BANKING AND CORPORATE COUNSEL`.** Opening requires a legal person, a constituent instrument, a register of officers (**[S-F: BLK-C-01]**) and a board or equivalent resolution |
| **BK-02** | **Who can operate accounts?** | **[S: Ch.4]** financial authority: *"Funds managers within approval matrix — Recording, reconciliation, payment preparation and approved disbursements — **No single person should receive money, approve expenses, record transactions and audit them**"*; **[S: App. A §20.4]** Funds Manager **can** maintain the ledger, collect receipts, prepare the weekly payment sheet, flag anomalies, reject incomplete documentation, coordinate with reviewers; **cannot** self-approve, change percentages/priority order/multiplier, release reserves or social-impact funds without approvals, hide transactions or **maintain off-book cash**, or audit own work as final reviewer | **`UNRESOLVED` (A-N16).** Who the operators are, on whose mandate, in which entity. **[S-B: §40.2]** requires *"Two-signature money"* as a pilot control — **GC-04: the control is specified, the mandate is not** |
| **BK-03** | **Who can approve payments?** | **[S: App. E]** — Routine expense: *"Team Leader within limit"* with *"Funds Manager checks"*, escalate *"Over limit or unusual supplier"*; Reserve release: *"National approval matrix"* with *"National Funds + Finance"*, escalate on *"Emergency, large amount, wage protection"*; Social-impact programme: *"Social-impact approval tier"* with *"Finance + Governance"* | **Approval levels are set by *"risk, money, data sensitivity, safety and public impact"* — but no row names a legal signatory, because no legal person exists.** **[S: Ch.33]**: *"Reserve release rules — `NOT FINAL`"*; *"Partnership signatory authority — `Not final`"* |
| **BK-04** | **What continuity rules apply?** | **[S-A: A-14 §13.4]** `PROPOSED`: *"**Minimum of two authorised signatories with a defined continuity rule so that worker compensation can be paid during an apex vacancy** — the priority-1 obligation must not depend on one person's availability"* **[V] Banking and entity law**; **reserve release during vacancy** requires *"the normal approval matrix **plus one additional independent confirmer**"*; **credential dual custody**; **sealed emergency-access procedure** openable *"only on a recorded trigger (S2, S4, S5)"* | **`UNRESOLVED` (A-N16).** The **number** two is a proposal; the **mandate** does not exist. **[S-A: §22 N16]** consequence, verbatim: *"**Priority 1 fails on one person's absence. The waterfall's first obligation becomes unpayable**"* |
| **BK-05** | **What dual-control requirements apply?** | **[S: App. A §20.3]** — four separate hands (receive / approve / record / review-audit), combinable temporarily **only** with compensating controls: *"lower limits, dual acknowledgement, city/state review, receipts and bank proof, random audit, prompt role split as volume grows"*; **[S-D: Rule D-95 item 2]** *"Dual custody of credentials and **two signatories at every level**"*; **[S-D: Rule D-103]** segregation *"resolved by routing, never by weakening"*; **[S-D: C-D-06]** the four hands may be **routed** to City level where a team has fewer than four people | `PV` — whether a routed control satisfies any banking, statutory-audit or donor requirement is a **V-03 / V-10 / V-13** question |
| **BK-06** | **How are personal and organisational money separated?** | **[S: App. A §20.4]** prohibits *"**off-book cash**"*; **[S-B: HC-03]** records the failure mode — an emergency cost *"met from a member's pocket … which creates debt inside the team"*; **[S-D: Rule D-92 limb (iv)]** records *"directed expenditure outside the waterfall"* in the Founder Recusal Register; **[S-B: §21.2]** prohibits every fund from purposes it marks ❌, *"enforced as **serious misconduct** [S: SOP-18] regardless of outcome"* | **`UNKNOWN`.** Whether the founder's or Krytos's money may be advanced to a future entity, and on what terms, is **[S-B: E-03]** (*"founder capital / Krytos / validated donation / accrued-and-waived fee"* — `UNRESOLVED`, `[V]` tax, entity, donor restriction) and **[S-B: EV-4]** (*"Authority to advance funds"*) |
| **BK-07** | **Local versus national control, and the audit trail** | **Local:** team accounts/ledgers with *"approval limits, receipts, weekly reconciliation"*; **City:** *"finance reconciliation and payment preparation"* **[S-B: §32.4]**; **National:** *"platform, standards, **reserve custody**, audit capability, legal and compliance, insurance, entity and tax"* **[S-B: §32.4]**; **Audit trail:** **[S: Ch.19 step 13]** *"Payment sheet, **formula version**, approvals and evidence retained"*; **[S: Ch.27]** append-only records; **[S-B: B-15(6)]** publication of reserve and float utilisation; **[S-E: TS-3]** published accounts | **`UNRESOLVED`.** Reserve custody is National **in policy** and `UNRESOLVED` **in instrument** (**A-N15**). **[S-F: chain 24]**: *"accounts cannot be published from an **unaudited, unsegregated, unmandated ledger**"* |

| Segregation test, random reconciliation, expiry test on delegations | **[S-F: Part XI]** audit-method column | Paper-based; no entity needed to **perform**, but an entity is needed to be **answerable** |

## IX.6 What Part IX does not create

**[A]** No bank · no account · no mandate · no signatory set · no approval limit · no rupee threshold · no float amount · no reserve target · no accounting policy · no chart of accounts · no payment rail · no continuity rule. **[S-F: Rule F-12]** applies with full force: *"An Unfunded Blocker Is A Blocked Blocker"* — and **[S-F: BLK-D]** lists fourteen funding requirements of which **not one has an amount.**

**[P] Rule G-5 restated.** **Nothing in this document establishes, proposes as a decision, or implies a bank mandate, a signatory set, a continuity rule, an approval limit or an account.** **A-N16 remains `UNRESOLVED` and A-B9 remains a BLOCKER until an authorised human decision-maker, advised by banking and entity professionals under A-V10 / V-10, closes it — and until the closure is recorded as evidence in the form [S-F: SR-03] requires: *"the mandate document."***

**[A]** **GC-04 is preserved alongside the block:** the corpus proposes *two* signatories (**[S-A: A-14 §13.4]**), states *two signatories at every level* as a structural requirement (**[S-D: Rule D-95]**), requires *"two-signature money"* as a pilot control (**[S-B: §40.2]**) and makes *"≥2 signatories"* a gate PASS condition (**[S-F: P0-C]**) — **while A-N16 records the underlying decision as `UNRESOLVED`.** **[S-F: Rule F-9]** governs: *"A `PROPOSED` item is not a decision … a proposal that has not been approved has no operative force."* **The number is a proposal. The mandate is missing.**

# PART X — FOUNDER, CEO AND NATIONAL COUNCIL UNDER EACH ENTITY OPTION

## X.1 The authority baseline, as the corpus fixes it

**[S]** Before any option is examined, what the source already establishes — and what it expressly leaves open:

| Element | Source text | Status |
|---|---|---|
| **Ownership** | *"The organisation is **owned by Krytos under founder/CEO Vasu, subject to the final legal structure**. The Council does not own the organisation over the CEO unless a future legal charter explicitly changes that"* — **[S: Const. Art.3]** | `APPROVED ARCHITECTURE` — **and expressly conditional** |
| **Ownership content** | *"Property, **brand**, strategy and **ultimate founder-level control** — Subject to law, contracts and approved governance"* — **[S: Ch.4 authority table]** | `APPROVED` |
| **Executive authority** | *"CEO and delegated executives — Direct operations, strategy, appointments and emergency intervention — **Cannot bypass law, finance, safeguarding or audit**"* — **[S: Ch.4]** | `APPROVED` |
| **Founder flexibility** | *"KRYTOS / Founder-CEO — **Ownership and broad executive flexibility within final legal structure.** CEO may work operationally"* — **[S: Ch.4]** | `APPROVED` — **conditioned on the final structure** |
| **Governance authority** | *"Proposed Council — Standards, ethics, audit and dispute review — **Does not own above CEO unless charter changes it**"* — **[S: Ch.4]** | `PROPOSED / REQUIRES FINAL APPROVAL` |
| **CEO vs Council** | *"**UNRESOLVED** — CEO retains ownership/executive authority; Council provides delegated standards/audit/dispute oversight"* — **[S: Ch.33]** | **`UNRESOLVED` (LDR-U01)** |
| **Council as a body** | *"**National Council — [U] — Not created here — UNRESOLVED, carried to Part 45**"* — **[S-D: Rule D-40 table]** | **Not created** |
| **The proposed model** | *"**The source leaves the final relationship between CEO, National Council and National Team unresolved.**"* Founder/CEO **cannot approve alone**: *"Illegal activity, unsafe protection action, hidden financial transfers, unilateral audit suppression, **final legal/tax conclusions without professionals**."* Council **cannot approve alone**: *"**Ownership transfer**, override of CEO outside charter, direct operational micromanagement, spending outside approved budget."* National Team **cannot**: *"Change constitutional principles, **finalise legal structure**, bypass Council audit, alter pay policy without approval"* — **[S: App. A §3.5]** | `PROPOSED` |
| **Reserved matters** | Seven items, incl. *"change of mission, name, brand or **entity structure**"*; *"**appointment of a permanent CEO**"*; *"changes to the **priority order** of the financial waterfall"* — **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]** | `PROPOSED`; **holder undetermined** |
| **The gap** | *"**Who holds the reserved matters in practice — Depends on the two rows above — `UNRESOLVED` — the single largest governance gap in the architecture** … A reserved matter that nobody can decide is **not protected; it is frozen**"* — **[S-D: Part 34 §34.1]** | **`UNRESOLVED`** |
| **The interim** | **Rule D-113** — five limbs; *"**This rule does not resolve the UNRESOLVED item.** It prevents the unresolved item from becoming an emergency in which someone resolves it unilaterally"*; **[S-D: LV-9]**: *"Whether a two-body provisional decision is valid **depends on the entity structure**"* | `PROPOSED` |
| **The concentration** | *"The architecture … places the first two [ownership and executive], plus brand, strategy, mission amendment and (by default) IP and data, **in the same pair of hands**"* — **[S-A: §11.4]**; *"**the institution's identity and its legal continuity are the same object, held by one person**"* — **[S-ST: §11.1]** | `ANALYSIS ONLY` |
| **The only check** | *"Visibility is the only check available where removal is not"* — **[S-D: Rule D-92]**; **Rule D-93** the founder may not assess; **Rule D-94** the four test counts are published internally | `PROPOSED` |

**[P] Rule G-29 (This Part Asks Who Decides, Not Who Should).** Every row below ends in the same place: **`UNKNOWN — REQUIRES GOVERNANCE COUNSEL`**, or a source quotation. **No governance authority is created, assigned, confirmed or denied by this instrument.**

## X.2 The fourteen questions — GVQ-01 … GVQ-14

**Structure families used as columns:**
**NFP** = OPT-A / OPT-B / OPT-C (Section 8 company · society · trust) · **CO** = OPT-D (company / business entity) · **UNINC** = OPT-I variant "internal Krytos division" and OPT-J J-1 "unincorporated network" · **MULTI** = OPT-H / OPT-I / HY-04 / HY-05 · **E/F/G** = OPT-E / OPT-F / OPT-G (LLP · partnership · proprietorship)

**Cell legend.** Every cell in the five option-family columns that reads **`UNKNOWN`** is shorthand, for legibility, for the standing entry required by this instrument:

> **`UNKNOWN — REQUIRES GOVERNANCE COUNSEL`**

**No cell in this Part contains an answer.** Where a cell carries additional text after `UNKNOWN`, that text is the reason the answer is unavailable or the specific question counsel must resolve — never a partial answer. Rule G-29 governs the whole Part.

| # | Question | **What the corpus fixes** | NFP | CO | UNINC | MULTI | E/F/G | **Governance-counsel question** |

## X.3 What the fourteen questions establish

**[A]** **All fourteen questions return `UNKNOWN — REQUIRES GOVERNANCE COUNSEL` for all five structure families.** That uniformity is the finding, and it has three causes, each sourced:

| Cause | Source |
|---|---|
| **1. The apex authority is itself unresolved, so no body can be identified as the decider under any form** | **LDR-U01**; **[S-D: C-D-08]** *"TRUE CONTRADICTION — UNRESOLVED IN SOURCE. Not resolved. Explicitly refused."*; **[S-F: Rule F-10]** |
| **2. The corpus deliberately specifies machines without outputs** | **[S-A: A-14 §13.5]**: *"The pack specifies the **machine**, not the output … Deliberately absent: any named individual, any seniority rule, any family provision, any founder prerogative to appoint a successor unilaterally."* **[S-A: A-13 §12.6]**: the trade-off *"is a decision, not an analysis"* |
| **3. Every answer is a function of a constituent instrument that does not exist** | **[S-D: LV-8]** *"Determines **who may hold reserved matters, board composition**, and 12A/80G/FCRA applicability"*; **[S-A: A-V9]** *"CEO versus Council authority; reserved matters; dispute resolution; **removal mechanisms; Krytos governing documents**"* |

**[P] Rule G-30 (GVQ-01 … GVQ-14 Are A Single Engagement, Not Fourteen).** **[S-F: XXV.4 Action 1]** requires that corporate, tax and labour counsel be commissioned **"as one engagement."** The fourteen governance questions belong to that engagement, addressed to **V-10 (governance / company secretary — A-V9)** in conjunction with **V-01 (corporate — A-V1)**, and they cannot be answered before **UE-01** and **UE-24** are supplied.

**[P] Rule G-31 (The Concentration Is Recorded, Not Remedied).** **[S-A: A-12]** is `ANALYSIS ONLY` and *"takes no decision, resolves nothing, and closes nothing."* This Part inherits that posture. **The corpus's own remedy is structural — [S-D: Rule D-95]: "Independence from any single person is achieved by structure, not by intention" — and the structure is the subject of this paper, not its output.**

# PART XI — ENTITY SUCCESSION STRESS TEST

## XI.1 Method

**[P]** Eleven scenarios, all drawn from the corpus — **eight from [S-A: A-14 §13.2]** and **three from [S-ST: F-11], [S-D: OQ-19] and [S-D: Part 42 Scenario D]** — tested against seven continuity dimensions. **Scenarios are not forecasts** (**[S-D: Part 42]** standing instruction). **No scenario is assigned a probability, and no numeric duration is invented.**

| Dimension | What continuity means here | Corpus anchor |
|---|---|---|
| **ASSET** | Who controls property, funds, reserves, restricted funds, materials and inventory | **[S-A: §11.2]**; **[S: App. A §20.2]**; **A-N15** |
| **BANK** | Whether money can move — mandates, signatories, payment of priority 1 | **A-N16**; **[S-A: A-14 §13.4]**; **[S-D: Scenario B]** *"two signatories keep PAY alive"* |
| **IP** | Who controls the name, mark, architecture, SOPs, curricula, software, data models, domain and accounts | **A-N12, A-N13**; **[S-E: N-E-19, N-E-20, Rule E-188]** |
| **OPERATIONAL** | Whether service, protection and venture delivery continue | **[S-A: A-14 §13.6]** continuity-during-dispute rule; **[S-D: INV-2]** |
| **LEADERSHIP** | Whether an authorised decision-maker exists at every required level | **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]** Interim Authority Order; **[S-D: SS states, TL-2/TL-3]** |
| **LEGAL** | Whether the organisation can still sue, be sued, contract, file, notify, hold a licence and answer a regulator | **[S: Const. Art.19]**; **[S-A: A-14 §13.4]** statutory registers; **[S-D: LV-10]** |
| **MEMBER** | Whether members, beneficiaries, partners and the public still know who they are dealing with | **[S-E: Rule E-59, SIM-11]**; **[S-A: §11.4]** *"what happens if Vasu is not there?"* |

**Cell key:** **`[S] designed`** = the corpus supplies an instrument · **`[P] proposed`** = a proposal exists, not adopted · **`U`** = `UNRESOLVED` in the corpus · **`⛔`** = fails, or has no instrument at all · **`[V]`** = entity-form dependent, validation required

## XI.2 The stress test — SUC-01 … SUC-11

| ID | Scenario | ASSET | BANK | IP | OPERATIONAL | LEADERSHIP | LEGAL | MEMBER |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **SUC-01** | **Founder temporarily absent** (days–weeks) — **[S-A: A-14 S1]** | `[P]` — reserved matters prohibit *"transfer or encumbrance of assets, reserves or the social-impact fund"* | ⛔ **A-N16 `UNRESOLVED`** — no mandate exists to test | `[P]` — dual custody proposed | `[S] designed` — *"Routine executive decisions; urgent safety, finance and legal matters"* under a pre-designated **Acting CEO** | `[P]` — Interim Authority Order **not adopted** | `[V]` — who may sign during an absence depends on the form | `[P]` — spokespersons by domain **[S-E: Rule E-104/105]** |
| **SUC-02** | **Founder long-term incapacitated** (months) — **[S-A: A-14 S2]** | `[P]` — as S1, plus *"no mission or structure change"* | ⛔ **A-N16**; reserve release would need *"one additional independent confirmer"* `[P]` | `[P]` | `[S] designed` — continuity decisions: *"contracts, signatories, staffing at National level"* | `[P]` — Acting CEO **plus the proposed Council exercising reserved-matter oversight** — *"a proposal **contingent on** LDR-U01"* | `[V]` — **who certifies incapacity** is *"a determination by the body defined in the instrument **[V]**"* — **no instrument exists** | ⛔ — nobody is authorised to say what has happened |
| **SUC-03** | **Founder resigns** — **[S-A: A-14 S3]** | `[P]` | ⛔ | `[P]` | `[S] designed` — *"Full continuity scope; preparation of the selection process"* | `[P]` — selection process **§13.5** begins immediately; **the machine exists, the output does not** | `[V]` — a resignation's legal effect depends on the office held, which depends on the form | ⛔ |
| **SUC-04** | **Founder removed** — **[S-A: A-14 S4]** | ⛔ — no removal instrument, so no asset-transfer authority | ⛔ | ⛔ | `[P]` — *"Continuity only"* | ⛔ **"Only by the mechanism defined in the governing instrument [V] — this pack does not create one"** | ⛔ **No removal mechanism exists in any form** — **[S-A: A-V9]** *"removal mechanisms"* is an open counsel question | ⛔ |

| **SUC-07** | **Sustained loss of trust in the founder** — **[S-A: A-14 S7]** | ⛔ | ⛔ | ⛔ | `[P]` — *"Continuity"* | ⛔ **"This is the hardest scenario and the pack does not resolve it. It requires the instrument defined in S4 plus an independent review route"** | ⛔ | ⛔ — **[S-D: Part 27.2]**: *"every mechanism … that depends on being applied to the most powerful person **will not be applied** … unless the architecture compels it"* |
| **SUC-08** | **Organisational crisis** — fraud, incident, breach, public controversy, financial collapse — **[S-A: A-14 S8]** | `[P]` — crisis group scope limited to *"preserve life, safety, legal compliance, evidence, **funds** and continuity"* | ⛔ — a crisis is precisely when a mandate gap is fatal | `[P]` — *"preserve … the **brand**"* | `[S] designed` — *"Only decisions necessary to preserve life, safety, legal compliance, evidence, funds and continuity"*; prohibited: constitutional change, Tier-1 change, restructuring, permanent leadership change | `[P]` — *"a small named crisis group with defined scope"* per **[S: Ch.5 escalation triggers]** | ⛔ — **[S-E: CC-1…CC-15]** require a legal entity to answer a regulator, an insurer and a court; **SIM-08**: *"Where the entity structure is unresolved, **the accounts cannot be as clear as the accusation is simple**"* | ⛔ — **[S-E: SIM-11]** *"Nobody knows who may speak"* |
| **SUC-09** | **Krytos is sold, transferred, encumbered or loses control** — **[S-ST: F-11]**: *"If that person dies, is incapacitated, is discredited, changes their mind, **sells Krytos**, or is pressured, **nothing in the architecture prevents redirection of the institution**"* | ⛔ — *"the institution's **legal purpose, brand, reserves and social-impact fund are all downstream of one person's ownership of the parent**"* | ⛔ | ⛔ — *"a **licensor could withdraw it**"* | ⛔ | ⛔ | ⛔ — **no golden-share or protective-vote mechanism exists** **[S-ST: F-11 item 2]** | ⛔ — worst case, verbatim: *"**A national institution with lakhs of members and real community trust is legally repurposed or dissolved by a decision no member can contest**"* |
| **SUC-10** | **Apex contested — two people claim authority** — **[S-D: Part 42 Scenario G]**; **[S-D: OQ-19]** *"Who holds the reserved matters if the apex is contested?"* | ⛔ — reserved matters frozen | ⛔ — two claimants, one mandate | ⛔ | `[S] designed` — **continuity-during-dispute rule**: *"operations continue under the existing delegated authority and **no faction may interrupt service, payment, safety response or record-keeping**"* — *"the single most important line in this chapter"* | `[P]` — *"Resolved by record, quickly, **if the record is current**"*; where both claim a delegation, *"the record may genuinely support both"* | ⛔ — **A-N19** ladder is `PROPOSED` and its decider is `AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED` | ⛔ |
| **SUC-11** | **Whole City team lost** — **[S-D: Part 42 Scenario D]**: *"the mohallas were **routed to the City for segregation of duties** (Rule D-103), so losing the City loses the control. **This is the architecture's largest single-point exposure below the apex**"* | `[P]` — City-held materials and stock | ⛔ — **the four finance hands are routed to City level** **[S-D: C-D-06, Rule D-33]**; losing the City loses the routed control | `[P]` | `[P]` — *"City-level TL-2; State cover"* — but *"State teams can 'temporarily support or supervise a struggling City Team' — **but only one at a time in practice**"* **[S-B: §21.3]** | `[P]` | `[V]` | ⛔ — `K` mohallas lose verification, funds review and assessment simultaneously |

## XI.3 The stress-test result

**[C]** Counting the 77 cells above:

| Cell class | Count | Share of 77 |
|---|---|---|
| **⛔** — fails, or has no instrument at all | **39** | 50.6% |
| **`[P] proposed`** — a proposal exists but is not adopted (**[S-F: Rule F-9]**: no operative force) | **27** | 35.1% |
| **`[S] designed`** — an approved or source-designed instrument exists | **6** | 7.8% |
| **`[V]`** — entity-form dependent, validation required | **5** | 6.5% |
| **`U`** — explicitly `UNRESOLVED` in the corpus | **0** | — |

**By dimension:**

| Dimension | ⛔ | `[P]` | `[S] designed` | `[V]` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **ASSET** | 5 | 6 | 0 | 0 |
| **BANK** | **10** | 1 | 0 | 0 |
| **IP** | 5 | 6 | 0 | 0 |
| **OPERATIONAL** | 1 | 4 | **6** | 0 |
| **LEADERSHIP** | 3 | 8 | 0 | 0 |
| **LEGAL** | 6 | **0** | **0** | 5 |
| **MEMBER** | **9** | 2 | 0 | 0 |

**[A] Four findings:**

1. **Half the continuity surface has no instrument at all.** The 39 ⛔ cells are concentrated in **BANK** (⛔ in **ten of eleven** scenarios — **A-N16**), **MEMBER** (⛔ in nine), **LEGAL** (⛔ in six, and `[V]` in the other five), and **ASSET** and **IP** (⛔ in five each). **BANK fails almost universally because a mandate cannot exist without a legal person — and the one scenario in which it does not fail outright, SUC-06, fails by proposal only.**
2. **The scenarios that fail worst are the ones the corpus says it cannot design for.** **SUC-04 removal**, **SUC-07 loss of trust** and **SUC-09 Krytos control transfer** are ⛔ across **all seven** dimensions. **[S-A: A-14]** states plainly that S4 depends on *"the mechanism defined in the governing instrument [V] — this pack does not create one"* and that S7 *"the pack does not resolve."* **SUC-09 is not among A-14's eight scenarios at all — it is [S-ST: F-11]'s, and it is the scenario in which the architecture has no instrument because the event happens *above* the architecture.**
3. **Operational continuity is the only strong dimension, and it is strong alone.** All six `[S] designed` cells are in **OPERATIONAL** — the continuity-during-dispute rule, the crisis-decision rule, the Acting CEO's scope, the emergency-decision list. **LEADERSHIP has none**: its eight `[P]` cells are proposals (the Interim Authority Order, the selection machine, the SS-state register) and its three ⛔ cells are SUC-04, SUC-07 and SUC-09. **[A]** The architecture keeps *work* going; it cannot keep *authority, money, property, legal personality or public identity* going.
4. **No scenario is survivable in the LEGAL dimension without an entity.** Every LEGAL cell is either `[V]` (five) or ⛔ (six) — **there is not one `[S] designed` or `[P]` cell in that column.** **[S-A: §11.4]** states the counterparty consequence: *"**authorities are asked to recognise the body**"* — and there is no body.

**[P] Rule G-32 (The Continuity Test Cannot Be Run Yet).** **[S-A: A-14 §13.8]** proposes an annual unannounced 72-hour exercise operating *"as though the CEO were unavailable, using only the Interim Authority Order, the dual-custody arrangements and the documented handover state."* **None of the three instruments exists.** **[S-D: Rule D-122]**: *"an unexercised control counts as absent."* **[S-F: BLK-F-09]** places the full-scope test in the LATER class but notes *"a tabletop version can run earlier"* — **and a tabletop version of SUC-01 through SUC-03 is the cheapest available evidence that this Part's ⛔ cells are real.**
