# PART XII — LIABILITY AND PROTECTION UNDER EACH STRUCTURE

## XII.1 The framing rule, inherited unchanged

**[S]** **[S-A: A-10 framing rule]**: *"this document **does not state what insurance the organisation has, needs, or can obtain.** It states what must be *established in writing, by a qualified professional, before protection activity is activated or publicised*. **The distinction matters: an architecture that assumes cover exists will act as though it does.**"*

**[P] Rule G-33 (No Liability Position Is Taken).** This Part maps **exposures** to **entity implications**. It does not state whether any exposure exists in law, whether any defence is available, whether any liability is limited, or whether any person would be liable. **Every row ends in a discipline, not a conclusion.**

## XII.2 Liability exposure map — LB-01 … LB-16

| ID | Exposure | What the corpus says | **Entity implication** | Discipline |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **LB-01** | **Member injury** — a member hurt while helping | **[S-A: A-10 L2]**: *"What **personal accident or medical-expense cover** can be provided for members acting in organisational capacity, at what cost per member, and what activities are excluded?"* — *"**A member injured while helping is the most morally urgent liability the organisation has**."* **L12**: what a member must be told in writing before taking a safety role — *"**A member who believes they are covered and is not has been misled by the organisation**"* | Cover is written on a legal person; **who the person is determines who is insured, who is notified and who is sued.** **[S-F: chain 7]**: *"cover is written on a legal person"* | V-06 broker ◆, V-09 counsel, V-05 |
| **LB-02** | **Third-party harm** — injury to a person being helped, or property damage | **[S-A: A-10 L3]**: *"What **public/third-party liability** cover is available for service activities, events, distributions, and emergency assistance — **including injury to a person being helped and damage to property**?"* — *"Third-party claims arise from **ordinary service**, not only from protection activity."* **§9.4**: any injury to any person is recorded as an incident; any third-party claim *"routed to National Legal the same day … **never answered substantively by a local leader**"*; **no local settlement, payment or admission** | **Vicarious or principal liability for a member's acts is indeterminate** — **L7**: *"Classification is UNRESOLVED, so **liability is currently indeterminate**."* The entity is the defendant or it is not, and today it cannot be | V-06, V-09, V-05 ◆ |
| **LB-03** | **Data breach** | **[X]** carried at **[S-A: Annexure C item 1]**: breach notification to the Board *"without delay"* with a detailed report **within 72 hours**; notification of affected data principals in plain language; **substantial financial penalties**; separate **CERT-In incident reporting within 6 hours**. **[S-F: III.3 item 8]** consequence if skipped: *"**[X] DPDP penalties up to ₹200 crore for failure to notify, ~₹250 crore for inadequate safeguards [V]**."* **SOP-22** Data Breach is one of the nine **existence-level** SOPs **[S-B: §25.3]** | **Duties attach to a fiduciary. Without one there is no notifier** — **[S-F: chain 6]**. **[S-A: §11.2]**: *"the legal data fiduciary is **presumably** the entity, i.e. ultimately Krytos."* **A-N14** requires assignment *"**per entity and per data category**"* | V-07 ◆, V-12 |
| **LB-04** | **Contract failure** — defective delivery, non-payment, warranty | **[S: App. A §13.4]** every engagement defines *"scope, price … acceptance criteria, revision limits, data access, IP ownership, confidentiality, payment terms and **complaint route**"*; **[S-B: EV-6]** *"contract enforceability, interest and consumer law; **exclusion of liability for venture work**"*; **[S-E: LV-E-24]** consumer protection and service warranties | **Capacity to contract is the entity.** **[S: Ch.31]**: contract templates depend on *"Entity structure"* and are `Not started` / `Blocker` | V-01, V-06, V-17 consumer |
| **LB-05** | **Financial loss** — to a member, a customer, a donor or the organisation | **[S-B: B-05]** accrued unpaid compensation; **EV-3** *"whether accrued unpaid compensation creates a **wage debt**"*; **[S-B: HC-03]** a shock cost *"met from a member's pocket … **creates debt inside the team**"*; **[S-E: CC-2]** financial allegation requires *"the accounts, the specific transactions, the audit position"* | **Who owes, who is owed, and against whose assets.** **[S-E: SIM-08]**: *"Where the entity structure is unresolved, **the accounts cannot be as clear as the accusation is simple**"* | V-03, V-05, V-06, V-13 |
| **LB-06** | **Volunteer action** — an act by an unpaid member | **[S-A: A-10 L7]** vicarious/principal liability *"**changes with member classification** (volunteer / stipend / contract / employee)"*; **[S-A: §3.1 A.3 row 7]**: *"Whether a member's personal political activity can, in any circumstances, be **attributed to the organisation legally (agency, vicarious liability, common intent)** — Determines how strict A-02 must be"*; **[S-B: §15.4]** volunteers carry the **same** obligations as paid members | **Classification determines attribution. Classification is `UNRESOLVED` (UE-02).** A volunteer acting in organisational capacity with no entity behind them is the exposure the corpus rates most urgent | V-05 ◆, V-09, V-06 |
| **LB-07** | **Protection incident** | **[S: Ch.16]** nine-step sequence and six DO-NOTs `APPROVED`; **[S-A: A-08]** DO-NOT list to be entrenched Tier-1; **[S-A: A-10 L8]**: *"What is the **legal boundary of the intervention step**? What does applicable criminal procedure permit a private person to do, and **what are the consequences of exceeding it**?"* **[X]** BNSS 2023 **s.40** private-person arrest with a **six-hour handover**, **s.31** duty of the public to assist a Magistrate or police officer reasonably demanding aid — *"cited **only** to establish that the protection boundary requires legal review. **No operational instruction is derived from it**."* **L9** Good-Samaritan and first-aid protections; **L10** mandatory reporting duties; **L15**: *"Does any activity contemplated … create liability that protocol should simply **prohibit**? — **The cheapest risk control is the activity not performed**"* | **[S-ST: R4]** classifies protection as *"**liability-heavy, insurance-dependent**"* — a **third legal nature** distinct from both charitable and commercial. **[S-F: chain 14]**: the Activation Gate *"cannot pass without written liability answers."* **HY-02 note: the corpus never states where the protection pillar belongs in a separated structure** | V-09 ◆, V-06, V-08, V-01 |
| **LB-08** | **Leadership decision** — a wrongful appointment, removal, assessment or escalation | **[S-D: LV-4]** improvement plans and removal — *"procedural fairness requirements; **sham-plan risk**"*; **LV-5** reversed burden of proof; **LV-3** demotion; **[S-D: Scenario K]**: *"**A correct act beyond authority has no clean path** … the architecture has no instrument for '**retrospective authorisation**' — and creating one would create a loophole"*; **[S-D: LV-22]** insurance *"for leaders acting under TL-3"* | **An officer's or governing member's personal exposure** — **L6**: *"cover … for **officers, governors and the founder personally**, given that the Council structure is not yet constituted"* — *"**Personal exposure deters capable people from governance roles**"* | V-05, V-10, V-06 |
| **LB-09** | **Misrepresentation** — an over-claim, or a claim made without a legal person behind it | **[S-E: Rule E-8]** *"a statement that cannot be traced to a record may not be published however true it is"*; **[S-E: N-E-24]** the entity structure *"determines **what may lawfully be said about status**"*; **[S-E: MC-8]** *"Volunteer WhatsApp club"* — response *"Publish the entity status accurately, **including what is pending**"*; **[S-ST: §18.3 item 4]** *"Under-claiming is a trust strategy; **over-claiming is a liability**"*; **[S-A: A-V11]** communications/advertising counsel on *"representations and the expectations they create"*; **[S-A: A-07]** the Public Protection Statement may not be published before **L2, L3, L4, L8, L12** are answered | **Every public statement about status is a representation by somebody.** Today the honest statement is that no entity exists — which **[S-E: Rule E-59]** requires be given on request, and which **[S-E: §20.4 L4]** says institutional counterparties will test | V-16 ◆, V-01, V-06 |
| **LB-10** | **Safeguarding failure** — harm to a child or vulnerable adult | **[S-A: §23.2 A-B6]** BLOCKER — *"**Harm to a child is the highest-consequence event available to this organisation**; [S: Ch.12] already warns that internal training is not automatically sufficient"*; **[S-C: HV-2]** minimum ages, permissible hours, prohibited work, guardian consent and revocability, the two-adult rule, certified supervision, online safety, reporting duties; **[S-A: A-10 L10]** mandatory reporting duties — *"who must report, to whom, within what time"*; **[S-D: LV-17]** *"statutory obligations; **designated-role requirements**"* | **"Designated-role requirements" implies a designating person.** **[S-C: HV-6]** asks whether an unincorporated network may *"accept guardian consent."* Certification, protocols and consent forms must be held by somebody answerable | V-08 ◆, V-09, V-01 |
| **LB-11** | **Funder dispute** — a donor, grant-maker, CSR body or sponsor | **[S: App. D SOP-07]** escalate on *"**Restricted/illegal/conditional donation**"*; **[S-ST: F-32]** *"Mission capture by funders; … **regulatory exposure if foreign contribution is received without FCRA registration or prior permission** [X]"*; **[S-C: HV-9]** *"donor intent, restricted gifts, **sponsorship conditions**"*; **[S-E: Rule E-17]** money buying influence; **[S-B: B-24]** *"**donor intent must be honoured**"* | **A donor gives to a person.** **[S-B: §33.4]**: *"Without B-24, a donor **cannot fund service even if they wish to**, because there is no instrument to receive the gift."* Dispute resolution, restriction enforcement and refund all presuppose a counterparty | V-04, V-01, V-06, V-17 |
| **LB-12** | **Dissolution** — and the liabilities that survive it | **[S: Const. Art.19]** `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`; **a reserved matter** **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]**; **[S-A: A-N12]** risk: *"**exit and dissolution become chaotic**"*; **[S-E: N-E-17]**: *"**Any choice has succession and dissolution consequences**"* | **Who is left to answer a claim after the person is gone, and out of what.** Part XVIII | V-01 ◆, V-03, V-04, V-10 |
| **LB-13** | **Political / electoral exposure** | **[S-A: §3.1 A.3 rows 5, 6]**: whether election-period activity attracts *"any notification, permission, expenditure, or **model-code-type obligation**"*; whether distribution of material near an election *"could be treated as an **electoral offence, an inducement, or expenditure by a third party**"* — *"**Highest-consequence operational question in this code**"* | **[X]** exemption may be conditional on absence of political activity, *"with cancellation risk"* **[S-D: §50.5]**. **The entity is the thing at risk** | V-17 electoral ◆, V-04 |
| **LB-14** | **Regulatory / permission exposure** | **[S-A: A-10 L11]** permissions, notifications or registrations for food distribution, health camps, ambulance-like transport, disaster response, animal handling, use of public space, collection of donations or materials; **[S-ST: Annexure B]** *"no diagnosis/treatment without licence"*; **[S-B: NL-5]** obligations *"attach at thresholds, not smoothly"* | **Permissions are granted to persons.** An unpermitted activity performed by an unincorporated network has no permit-holder | V-02 ◆ |
| **LB-15** | **Cross-entity / related-party exposure in a separated structure** | **[S-ST: R4 new risk]**: *"administrative burden, **related-party scrutiny**, and a possible public perception of 'charity and business being mixed' whichever way it is done; **donor confusion**"*; **[S-ST: Annexure B]** *"**related-party and transfer-pricing discipline** if service and business are separated"* | **Separation creates a new liability class that a single entity does not have.** It is the price of HY-02/HY-03 and must be weighed, not assumed away | V-04 ◆, V-03, V-01 |
| **LB-16** | **Founder personal exposure** | **[S-A: A-10 L6]** officers, governors and **the founder personally**; **[S-ST: F-11 item 4]**: *"**Institutional investors, partners, government counterparts and serious professionals decline to engage (they do due diligence on continuity)**"*; **[S-ST: §11.1]**: *"if the public face is one person, **every controversy about that person is a controversy about the institution**"* | **In some forms the founder's personal exposure increases; in others it is separated from the institution's. Which, is a counsel question per form — and it is the question the founder has the strongest personal interest in asking** | V-01, V-06, V-10 ◆ |

## XII.3 The protection pillar under each option family

**[A]** The corpus gives three statements that bear on this directly, and no more. **Everything else is `UNKNOWN FROM CURRENT CORPUS`.**

| Option family | Corpus statement bearing on protection | Consequence recorded |
|---|---|---|
| **NFP (OPT-A/B/C)** | **[S-A: A-10 L1]** frames the insurance question as being about *"a **mass-membership voluntary organisation** performing community service and emergency first response in India"* — i.e. the corpus asks the question in a **voluntary-sector** frame. **[S-A: A-V4]** asks about *"**trustee liability**"* | **`PV`** — whether a charitable form may carry a protective function at all, and whether its objects may reach it (§V.2 item 9), are V-01 questions. **No statement permits or forbids it** |
| **CO (OPT-D)** | **[S: App. A §3.2 row 2]** Advantages: *"Cleaner contracts, accounts and **liability separation**"* | **`PV`** — "liability separation" is a source advantage claim, **not** a conclusion that protection liability is separated. Whether it is, and from whom, is a counsel question |
| **UNINC / internal division (OPT-I variant, OPT-J J-1)** | **[S: App. A §3.2 row 1]** Risks: *"**May mix charitable, commercial and protection risk with broader Krytos**"* | **`PR`** — the source's own risk statement, recorded at **FC-03** |
| **MULTI (OPT-H, HY-02…HY-05)** | **[S-ST: R4]**: protection is *"**liability-heavy, insurance-dependent**"* and has a **legal nature distinct from both service and business** | **`UNKNOWN FROM CURRENT CORPUS`** — **the corpus never states which entity in a separated structure holds the protection pillar.** This is recorded as a gap counsel must close, at §VII.2 HY-02 |
| **E / F / G** | None | **`UK`** — Rule G-19 |

**[P] Rule G-34 (No Protection Activity May Be Tested, Trained For Operationally, Publicised Or Represented Before A-B2 And A-B3 Close).** **[S-A: A-10 §9.2 rule 1]**: *"**No written confirmation, no activation.**"* **[S-F: XXV.7]**: *"Any protection or first-response activity — A-B2 insurance answers and A-B3 boundary opinion are preconditions. **An injury during a test is not a test result**."* **This rule is not relaxed by anything in this paper, and no entity option relaxes it.**

# PART XIII — INSURANCE POSITION UNDER EACH STRUCTURE

## XIII.1 The framing rule, restated

**[P] Rule G-35 (Questions For A Broker Or Counsel, Never A Coverage Statement).** Inherited from **[S-A: A-10 §9.2 rule 4]**: *"**Nothing in this framework may be represented to a member, partner, authority or the public as insurance cover unless it is confirmed in writing by the insurer.**"*

**No row in this Part says `COVERAGE EXISTS`. No row says cover is available, obtainable, affordable or appropriate.** Every row is a **QUESTION FOR BROKER/COUNSEL** traced to a source ID.

**[S]** **[S-A: §22 N10]** status, verbatim: *"**UNRESOLVED — questions posed, not answered.**"* Risk: *"**Uncovered injury; unsupported member; unanswerable regulator.**"* Action: *"Commission written answers; **hold as governance evidence**."* Window: *"Before protection activation."*

## XIII.2 Insurance categories as broker / counsel questions — INS-01 … INS-12

| ID | Category | **QUESTION FOR BROKER / COUNSEL** | Source ID | Entity dependence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **INS-01** | **Public / third-party liability** | *What public or third-party liability cover is available for service activities, events, distributions and emergency assistance — including injury to a person being helped and damage to property — and what are the standard exclusions?* | **[S-A: A-10 L3, L1]**; **[S-A: A-V4]**; **[S-ST: Annexure B Insurance ◆]** *"public/third-party liability for events and service work"* | The insured must be a person. **Limits, excess and exclusions may differ by form** — `[V]` |
| **INS-02** | **Professional liability / professional indemnity** | *What professional indemnity is required where members give advice — legal, tax, medical-adjacent, technical, engineering, safeguarding — and where a venture delivers software, design, analytics or training to a customer?* | **[S-A: A-10 L5]**; **[S-B: EV-5]** *"professional indemnity on client work"*; **[S-ST: Annexure B]** *"professional indemnity for advice"* | **[A]** **The Specialist tier will include professionals; their advice creates exposure** **[S-A: L5]**. Whether the insured is the member, the entity or both is `[V]` |
| **INS-03** | **Worker-related liability and employer's obligations** | *What obligations and cover attach in respect of persons performing work, at each of the five source classifications — employee, contract labour, volunteer, stipend, business participant — and does the answer change the organisation's liability for their acts?* | **[S-A: A-10 L7]**; **[S-A: A-V3]**; **[S-ST: Annexure B Employment ◆]** | **Directly dependent on UE-02.** **L7**: *"Classification is UNRESOLVED, so **liability is currently indeterminate**"* |
| **INS-04** | **Volunteer-related cover** | *What personal accident or medical-expense cover can be provided for members acting in organisational capacity, at what cost per member, and what activities are excluded? Is any cover available at all for **volunteer first-response activity**?* | **[S-A: A-10 L2]**; **[S-A: A-V4]** *"whether any cover exists for **volunteer first-response activity**"*; **[S-C: HD-C9]** *"Determines whether volunteers and members are covered at all — **a human question as much as a financial one**"* | **L2 is the corpus's most morally urgent question.** Cost per member is a number that **does not exist** — Rule G-6 |
| **INS-05** | **Personal accident** | *As INS-04, and: what must a member be told in writing, in their own language, about what is and is not covered, **before** they take a safety role?* | **[S-A: A-10 L2, L12]**; **[S-A: A-10 §9.2 rule 3]** *"Every member taking a safety role receives a **written cover statement** … Signed acknowledgement retained"*; **[S-A: A-H?]** cover statement as a Level 3–4 restraint | The **statement** is issued by somebody. **L12**: *"A member who believes they are covered and is not **has been misled by the organisation**"* |
| **INS-06** | **Property and equipment** | *What cover exists for materials, tools, inventory, City warehouse stock, equipment and premises used for organisational activity, and who may hold it?* | **Derived** from **[S: App. A §14.3]** *"materials, tools"*; **[S: Ch.7]** City warehouse; **[S: App. D SOP-15]** inventory issue; **[S-A: A-10 §9.4 item 2]** property damage recorded and reported to the owner and to Finance. **⟨new — v1.3-G⟩ the corpus never raises property cover as a question** | Insurable interest requires an owner. **UE-32**: may a team hold property at all? |
| **INS-07** | **Cyber and data** | *What cover exists for a data breach, a credential compromise, a platform failure or a ransom event, and how does it interact with the statutory notification obligations and the rehearsed breach runbook?* | **[S-A: A-V4]** *"cyber"*; **[S-B: EV-5]** *"cyber"*; **[S-A: A-17 P5]**; **[S-A: A-14 §13.4]** credential dual custody; **[X]** DPDP notification windows | **Depends on who the fiduciary is (UE-07)** and on whether any technology is operated at all — **[S-B: §40.2]** the pilot has none, so **this is a post-pilot question at pilot scale and a pre-pilot question at platform scale** |
| **INS-08** | **Governance / officers / directors / trustees** | *What cover is required for officers, governors and the founder personally, given that the Council structure is not yet constituted? Is **trustee liability** cover available, and for whom?* | **[S-A: A-10 L6]**; **[S-A: A-V4]** *"**trustee liability**"*; **[S-B: EV-5]** *"trustee liability"*; **[S-ST: Annexure B]** *"director/officer cover"*; **[S-D: LV-22]** cover for leaders acting under emergency authority | **The most form-sensitive category in this Part.** **L6** reason, verbatim: *"**Personal exposure deters capable people from governance roles**"* — which is a **UE-25** input |
| **INS-09** | **Vehicle and transport** | *What cover and what permissions attach to transporting an injured person, moving a person in crisis, ambulance-like transport, or the use of any vehicle in organisational activity — and should any of it simply be prohibited?* | **[S-A: A-10 L15]** *"Does any activity contemplated (**e.g. transporting an injured person**, administering anything, moving a person in crisis) create liability that protocol should simply **prohibit**? — The cheapest risk control is the activity not performed"*; **L11** *"ambulance-like transport"* | **[A]** **Whether the entity may operate or authorise transport at all** is a V-02 permission question before it is an insurance question |
| **INS-10** | **Legal-defence support** | *To what extent is the organisation obliged or permitted to fund a member's legal defence, on what conditions, and is any of that cost insurable?* | **[S-A: A-10 §9.3 `[V]` note]**, verbatim: *"The extent of the organisation's obligation or discretion to fund a member's legal defence, and the conditions on which it may do so, **require counsel review and depend on entity form, classification and available cover.** This protocol defines the **process**, not the **entitlement**"*; **[S-A: A-V4]** *"legal defence"*; **[S-ST: F-10 item 11]** worst case: *"**A member imprisoned or bankrupted after acting on the organisation's doctrine. There is no version of this that the organisation survives intact**"* | **Expressly entity-form dependent — the corpus says so in terms** |
| **INS-11** | **Media / defamation / communications** | *What cover exists for media and defamation exposure arising from published claims, corrections, refusals, beneficiary stories or a crisis response?* | **[S-E: LV-E-20]** *"Insurance for members, third parties, public liability and **media/defamation exposure**"*; **[S-E: LV-E-22]** defamation, contempt and the limits of public correction | The publisher must be a person. **[S-E: Rule E-186]** makes correction approval-free — **which means the publisher cannot choose not to publish** |
| **INS-12** | **Business / venture delivery** | *Whether any cover exists for venture delivery failure, and what a customer contract may lawfully exclude?* | **[S-B: EV-5]** *"whether any cover exists for **venture delivery**"*; **[S-B: EV-6]** *"exclusion of liability for venture work"* | In a separated structure (**HY-02**), the venture entity's programme and the service entity's programme are **different programmes** — `[V]` |

**[P]** **Every cell below reads `QUESTION FOR BROKER/COUNSEL`.** No cell asserts availability. The differentiation is **only** in *which question the form changes*.

| **OPT-B Society** | Whether **governing-body member** cover is available on the same terms, and whether state-specific regimes affect the risk profile | **[S-ST: §5 item 3]** *"state-specific trust/society regimes differ by state"* — `[V]` |

| **OPT-E / F / G** | **`UNKNOWN FROM CURRENT CORPUS`.** Whether cover is written on the firm, on the partners, or on the individual — and whether members' and volunteers' exposure is thereby increased — is a broker and counsel question with **no corpus anchor** | Rule G-16, Rule G-19 |
| **OPT-H / HY-02 Two-entity** | **Two programmes, or one across two persons?** Whether a member working across both is covered for both; whether **related-party** arrangements affect underwriting; whether the **brand licence** creates an exposure for the licensor | **[S-ST: R4 (ii)]**; **HY-02 preconditions** — `[V]` |
| **OPT-I / HY-03 Parent + operating** | Whether risk is **mixed with the broader Krytos pool** — the source's own words — and whether the parent's existing programme extends, excludes or duplicates | **[S: App. A §3.2 row 1]** *"May mix charitable, commercial and protection risk with broader Krytos"* — `[V]` |

## XIII.4 The four insurance rules the corpus imposes, carried forward unchanged

| Rule | Source text |
|---|---|
| **1. No written confirmation, no activation** | *"Protection activity may not be activated, trained for operationally, publicised, or represented to any external party until the relevant items in §9.1 are answered in writing and the answers are recorded as governance evidence"* — **[S-A: A-10 §9.2]** |
| **2. The Public Protection Statement is gated** | *"The Public Protection Statement (A-07) **may not be published before the answers to L2, L3, L4, L8 and L12 exist.** Publishing a description of protective activity without cover converts an internal doctrine into a **public representation**"* — **[S-A: A-10 §9.2 rule 2]** |
| **3. Every safety role receives a written cover statement** | *"in their own language: what is covered, what is not, what they must do if something happens, and whom to call. **Signed acknowledgement retained**"* — **[S-A: A-10 §9.2 rule 3]** |
| **4. Late notification commonly voids cover** | *"What is the **incident-notification obligation to an insurer, and within what time**? — **Late notification commonly voids cover**"* — **[S-A: A-10 L14]**. **[A]** Notification requires a **policyholder**, which requires an entity |

**[P] Rule G-36 (Insurance Is A Pre-Activation Cost, Not A Post-Incident One).** **[S-B: OC-20]**: `C_ins` is *"**Unknown until a broker answers [V]** — and a **pre-activation cost** for protection activity."* **[S-A: §23.2 A-B2]** reasoning: *"**You cannot retro-insure a known event**; an uncovered injured member is an **irreversible** moral and financial failure."* **[S-F: BLK-D-08]** lists insurance premia as a pre-pilot funding requirement with the note *"**May be unavailable for volunteer first-response activity [V]**."* **Whether the premium is affordable cannot be assessed, because [S-B: OC-22] records no compliance cost and `LDR-U17` records that the economic blueprint was never attached.**

# PART XIV — TAX AND REGULATORY VALIDATION REQUIREMENTS

## XIV.1 The governing rule

**[P] Rule G-37 (`REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE` Is A Status, Not A Placeholder).** Every row in this Part carries that status. **No row states a rate, a threshold, a liability, an exemption, an eligibility, a filing obligation, a due date or a consequence.** Where the corpus supplies an external proposition, it is reproduced with its **`[X]`** marker and its own **`[V]`** requirement intact — **Rule G-10**.

**[S]** **[S: App. A §3.2]** states the trigger: *"Before **taking public money, employing people, contracting at scale, operating regulated services, handling sensitive personal data or deploying protection/first-response functions**, qualified professionals must validate the legal entity, **tax registration**, labour/employment model, contracts, accounting design, safeguarding obligations, insurance and **regulatory permissions**."*

**[S]** **[S: Ch.31]** makes *"Tax/accounting treatment of revenue and funds"* a `Blocker` whose **dependency is "Entity choice"** and whose evidence is *"Tax memo / accounting policy."*

**[S]** **[S-F: V-04]**: *"**A practising Chartered Accountant with Indian not-for-profit tax experience, plus tax counsel** … **YES ◆ — 12A/80G/FCRA conditions attach to the entity chosen under V-01.**"*

## XIV.2 Tax and regulatory question register — TX-01 … TX-40

### A. Income tax and exemption

| ID | Question | Source | Status |
|---|---|---|---|

# PART XV — 12A / 80G / FCRA APPLICABILITY BY STRUCTURE

## XV.1 The three regimes, exactly as the corpus records them

**[P]** All three are **`[X]` external propositions carried forward by the corpus**, each with its own `[V]`. **Rule G-10 and Rule G-22 apply: nothing below asserts that any registration is available, that any benefit arises, or that any condition is met.**

| Regime | Corpus text | Sources |
|---|---|---|
| **12A** | *"12A income-tax exemption and its conditions, including **application of income (~85%)** and the **prohibition on political activity or support to parties/candidates**"* **[S-ST: Annexure C item 2]**; *"**[X]** 12A exemption is conditional on applying **at least 85% of income to charitable purposes** and on **no political activity or support to any party or candidate**, **with cancellation risk**"* **[S-D: §50.5]**; *"tax exposure if 12A conditions (including the ~85% application-of-income rule and the no-political-activity condition) are not met **[X]**"* **[S-ST: F-32]**; *"Published practitioner guidance indicates such a condition exists and that **registration can be jeopardised [X]** — must be confirmed for the actual entity and circumstances"* **[S-A: §3.1 A.3]** | **[S-ST: Annexure B/C]**; **[S-D: §50.5, LV-19]**; **[S-E: LV-E-2]**; **[S-A: §3.1 A.3]**; **[S-F: BLK-B-02, V-04]** |
| **80G** | *"80G donor deduction and its **cash-donation limit**"* **[S-ST: Annexure C item 2]**; *"**donor-deduction eligibility and its cash-donation limits**"* **[S-ST: Annexure B Tax ◆]**; *"Whether **donation/deduction eligibility for donors is affected by any political adjacency**"* **[S-A: §3.1 A.3 row 3]** | Same set, plus **[S-C: HV-9]** |
| **FCRA** | *"FCRA **generally requiring about three years of track record and a dedicated account** before foreign contribution may be received"* **[S-ST: Annexure C item 2]**; *"FCRA generally requiring a **multi-year track record** and a **dedicated account**"* **[S-A: Annexure C item 2]**; *"regulatory exposure if foreign contribution is received **without FCRA registration or prior permission**"* **[S-ST: F-32]**; *"**FCRA eligibility and timing**"* **[S-A: A-V2]** | Same set, plus **[S-D: LV-20]**, **[S-E: LV-E-3]**, **[S-B: §33.2, E-12]** |

## XV.2 Applicability by candidate structure

**[P]** The brief requires this analysis. **It is delivered as a question schedule per structure, because the corpus's own position is that applicability *"cannot be assessed"* until the structure is chosen** — **[S-D: §50.5]**: *"**NO — the entity structure is `UNRESOLVED` (LDR-U08), so 12A's political-activity condition, 80G and FCRA applicability cannot be assessed**."*

| Structure | **12A — questions counsel must answer** | **80G — questions counsel must answer** | **FCRA — questions counsel must answer** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **OPT-A Section 8 company** | Is exemption available to this form; on what application-of-income condition; does the commercial pillar's revenue count as income for that condition; is there a business-activity restriction or a separate-business requirement; what is the cancellation risk from political adjacency? | Is donor deduction available; what cash-donation limit applies; does commercial activity affect it? | Is foreign contribution receivable; after what track record; in what dedicated account; on what prior permission; does the three-year expectation interact with a pilot that has no track record? |
| **OPT-B Society** | As OPT-A, **plus**: does the **state-specific society regime** affect eligibility or reporting — **[S-ST: §5 item 3]** *"state-specific trust/society regimes differ by state"*? | As OPT-A, plus any state-level donor-facing consequence | As OPT-A, plus whether a multi-state society structure changes the position |
| **OPT-C Public charitable trust** | As OPT-A, **plus**: how are **trustees** treated for these purposes; does **trustee liability** cover interact with any exemption condition (**[S-A: A-V4]**)? | As OPT-A | As OPT-A |
| **OPT-D Company / business entity** | **Is exemption available at all to a form whose source-recorded purpose is commercial?** **[S: App. A §3.2 row 2]**: *"suitable for commercial ventures"*; risks: *"**Social service and donations may need separate treatment**"* → **STRUCTURAL SEPARATION MAY BE REQUIRED — COUNSEL TO DETERMINE** | Same question, with the same separation consequence | Same |
| **OPT-E LLP · OPT-F Partnership · OPT-G Proprietorship** | **`UNKNOWN FROM CURRENT CORPUS`** — no statement exists. **Question for counsel: is exemption available to this form at all, and if not, is the option excluded?** | **`UNKNOWN`** | **`UNKNOWN`** |
| **OPT-H Hybrid / two-entity** | **Which entity's income is measured against the application-of-income condition?** Does a payment from the commercial entity to the service entity count as application of income, as a related-party transaction, or as something else? **[S-ST: R4 (ii)]**: *"strict **transfer-pricing discipline [V]**"* | Which entity may issue a deduction-eligible receipt; may the commercial entity receive a donation at all? | Which entity holds the dedicated account; does the **track record** of one entity assist the other? |
| **OPT-I Parent + operating entity** | Does the parent's activity affect the operating entity's condition; does *"mixing charitable, commercial and protection risk with broader Krytos"* **[S: App. A §3.2 row 1]** have a tax consequence? | As above | As above — **and UE-24: Krytos's own position is unknown** |
| **OPT-J Other** | To be determined once counsel identifies the form (Rule G-17) | — | — |

| **2** | **Commercial activity** | **[S-C: HV-9]** *"**12A/80G interaction with any commercial activity**"*; **[S-B: EV-2]** *"12A/80G conditions and **the effect of any commercial activity**"*; **[S: App. A §1.3]** *"the business component remains central"* | **12A** primarily; **80G** secondarily |

# PART XVI — DATA GOVERNANCE AND FIDUCIARY IDENTITY

## XVI.1 The central question, preserved as unresolved

> ## **WHO IS THE DATA-CONTROLLING / FIDUCIARY ENTITY?**
> ### **`UNRESOLVED — A-N14. THIS INSTRUMENT DOES NOT ANSWER IT.`**

**[S]** **[S-A: §22 N14]** — *"**Data fiduciary identity and named accountable officer** — `UNRESOLVED` — Risk: **Regulator has no contactable person; duties unassigned** — Action: **Assign per entity and per data category** — Owner: CEO + Legal + AI & Data — Validation: **Yes** — Window: **Before the first 1,000 personal records**."*

**[S]** **[S-A: §11.2 Data estate row]** — concentration **High**; *"**the legal data fiduciary is presumably the entity, i.e. ultimately Krytos**"* — status **`UNRESOLVED (A-17)`**.

**[S]** **[S-F: chain 6]** — *"Entity structure → data fiduciary identity (A-N14) → privacy notice and breach notification … **fiduciary duties attach to a legal person; without one there is no notifier** and [X] DPDP timelines cannot be met."*

**[S]** **[S-D: §50.6]** — *"Is a DPIA required? **`CANNOT BE DETERMINED` — depends on Significant Data Fiduciary status, which depends on scale and on the unresolved entity structure [V]**."*

**[A]** The word *"presumably"* in **[S-A: §11.2]** is the corpus's own marker that this is an inference and not a determination. **This paper does not convert it into one.**

## XVI.2 Data classes and their fiduciary questions — DT-01 … DT-12

**Classification basis:** **[S-A: A-17 §16.1]** four levels **C1–C4**; **[S-A: A-20]** sealed, case-scoped C4; **[S-D: §31.2]** leadership-record classification; **[S-B: NM-4]** *"sensitive personal information … **Data is not an asset to be sold**."*

| ID | Data class | Corpus classification and controls | **Fiduciary question** | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **DT-01** | **Member records** | **C3**; full lifecycle *"application → screening → identity verification → interview → training → probation → role"*; **no identity documents below L3**; **A-N32** prohibited-collection list; deletion on request at L1–L2 | Who is the fiduciary for a person who has **not joined anything** — an L0 observer, an L1 participant, an L2 contributor? **May an unincorporated network hold these records at all?** | **[S-C: §6, HV-3, HV-6]**; **[S-A: A-17, A-18]** |
| **DT-02** | **Beneficiary data** | **[S-A: A-19]** Beneficiary Privacy & Dignity Code; **access separation matrix**; **three separate consents** — to receive help, to be recorded, to be published — *"each separately refus­able"*; **no photos of recipients**; storytelling/service separation | Who receives the consent, and in whose name? **[S-F: Part XI]**: *"Specificity test: **a general consent covering publication is invalid**"* | **[S-A: A-19]**; **[S-E: E-34]** |
| **DT-03** | **Incident data** | **[S-A: A-09]** incident matrix, mandatory triggers, do-not-attend list; **A-06 V42–V43** visibility fields on every incident record; **legal hold** applied on a member-support matter | Who holds an incident record that may become evidence in a criminal or civil matter, and who is served with a notice about it? | **[S-A: A-09, A-10 §9.3 stage 4]** |
| **DT-04** | **Safeguarding data** | **C4 sealed**; **case-scoped access with reason codes**; **two-person rule**; **prohibited uses including commercial use**; **subject rights**; *"Sensitive records created before the model exists **cannot be retro-protected**"* (**A-H3**) | Who is answerable to a survivor, to a guardian and to a regulator — and may a network without legal personality accept guardian consent? | **[S-A: A-20]**; **[S-C: HV-2, HV-6]**; **[S-D: LV-17]** |
| **DT-05** | **Performance data** | Teams may be ranked; **a person is never scored** (**[S-C: §23.2]**, **[S-D: Rule D-109]** six-condition test); bands not scores (**Rule D-63**); **small-cell suppression** (**Rule D-108**) with the threshold `UNKNOWN`; leaderboard publicity `UNRESOLVED` (**LDR-U11**) | Who publishes, who is answerable for a suppression decision, and who owns the record when a team ceases to exist? | **[S-D: Part 6, Part 21, Rule D-108]**; **[S-E: N-E-23]** |
| **DT-06** | **AI training and AI output data** | **A-21** risk tiers, review caps, language guard; **A-22** seventeen-field consequential-recommendation record — *"prompt, output, model, sources, user, **reviewer**, final decision"*; **Rule D-107** historical bias never used as prediction labels (`UNVALIDATED`); *"**audit fields cannot be retro-fitted onto past logs**"* (**A-I8**); **no AI in the pilot** | Who is the fiduciary for a model's training data, and who is the accountable officer for an automated recommendation the architecture forbids anyway? | **[S-A: A-21, A-22]**; **[S-D: L-26, AI-1…AI-8]** |
| **DT-07** | **Financial data** | Payment sheets with formula version; ledgers; reconciliation; reserve and social-impact positions; **accrued compensation ledger (B-05)**; published accounts (TS-3) | Whose financial records are these, and against whose statutory audit are they produced? | **[S: Ch.19, App. A §20.5]**; **[S-E: TS-3]**; **UE-27** |
| **DT-08** | **Contracts** | Customer contracts, member engagement terms, MOUs, processor/vendor contracts (**A-N30**), brand licences | Who is the contracting party of record, and where is the register? **[S-A: A-14 §13.7]** requires a *"contracts and partnership register"* at handover | **[S: App. A §13.4]**; **[S-A: A-17 P6]**; **UE-26** |
| **DT-09** | **Communications** | Public statements, claim register, terminology register, **correction log**, **refusal log**, the communication archive, crisis records | Who is the publisher of record — and **[S-E: Rule E-186]** makes correction entry approval-free, so **the publisher cannot decline to publish a correction** | **[S-E: TS-1…TS-14, Part 53]**; **[S-A: A-04]** |
| **DT-10** | **Digital assets** | Domain, code repository, cloud, payment gateway, communication channels, social accounts, **statutory portals** — all under **dual custody** `[P]`; **Rule E-188 prohibits personal registration**; **N-E-20** *"**Informal is the current default and is the risk**"* | **In whose name is each asset registered?** Rule E-188 forbids the answer that is currently the default. **Organisational registration requires an organisation** | **[S-E: N-E-19, N-E-20]**; **[S-A: A-14 §13.4]** |
| **DT-11** | **Contribution and portability records** | **Narrative, non-ordinal**, held by the person, countersigned by the verifier; *"any score, rank or band is a breach"*; portability across localities is **BLK-F-07** (*"requires ≥ 2 localities"*) | Who guarantees portability of a record a person holds, and who is answerable if it is lost? | **[S-C: H-09]**; **[S-E: E-41]**; **[S-F: XI.2]** |
| **DT-12** | **Composition and equality data** | **A-N5** `UNRESOLVED — deliberately`; **[S-E: Rule E-48]** prohibits caste data **for any purpose**; **[S-A: A-18 §17.4]** ten prohibitions; **the pilot should collect none** | If it is never collected, there is no fiduciary question. **If it ever is, the fiduciary question is the most serious in this Part** | **[S-A: §22 N5]**; **[S-E: N-E-22]**; **[S-C: HD-C11, HD-14]** |

## XVI.3 Entity dependence of the privacy programme

**[A]** The corpus's own privacy minimum (**A-B7 / BLK-C-02 / P0-H**) consists of five instruments. **Four of the five name a person.**

| Instrument | Source | **Named person required?** |
|---|---|---|
| **C1–C4 classification and field register** | **A-N25**; **[S-A: A-17 §16.1–16.2]** | No — but somebody must adopt it |
| **Retention and erasure schedule with counsel-set periods** | **A-N26**; `UNRESOLVED` | No — but counsel must be instructed **by somebody** |
| **Privacy notice, published in the operating language** | **A-N27**; **[S-F: BLK-B-11]** LA-1 texts | **YES — a notice names the fiduciary.** **[S-F: Q19]** lists *"the privacy notice's named data fiduciary"* among the things the entity decision unblocks |
| **Breach runbook, rehearsed, with named roles and clocks** | **A-N29**; **[X]** 72-hour Board report, 6-hour CERT-In | **YES — [S-F: chain 6]: "without one there is no notifier"** |
| **Named privacy officer / accountable individual** | **A-N14**; **[S-F: BLK-A-13, SR-05]**; **[S-A: A-14 §13.4]** *"A named accountable officer for data protection **at all times, including during a vacancy**, with the ability to execute breach notification duties"* | **YES — and the appointment must survive an apex vacancy** |

**[P] Rule G-40 (The Privacy Notice Cannot Be Published Before The Fiduciary Is Named, And The Fiduciary Cannot Be Named Before The Entity Exists).** This is **[S-F: Rule F-8]** (*"Validation Precedes Build"*) applied to the privacy minimum: *"Building a privacy notice before the retention schedule is validated … produces an artefact that must be rebuilt."* **The pilot may design all five instruments on paper. It may not publish the notice or collect systematically.** **[S-F: XXV.7]**: *"Any systematic personal-data collection — A-B7 privacy minimum, including a retention schedule. **Over-retention is a breach from day one**."*

# PART XVIII — DISSOLUTION, EXIT AND ASSET CONSEQUENCES

## XVIII.1 The source text, in full

**[S]** **[S: Const. Art.19 — Dissolution / restructuring]** `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`, verbatim and complete:

> *"Dissolution, merger, restructuring, transfer of assets, reserve use and social-impact fund handling must follow **legal structure, donor restrictions, contracts and statutory obligations**."*

**[A]** **That is the entire content of the corpus's dissolution architecture.** Nineteen articles, and the nineteenth says only that the answer depends on four things, the first of which is the unresolved one. **No instrument in v1.2 or v1.3-A…F proposes a dissolution clause, names a dissolving authority, specifies an asset destination, or addresses creditor or member claims on winding up.** `insolvency` and `winding up` do not appear as terms of art (§I.3).

**[S]** The corroborating statements:

| Source | Text |
|---|---|
| **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]** | *"**dissolution, merger or restructuring [S: Const. Art.19]**"* is a **reserved matter** — *"may **not** be taken by an acting or interim authority under any scenario"* |
| **[S-A: A-14 S5]** | Prohibited to the interim authority on the founder's death: *"Tier-1 change; **asset transfer**; mission change; **dissolution or restructuring** ([S: Art.19] requires professional validation)"* |
| **[S-A: A-V1]** | Corporate counsel must advise on *"**dissolution and restructuring**"* |
| **[S-ST: Annexure B]** | *"**dissolution and asset-transfer constraints**; enforceability of a mission lock"* |
| **[S-F: BLK-B-01]** | Validate *"Entity form, objects clauses permitting all four pillars, **dissolution/restructuring ([S: Const. Art.19])**"* |
| **[S-A: §22 N12]** | Risk of unresolved brand ownership: *"**exit and dissolution become chaotic**"* |
| **[S-E: N-E-17]** | *"**Any choice has succession and dissolution consequences**"* |
| **[S-ST: F-11 item 11]** | Worst case: *"A national institution with lakhs of members and real community trust is **legally repurposed or dissolved by a decision no member can contest**"* |
| **[S-B: §21.4]** | *"Reserves are **not team property**; a team has no balance to transfer"* — so on a team's closure there is no asset to distribute |
| **[S: App. A §13.5]** | Venture closure: *"Closure should **settle customers, recover assets, revoke accesses, record lessons** and prevent leaderboard manipulation"* — the only closure procedure in the corpus, and it is a **venture** procedure, not an entity one |
| **[S-D: Rule D-26]** | **Dignified exit** for a person — the corpus designs exit for roles and members in detail, and for the institution not at all |

| Dimension | NFP forms (OPT-A/B/C) | Company form (OPT-D) | LLP / partnership / proprietorship (OPT-E/F/G) | Hybrid or two-entity (OPT-H) | Parent + operating (OPT-I) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| **DS-06 What happens to data?** | `[V]` — **retention schedule `UNRESOLVED` (A-N26)**; **legal hold** may require preservation; **subject rights** under **[S-A: A-20 §19.5]** and **[S: Const. Art.6]** survive the person or do not | `[V]` | **`UK`** | `[V]` — **which of two fiduciaries erases what?** | `[V]` |
| **DS-07 What happens to active projects and committed service?** | `[V]` — **[S-B: §33.3]**: safeguarding-critical service *"**MUST continue**"* and *"may never stop"* for a financial reason. **[A]** Whether it can stop for a **dissolution** is not addressed anywhere | `[V]` | **`UK`** | `[V]` | `[V]` |
| **DS-08 What happens to members, volunteers and workers?** | `[V]` — **notice and final settlement** **[S-C: HV-1]**; **accrued unpaid compensation** may be a **wage debt** **[S-B: EV-3]**; **dignified exit** **[S-D: Rule D-26]** is designed for roles, not for an institution | `[V]` | **`UK`** | `[V]` — **a member of one entity may be a worker of the other** | `[V]` |

## XVIII.3 The three dissolution findings the corpus supports

**[A]** Each is an analysis of source statements, not a legal conclusion.

| # | Finding | Basis |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Dissolution is the one constitutional article that is entirely a pointer.** Art.19 contains no rule — only four dependencies, the first of which is `LDR-U08`. **The constitution therefore cannot be applied to its own termination.** | **[S: Const. Art.19]** |
| **2** | **Dissolution is a reserved matter, and the holder of the reserved matters is undetermined.** So the architecture simultaneously forbids an interim authority from dissolving the organisation and identifies nobody who may. **In a crisis (SUC-08) or a contested apex (SUC-10) this is a deadlock, not a protection.** | **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]**; **[S-D: Part 34]**; **UE-14** |
| **3** | **The choice of structure is the choice of exit route, and it is made before the exit route is known.** **[S-ST: R4 (i)]** warns that deferring is *"expensive or **impossible later** once contracts and reputation attach to one name"*; **[S-A: N12]** warns that unresolved brand ownership makes *"exit and dissolution … chaotic"*; **[S-ST: F-11]** warns that without a lock the institution can be *"legally repurposed or dissolved by a decision no member can contest."* **[A]** Taken together, these say that the entity decision **is** the dissolution decision, taken early and in the dark — which is precisely why **[S-ST: Annexure B]** requires counsel to advise on *"**feasibility of altering objects later**"* and on *"dissolution and asset-transfer constraints"* **before** the choice is made | **[S-ST: R4, F-11, Annexure B]**; **[S-A: N12]** |

**[P] Rule G-42 (No Dissolution Clause Is Proposed).** This Part does not draft, sketch or recommend a dissolution provision, a cy-près direction, an asset-transfer destination or a member-claim rule. **Art.19's four dependencies are reproduced, and the questions are put.** **UE-21 remains `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`.**

---

# PART XIX — PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION PACKAGE

## XIX.1 Method and the cross-walk

**[P]** The brief names nine professionals. **The corpus names twelve counsel categories (A-V1…A-V12), ten human-system categories (HV-1…HV-10), twenty-five leadership categories (LV-1…LV-25), thirty identity categories (LV-E-1…LV-E-30) and ten economic categories (EV-1…EV-10) — consolidated by [S-F: Part III] into seventeen categories V-01…V-17 covering 77 distinct items.** This Part maps the nine onto the corpus's own categories **without replacing any ID** (**Rule F-4**).

| Brief's professional | Corpus category / categories | **[S-F]** category | Pre-pilot? |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Corporate lawyer** | **A-V1**; HV-6; LV-8; LV-E-1, LV-E-11; EV-4 (partly) | **V-01** | **YES ◆ — A-B1** |
| **Tax advisor** | **A-V2**; HV-9; LV-19, LV-20; LV-E-2, LV-E-3; **EV-2** | **V-04** (with **V-03** for the CA limb) | **YES ◆** |
| **Labour lawyer** | **A-V3**; **HV-1**; LV-3, LV-4, LV-6, LV-7 (partly); LV-E-24 (partly); **EV-3** | **V-05** | **YES ◆ — A-B1** |
| **Company secretary** | **A-V9** (the corpus merges company-secretarial and governance work into one category — **[S-F: PD-9]**: *"A-V9 is the only counsel category that addresses LDR-U01 directly"*); LV-9, LV-10 | **V-10** | Important-before-scaling, **except A-B9 bank mandate which is pre-pilot** |
| **Banking professional** | **A-V10** (*"the only one that addresses the bank-mandate BLOCKER A-B9"* — PD-9); **EV-10** | **V-10** (banking limb), with **V-03** for custody accounting | **YES — A-B9** |
| **Insurance advisor** | **A-V4**; LV-22; LV-E-20; **EV-5**; **[S-A: A-10 §9.1 L1–L15]** | **V-06** | **YES — A-B2 BLOCKER** |
| **Privacy professional** | **A-V6**; **HV-3**; LV-11…LV-14, LV-25; LV-E-10…LV-E-15, LV-E-28; **EV-7**; **[S-A: P1…P6]** | **V-07** | **YES ◆ — A-B7 BLOCKER** |
| **IP lawyer** | **A-V8**; LV-24 (partly); LV-E-4…LV-E-7, LV-E-9 | **V-11** | Important-before-scaling (**A-I6**), **but LV-E-9 non-police ID card design is pre-pilot — [S: Ch.31] blocker** |
| **Governance expert** | **A-V9**; **[S-A: A-13, A-14, A-16; A-N17, A-N18, A-N19]**; LV-9, LV-10; LV-E-23 (partly) | **V-10** | Important-before-scaling (**A-I1…I3**) |

**[A]** **The brief's "company secretary" and "governance expert" are one corpus category (A-V9 / V-10).** They are separated below because their questions differ — the company secretary's are **formal and filing-oriented**, the governance expert's are **authority and process-oriented** — but **the corpus records them as a single engagement**, and **[S-F: XXV.4 Action 1]** requires corporate, tax and labour counsel to be commissioned **"as one engagement."**

**[P] Rule G-43 (No Professional Is Named).** Inherited from **[S-F: Rule F-7]**: *"Every entry names a **role or category**, never a person, firm or invented title."*

## XIX.2 PV-01 — Corporate / entity lawyer

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| **Questions they must answer** | **(1)** Which legal forms are available in India for an organisation carrying **all four pillars**, and which are not? **(2)** For each available form: what are the **objects-clause requirements**, and can objects reach all twenty purposes at §V.2? **(3)** What is the **feasibility of altering objects later**? **(4)** What **protective provisions** are available — and can a mission lock, a reserved-matters list or a suspensive veto be made **legally enforceable** in that form? **(5)** What **political-activity restrictions attach to that form**, and what does "political activity" mean for it? **(6)** What are the **dissolution, merger, restructuring and asset-transfer constraints**? **(7)** In each form, **who holds legal title, who holds control, and may the two be separated**? **(8)** May an **unincorporated network or an internal division of Krytos** lawfully: issue credentials, hold records, publish a directory, accept guardian consent, contract, employ, hold a bank balance, be insured, be audited, and answer a regulator? **(9)** May a **Mohalla team or City function hold any balance or asset at all**? **(10)** What is the **authority to advance funds** from a founder or a parent to the entity, and on what instrument? **(11)** For any multi-entity option: what **related-party** and **cross-entity** formalities are required? **(12)** Is **LLP, partnership or proprietorship** available for any of the four functions — and if not, may those options be **excluded and the option set closed**? **(13)** What **execution formalities and authority limits** apply to contracts, MOUs and banking documents? **(14)** What **statutory registers, seals and filings** does each form require, and in whose custody? |
| **Documents they need** | v1.2 incl. Appendix A **[S]** · the Strategic Stress Test **[S-ST]** · v1.3-A…v1.3-F · **this paper** · **[S-A: A-12 §11.2]** asset-by-asset concentration map · **[S-D: Part 34]** authority map · **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]** the seven reserved matters · **[S: App. A §3.1–§3.5]** Krytos position, legal-structure table, authority distinctions, CEO–Council–National Team model · **[S: Const. Art.3, Art.10, Art.18, Art.19]** · **[S: App. A §20.2, §21.2]** account and fund architecture · **[S: App. E]** approval matrix · **[S: App. D]** 22 SOPs · **and, critically, UE-24: Krytos's own constitutive documents, which the corpus does not contain** |
| **Decisions they inform** | **LDR-U08 / SR-01** (structure) · **A-N12 / SR-12** and **A-N13 / SR-13** (whether brand and IP may be held by the entity) · **A-N14 / SR-05** (which person is the fiduciary) · **A-N15 / SR-19** (custody instrument) · **UE-21** (dissolution posture) · **UE-31** (enforceability of the mission lock) · **UE-32** (whether a sub-national layer may hold anything) · **GC-01** (whether the chosen form can carry waterfall priorities 4 and 8) · **GC-02** (whether an internal-division pilot can satisfy P0-A) |
| **Decisions they cannot make** | **They cannot choose the structure.** **[S: App. A §3.5]**: the Founder/CEO may not approve *"final legal/tax conclusions without professionals"* and the National Team may not *"finalise legal structure"* — **and the converse holds: a professional may not decide for the owner.** They cannot resolve **LDR-U01**, **A-N19**, **LDR-U19** or **LDR-U16** (**[S-F: XXV.5]**: *"genuinely unresolvable without authorised human decisions"*). They cannot determine brand or IP **ownership** — *"a property question about the founder's own assets"* **[S-F: XXV.5]**. They cannot authorise informal operation (**Rule G-8**) |
| **Required outputs** | **A written opinion held as governance evidence** — the exact evidence **[S-F: PAK-02]** requires for the *"Entity-structure decision paper and counsel opinion"* artefact; plus, once a structure is chosen: **certificate, objects clause, register of officers** (**[S-F: BLK-C-01]**); a **registration plan** (**[S: App. A §31]**); a **dissolution/restructuring note**; a **mission-lock enforceability note**; an **unincorporated-operation note** if that route is contemplated |
| **Source IDs affected** | **V-01 · A-V1 · HV-6 · LV-8 · LV-E-1 · LV-E-11 · EV-4 · LDR-U08 · A-B1 · BLK-A-01 · BLK-B-01 · BLK-C-01 · SR-01 · PAK-01 · PAK-02 · P0-A · P0-B · CP-2 STEP 1–2 · master path STEP 1–2 · GC-01 · GC-02 · UE-01, UE-05…UE-07, UE-21, UE-24…UE-26, UE-30…UE-32** |

## XIX.3 PV-02 — Tax advisor (Chartered Accountant with Indian not-for-profit experience, plus tax counsel)

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| **Questions they must answer** | **TX-01 … TX-25** in full — in particular: **(1)** eligibility for and conditions of income-tax exemption per form; **(2)** the **application-of-income** condition and how commercial venture receipts are treated within it; **(3)** whether the **eight-priority waterfall** conflicts with any application-of-income requirement (**GC-01**, **[S-D: §50.5 PC-8]** `CANNOT BE ASSESSED`); **(4)** tax treatment of **each of the eleven venture categories**; **(5)** **GST / indirect tax** on services rendered and on physical goods, and the registration threshold; **(6)** **TDS** obligations on worker, supplier, contractor, professional-fee and rent payments (**⟨new — v1.3-G⟩ TX-13**); **(7)** tax treatment of **member payments** at each classification; **(8)** tax treatment of **founder compensation** (priority 4) and **residual surplus / founder upside** (priority 8); **(9)** tax treatment of the **social-impact fund** and **reserves**; **(10)** characterisation of a **float**, a **seeded reserve**, a **waived organisation fee** and a **founder or Krytos advance**; **(11)** **80G**-type donor deduction availability and the **cash-donation limit**; **(12)** **FCRA** eligibility, timing, dedicated-account requirement and the effect of any political adjacency; **(13)** **CSR** recipient eligibility (**⟨new⟩ TX-19**); **(14)** **related-party and transfer-pricing** discipline in any separated structure, including any **brand or IP licence**; **(15)** **state-level** imposts — professional tax, shop-and-establishment, labour welfare, state charitable regimes; **(16)** the **compliance cost** `C_comp` of each form (**CB-21**) so it can be weighed against `H_gov` and the pilot's funding; **(17)** the **thresholds at which obligations attach** (**[S-B: NL-5]**) |
| **Documents they need** | Everything PV-01 needs, **plus** **[S: Ch.18, Ch.19, Ch.20]** and **[S: App. A §14, §20, §21]** in full · **[S-B]** §4 cost structure, §8 waterfall mechanics, §21 reserves, §33 service funding, §43 economic decision register · **[S-B: EV-1, EV-2]** · **[S-ST: Annexure B Tax ◆]** · **[S-A: §3.1 A.3]** · **[S-D: §50.5, LV-7, LV-19, LV-20]** · **[S-E: LV-E-2, LV-E-3]** · **[S-C: HV-9]** · **and [S-B: E-07]** the receipts-versus-invoiced question |
| **Decisions they inform** | **LDR-U08** (which forms are tax-viable for the design) · **GC-01** · **UE-11** donations · **UE-12** who funds the centre · **UE-20** founder compensation · **E-C3/E-C5** percentages and social-impact formula **once measured** · **E-07** accounting basis · **Part XV** in its entirety |
| **Decisions they cannot make** | They cannot select the structure · cannot set any percentage, cap or multiplier (**LDR-U18** is `NOT FINAL`; Rule G-6) · cannot decide founder compensation (**`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`**, **Rule D-92** recusal) · cannot determine whether an activity is "political" for organisational purposes — that is **A-01/A-05** policy, and only its **legal** characterisation is theirs · cannot assert that any registration will be granted |
| **Required outputs** | **A written tax opinion per candidate form**, held as governance evidence (**[S-F: PAK-02]**: *"Tax position opinion — 12A / 80G / FCRA — Evidence: **Written opinion**"*); a **tax memo and accounting policy** (**[S: App. A §31]** Tax row); a **compliance calendar** entry set (**[S-ST: F-32 item 7]**); a **related-party and transfer-pricing note** if any separation is contemplated |
| **Source IDs affected** | **V-03 · V-04 · A-V2 · HV-9 · LV-7 · LV-19 · LV-20 · LV-E-2 · LV-E-3 · EV-1 · EV-2 · TX-01…TX-25 · BLK-B-02 · SR-01 · PAK-02 · PAK-03 · GC-01 · UE-10, UE-11, UE-12, UE-20, UE-27** |

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| **Questions they must answer** | **WCQ-01 … WCQ-26** in full — in particular: the mapping of ten role types onto the five source categories; the classification of **each of the nine participation levels and each mode**; whether **L4 specialist payment is supplier or wage**; minimum-wage and **payment-cycle** legality; **statutory social-security and deduction** obligations and their thresholds; whether **pro-rata partial payment (B-06)** is lawful; whether **accrued unpaid compensation (B-05)** is a **wage debt**; whether **trainee time** is payment-eligible; lawfulness of **role-attached political restrictions (A-02)**; enforceability of **conduct, suspension, improvement-plan and removal** procedures; lawfulness of **demotion and dignified exit (Rule D-26)**; **suspension with or without pay** against the priority-1 invariant; **reversed burden of proof** in retaliation complaints; **youth participation** constraints; **appointment letters and contracts in member languages**; **notice and final settlement**; which roles **may never be unpaid (E-10)**; whether a person working across a City shared-service function and a team has **one employer or two**; and the effect of classification on **the organisation's liability for a member's acts (L7)** |

| **Source IDs affected** | **V-05 · A-V3 · HV-1 · LV-3 · LV-4 · LV-6 · LV-7 · LV-E-24 · EV-3 · WCQ-01…WCQ-26 · LDR-U09 · A-B1 · BLK-A-02 · BLK-B-03 · SR-02 · PAK-02 · PAK-04 · CP-2 STEP 4 · master path STEP 2 · UE-02** |

## XIX.5 PV-04 — Company secretary

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| **Questions they must answer** | **(1)** What **constituent instruments, resolutions, registers and filings** does each candidate form require, in what order, and with what evidence of completion? **(2)** What **statutory registers** must exist, in whose **custody**, at what **location**, with what **handover log**? — **[S-A: A-14 §13.4]** marks this *"**[V] Entity-form dependent**"* · **(3)** What is the **filings calendar** and who maintains it? · **(4)** What **execution formalities** apply to contracts, banking mandates, property transfers and licences? · **(5)** How is a **board or governing-body meeting** validly convened, quorate and minuted in each form — and may a **sole owner** be bound by its resolutions? · **(6)** What **officer registrations** are required, and who may hold them? · **(7)** How is a **change of signatory, officer or registered address** effected, and how long does it take? — the operational question behind **A-N16** · **(8)** What **annual return, compliance certificate and audit** requirements attach? · **(9)** How is a **merger, restructuring or dissolution** effected procedurally? · **(10)** What are **Krytos's** own secretarial requirements — **[S-A: A-V9]** lists *"Krytos governing documents"* as an express question |

| **Required outputs** | **A secretarial and filings plan per candidate form**; a **statutory-register and custody schedule**; a **compliance calendar**; **template resolutions** for incorporation, banking mandate, officer appointment and reserved-matter adoption; **the register of officers** required by **[S-F: BLK-C-01]** |
| **Source IDs affected** | **V-10 · A-V9 · LV-9 · LV-10 · BLK-C-01 · BLK-C-06 · SR-01 · SR-03 · PAK-01 · PAK-03 · UE-25, UE-26, UE-27 · GVQ-02…GVQ-04, GVQ-12** |

## XIX.6 PV-05 — Banking professional

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| **Questions they must answer** | **(1)** What **account structures** can implement the six source financial areas at **[S: App. A §20.2]** — team accounts/ledgers, venture revenue accounts, organisation account, reserve funds, social-impact funds, petty cash — and which of them require **separate accounts** rather than separate ledgers? **(2)** What **minimum signatory set** and what **continuity mechanism** will keep priority-1 payments moving through death, incapacity, removal, resignation or a contested apex? **(3)** What **custody instrument** can hold **reserves** such that the restriction survives a change of control? **(4)** What **custody instrument** can hold the **restricted social-impact fund** such that the programme list and the four exclusions are enforceable against a successor? **(5)** May a **local team or City function** hold a balance, an imprest or a float — and if not, how are team accounts operated? **(6)** What **payment rails and processing costs** attach to a **weekly** cadence at pilot scale, and what changes at monthly or fortnightly cadence (**E-09**)? **(7)** What **collection instruments** are available for **SME customers**, and what is the cost of a collection cycle (**B-13**, **P-05**)? **(8)** How are **donor-restricted gifts** received, held, applied and reported — and may they be received before any registration? **(9)** What **dual-custody and emergency-access** arrangements are available for banking credentials, payment-gateway credentials and statutory portals? **(10)** What **bank-side know-your-customer, beneficial-ownership and officer-verification** requirements will the chosen form trigger? **(11)** How is a **founder or parent advance** received and recorded so that its characterisation (**EV-1**) is unambiguous? |

## XIX.7 PV-06 — Insurance advisor / broker

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| **Questions they must answer** | **L1 … L15** in full, in writing — **INS-01 … INS-12** restate them by category. The five the corpus singles out as gating the Public Protection Statement: **L2** (member accident/medical cover), **L3** (public/third-party liability), **L4** (*"Is any cover available **at all** for acts of 'protection' or 'first response'? **If not, what is the consequence for the pillar's design?**"*), **L8** (the intervention boundary — counsel), **L12** (what a member must be told). Plus **L6** officers, governors and the founder personally; **L7** vicarious liability by classification; **L13** records needed to support a claim or defence; **L14** notification obligation and time limit; **L15** activities that should simply be prohibited. Plus **trustee liability** (**A-V4**, **EV-5**), **cyber** and **media/defamation** (**LV-E-20**) |

## XIX.8 PV-07 — Privacy professional (privacy counsel or certified Data Protection Officer)

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| **Questions they must answer** | **(1)** **Which legal person is the data fiduciary for each of DT-01 … DT-12**, and who is the **named accountable officer** — **[S-A: N14]** action: *"Assign **per entity and per data category**"*? **(2)** What **retention and erasure periods** apply per data category — the corpus *"**deliberately did not invent**"* them (**A-N26**)? **(3)** What must the **privacy notice** say, in which languages, and to whom? **(4)** What **lawful basis and consent design** applies at each of the nine participation levels, including **deletion at L1–L2**, **re-consent on upgrade**, **retention while paused or alumni**, and **portability and erasure** (**HV-3**)? **(5)** What are the **breach-notification obligations and timelines** to the Board, to data principals and to CERT-In **[X]**? **(6)** Does **Significant Data Fiduciary** status attach, at what scale, and with what obligations — India-based DPO, DPIA, independent data auditor, annual assessment, Board reporting **[X]**? — **[S-D: §50.6]**: *"`CANNOT BE DETERMINED` … depends on the unresolved entity structure"* · **(7)** What **children's-data** rules apply, and may an entity or network **accept guardian consent** (**HV-6**)? **(8)** What **processor and vendor contract** terms are required (**A-N30**, **P6**)? **(9)** What **cross-border processing** constraints apply to any cloud service? **(10)** What **AI-log retention** applies to the **A-22** seventeen-field record? **(11)** Is **composition monitoring** lawful in any form, and is any **non-identity proxy** lawful and non-discriminatory (**HD-14**, **Rule E-48**)? **(12)** What **small-cell suppression threshold** makes aggregate reporting safe (**Rule D-108**, threshold `UNKNOWN`)? **(13)** How are **sealed C4 records** and **legal holds** operated lawfully? **(14)** What **grievance-redressal** timelines apply? |
| **Documents they need** | **[S-A: A-17…A-22]** in full · **[S-C: §6, §7, H-02, H-09, H-10, H-18, HV-3]** · **[S-D: Part 29, Part 31, LV-11…LV-14, LV-25]** · **[S-E: LV-E-10…LV-E-16, LV-E-28, Part 53]** · **[S-B: EV-7, NM-4]** · **[S: Ch.22, Ch.23; App. F]** · **[S-A: Annexure C item 1]** the DPDP `[X]` set |
| **Decisions they inform** | **A-N14 / SR-05 / BLK-A-13** · **A-N26 / SR-06** · **A-B7 / BLK-C-02 / BLK-B-07 / P0-H** · **A-N25, A-N27…A-N35** · **UE-07, UE-08, UE-23** · **DT-01…DT-12** · **master path STEP 5** |

**[P] Rule G-44 (PV-01…PV-09 Are Not The Whole Package).** **[S-F: III.3]** lists **eleven pre-pilot validations**; **[S-F: BLK-D-07]** counts **eleven validations across eight professional categories** and calls their cost *"the single most overlooked pre-pilot cost in the corpus."* **A decision-maker who commissions only the nine in this Part will have commissioned an incomplete package.**
