# PART XX — ENTITY DECISION MATRIX

## XX.1 The neutrality rule

**[P] Rule G-45 (No Weighting, No Score, No Winner).** **The corpus authorises no weighting** — **[S-F: Part XX]** records the same constraint for its own decision-neutrality requirement, and **[S-F: VI.2]** ranks blockers *"**by count of chains rooted**, which is observable from Part VI"* precisely because *"no priority score is used, because the corpus does not supply one."* **This matrix therefore contains no rating, no score, no rank, no colour coding, no ordering of options and no winner.** Every cell states **what must be determined**, and names **who determines it**.

**[P] Rule G-46 (A Matrix That Produces A Winner Has Been Misused).** If this Part is read as producing a preferred option, it has been misread. **The twelve dimensions are not commensurable, the corpus supplies no exchange rate between them, and three of them — founder control, mission protection and commercial compatibility — are in acknowledged tension with each other** (**[S-ST: F-11 item 12]**: *"this *reduces* founder flexibility, which the architecture currently protects … **That trade-off is real and must be decided deliberately**"*; **[S-A: A-13 §12.6]**: *"the trade-off is stated openly and is **a decision, not an analysis**"*).

## XX.2 The matrix — DM-01 … DM-12

**Option groups:** **A/B/C** = Section 8 company · society · public charitable trust | **D** = company / business entity | **E/F/G** = LLP · partnership · proprietorship | **H** = hybrid / two-entity | **I** = parent + separate operating entity | **J** = other (incl. unincorporated network, internal division, federation, separate IP holder, separate fund custodian)

| DM | Dimension | A / B / C | D | E / F / G | H | I | J |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **DM-01** | **Legal complexity** | *To be determined by counsel:* which of the twenty §V.2 purposes the objects may reach; whether commercial objects may be included; whether protection objects may be included | *To be determined by counsel:* whether social-service and donation activity may be carried by the same person, given **[S: App. A §3.2]**: *"Social service and donations may need separate treatment"* | **`UNKNOWN`** — no corpus statement | *To be determined by counsel:* the count of persons, the instruments between them, and the **related-party** formalities **[S-ST: R4 (ii)]** | *To be determined by counsel:* **and by disclosure of UE-24** — Krytos's own form and governing documents | *To be determined by counsel* — for **J-1** specifically, **[S-C: HV-6]**: whether an unincorporated network may issue credentials, hold records, publish a directory, accept guardian consent |
| **DM-02** | **Tax consequences** | **Part XV** in full: exemption availability; **application-of-income**; the **political-activity** condition and cancellation risk **[X]**; whether commercial receipts are inside or outside the measured income | **Part XV**: taxation of venture revenue; GST; whether exemption is available at all | **`UNKNOWN`** | Which person's income is measured; **transfer pricing** on every inter-entity flow **[S-ST: R4]**; the priority-5 fee and priority-7 contribution crossing a boundary | Whether the parent's activity affects the operating entity's position; characterisation of a parent advance (**[S-B: E-03]**: *"founder capital / Krytos / validated donation / accrued-and-waived fee"*) | Per the form counsel identifies |
| **DM-03** | **Governance clarity** | What governing body exists, how constituted, how removed, what quorum and majority — **[S-D: LV-8]** *"board composition"*; **UE-25** | As A/B/C | **`UNKNOWN`** | **Two governance systems or one shared body; and who decides a reserved matter that affects both** — **GVQ-04, GVQ-11** | Whether the parent's governance overrides the operating entity's — **[S-A: A-14 S5]**: *"Krytos's governing documents take effect in parallel [V]"* | For **J-3** federation: *"a common constitution, standards body and brand licence"* **[S-ST: §5 Scenario D]** |
| **DM-04** | **Founder control** | Whether a founder may be outvoted, removed or bound; what protective provisions exist — **[S-A: A-V1]**; **[S-ST: F-11 item 2]**: *"no golden-share/protective-vote mechanism"* exists today | As A/B/C — **and the corpus notes `shareholder` only once, in the federation option** | **`UNKNOWN`** — **but see [S-A: §11.2] and [S-ST: §11.1]: concentration is already rated `Absolute` for entity, brand, mission, IP and memory** | Who holds the licensor, and whether that **increases** or **dilutes** control — **[S-ST: §5 Scenario D]**: federation *"dilutes single-owner control … **reduces founder flexibility — a deliberate trade**"* | *"Ownership sits with Krytos"* **[S-A: §11.2]** — **and Krytos's control follows its own succession, "outside this architecture entirely"** | Per the form counsel identifies |
| **DM-05** | **Mission protection** | Whether a **mission lock** is enforceable in the form — **[S-A: §11.3 item 12]**; **[S-ST: Annexure B]** *"enforceability of a mission lock"*; the four ascending instruments at **[S-ST: F-11 item 7]** | As A/B/C | **`UNKNOWN`** | Whether the mission is protected **in the person that holds it** — **[S-ST: R4 (iii)]**: *"a holding structure with **the mission held separately** from both"* | Whether the parent's ownership is itself the protection or the exposure — **[S-ST: F-11]**: *"nothing in the architecture prevents redirection of the institution"* | For **J-4/J-5**: whether a separate IP holder or fund custodian protects the mission better than an operating entity does |
| **DM-06** | **Commercial compatibility** | **[S: App. A §3.2]**: *"**may not fit commercial waterfall without separate structure**"* → **FC-04 = SS** | **[S: App. A §3.2]**: *"**suitable for commercial ventures**"* → **FC-04 = PC** | **`UNKNOWN`** | *"**Separates commercial ventures from social programmes while preserving mission funding links**"* → **FC-04 = PC**, with *"**more complex accounting, governance and conflict-of-interest controls**"* | Whether the operating entity or a subsidiary carries commerce — **[S: App. A §3.2 row 2]** *"Strong candidate for business network"* | Per the form counsel identifies |
| **DM-07** | **Worker classification risk** | **Identical across all options and unresolved**: **LDR-U09**; **A-B1**; *"A member is paid in a way that creates an **unrecorded statutory liability**"* — and **[S-A: A-10 L7]**: liability *"**changes with member classification**"* | — | — | **A person may work for both entities — WCQ-20: one employer or two?** | — | For **J-1**, whether an unincorporated network may engage anyone at all |
| **DM-08** | **Banking complexity** | **A-N16 across all options**: minimum signatories, continuity rule, custody instruments for reserves and the restricted fund — **A-V10**; and **UE-32**: whether a sub-national layer may hold a balance | — | — | **Two mandates, or one across two persons; and whether the continuity rule must hold in each** | Whether the parent's mandate governs | For **J-5**, a custodian's own mandate |
| **DM-09** | **IP handling** | **A-N12 / A-N13 across all options**; the four candidate positions at **[S-E: N-E-17]** — *Founder/Krytos-owned / entity-owned / licensed / held in trust* — and *"**Any choice has succession and dissolution consequences**"* | — | **`UNKNOWN`** | **A brand licence between entities is mandatory** **[S-ST: R4 (ii), Annexure B]** — with terms, scope, duration, termination and post-termination position | *"**Whoever owns Krytos owns the operating system of the institution; a licensor could withdraw it**"* **[S-A: §11.2]** | For **J-4**, a separate holder — **⟨new — v1.3-G⟩** |
| **DM-10** | **Dissolution risk** | **[S: Const. Art.19]** across all options — the article is a pointer to *"legal structure, donor restrictions, contracts and statutory obligations"* and nothing more; **a reserved matter** with **no determined holder** | — | **`UNKNOWN`** | **Two dissolutions, possibly not simultaneous — DS-01…DS-09** | **The parent survives the operating entity by design** | For **J-5**, what happens to a restricted fund on dissolution of the operator |
| **DM-11** | **Professional validation needed** | **V-01 ◆, V-03, V-04 ◆, V-05 ◆, V-06, V-07 ◆, V-08 ◆, V-09, V-10, V-11, V-02 ◆, V-13, V-16, V-17** — i.e. **the full pre-pilot set of eleven validations across eight categories** (**[S-F: III.3, BLK-D-07]**) | Same set | Same set, **plus** the threshold question of availability | **The same set, applied to each person, plus related-party and transfer-pricing validation** | **The same set, plus validation of the parent** | Per the form |
| **DM-12** | **Human decision required** | **Ten decisions, and no document can make any of them:** UE-01 structure · UE-02 classification · UE-03 mandate · UE-07 fiduciary · UE-09/UE-10 custody · UE-05/UE-06 brand and IP · UE-14 apex authority · UE-15 deadlock · UE-16/UE-17 succession · UE-21 dissolution posture. **Four of them have no disinterested decider today** (**[S-F: Rule F-10], [S-F: XXIV.2], [S-F: XXV.5]**) | Same | Same | Same, **plus** which pillar goes where, and who holds the licensor | Same, **plus UE-24**: the founder must disclose Krytos's own position | Same, **plus** whether the option is available at all |

**[A]** **The dashes in DM-07, DM-08, DM-09, DM-10, DM-11 and DM-12 are not omissions.** They record the finding that **those six dimensions do not differentiate between options in any way the corpus states** — classification risk, banking complexity, IP handling, dissolution risk, validation load and human-decision load are **the same problem under every form**, differing only in the instrument that answers them. **Four dimensions genuinely differentiate: DM-02 tax, DM-04 founder control, DM-05 mission protection and DM-06 commercial compatibility — and those four are exactly the four that [S-ST: R4] identifies as the reason "a single entity will serve none of them well."**

## XX.3 The trade-offs the matrix cannot resolve

**[A]** Four tensions are recorded in the corpus and cannot be closed by analysis. **Each is a human decision.**

| # | Tension | Source |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Founder flexibility versus mission protection.** *"This amendment **reduces founder flexibility by design**"* **[S-A: A-13 §12.6]**; *"this *reduces* founder flexibility, which the architecture currently protects **[S: Ch.4 'broad executive flexibility']** … **That trade-off is real and must be decided deliberately**"* **[S-ST: F-11 item 12]** | **UE-31** |
| **2** | **Separation versus simplicity.** Separating gives *"cleaner contracts, accounts and liability separation"* and *"separates commercial ventures from social programmes"*; it costs *"more complex accounting, governance and conflict-of-interest controls"*, *"administrative burden, related-party scrutiny"*, and risks *"a public perception of 'charity and business being mixed' whichever way it is done; **donor confusion**"* | **OPT-H** |
| **3** | **Deciding now versus restructuring later.** *"single entity now, restructure later — **cheap now, expensive or impossible later once contracts and reputation attach to one name**"* **[S-ST: R4 (i)]** — against **[S-A: A-I6]** and **[S-F: BLK-F-15]**, which place ownership questions in the post-pilot class | **GC-05** |
| **4** | **An `APPROVED` waterfall versus a form that may not carry it.** Priorities 4 and 8 are `APPROVED` and a **reserved matter**; the nonprofit family's source-recorded risk is *"restrictions on profit distribution and founder upside"* | **GC-01** |

---

# PART XXI — MINIMUM DECISION SET

## XXI.1 Method

**[P]** For each of ten items, three states are specified:

- **`UNRESOLVED` (today)** — the current state, with its source ID and the corpus's own statement of why it is open.
- **`DECIDABLE`** — the **minimum inputs** that would make the decision possible. **Not the answer: the preconditions of an answer.**
- **`DECIDED`** — the **evidence** that would mark the decision made, taken from **[S-F: Part XXII]**'s own *"Evidence required"* column wherever the register supplies one, so that the standard is the corpus's and not this paper's.

**[P] Rule G-47 (This Part Does Not Decide Anything).** It specifies **what would have to be true** for a decision to be possible and for it to be recorded. **The transition is performed by an authorised human decision-maker, on professional advice, and is evidenced by a document.**

## XXI.2 MD-01 — `LDR-U08` LEGAL ENTITY STRUCTURE UNDER KRYTOS

| State | Content |
|---|---|

## XXI.5 The decision sequence, as the corpus already orders it

**[S]** **[S-F: VII.2]** master critical path, reproduced without alteration:

```
STEP 1   LDR-U08  DECIDE THE LEGAL ENTITY STRUCTURE
            |      (the entity-structure decision paper for counsel -
            |       the single next artifact, one-page answer item 19)   <-- THIS INSTRUMENT
            v
STEP 2   V-01 + V-04 + V-05  COUNSEL VALIDATES FORM, TAX AND CLASSIFICATION
            |      (LDR-U09 resolved as a consequence)
            v
STEP 3   A-N16  BANK MANDATE + TWO SIGNATORIES   [A-B9]
            v
STEP 4   PRIORITY-1 WORKER COMPENSATION CAN NOW LAWFULLY BE PAID
            |      <-- the first moment the economic model is real
            v
STEP 5   A-N14 + A-N26 + V-07  DATA FIDUCIARY, RETENTION, PRIVACY MINIMUM  [A-B7]
            v
STEP 6   A-N10 + V-06  INSURANCE AND LIABILITY ANSWERS  [A-B2]
            v
STEP 7   V-09 + V-08  INTERVENTION BOUNDARY + CERTIFIED SAFEGUARDING  [A-B3, A-B6]
            v
STEP 8   A-N1..A-N9, A-N11  NEUTRALITY CODE + PROTECTION SET ADOPTED  [A-B4, A-B5, A-B8]
            v
STEP 9   BLK-C-07 + BLK-B-08/09  COMMS POLICY + ID CARD DESIGN  [[S: Ch.31] blockers]
            v
STEP 10  BLK-A-09 + BLK-A-10  n_min SET + MVLS ADOPTED
            v
STEP 11  BLK-C-01..C-11 BUILT
            v
STEP 12  BLK-D-01..D-14 FUNDED
            v
STEP 13  GATE P0 (Part VIII)  ->  PILOT AUTHORISED
```

**[A]** **MD-01 is STEP 1. MD-02 is inside STEP 2. MD-03 is STEP 3. MD-04 is inside STEP 5. MD-05 and MD-06 are prerequisites of STEP 12's reserve and float funding. MD-07 and MD-08 are prerequisites of STEP 9's ID-card design and of GATE E7. MD-09 and MD-10 are not on the master path at all — [S-F: VIII.2] confirms the pilot may proceed with the interim procedure — but they are prerequisites of every reserved matter, and [S-F: CP-1] makes them the governance path.**

**[P] Rule G-48 (The Sequence Is Not Negotiable By This Instrument).** **[S-F: Rule F-8]**: *"**Validation Precedes Build.** Where an item requires both validation and construction, validation comes first."* **STEP 1 cannot be skipped, reordered or satisfied by a design act.**

# PART XXII — PILOT CONSEQUENCE

## XXII.1 Method

**[P]** The brief requires five classes. **Every activity in the corpus's pilot design is assigned to exactly one**, with its source anchor. **Rule G-8 governs class 2: nothing here permits informal operation.**

**[S]** The pilot the corpus defines: **one mohalla team, in one locality, running two or three time-costed service activities from categories 1–3 and one approved venture with one real customer, on paper, with no technology, no mark, no national channel, no leaderboard, no composition data and no promotion** — **[S-B: §40.2, §40.5]**; **[S-C: §57]**; **[S-D: Rule D-130]**; **[S-E: Rule E-222]**; **[S-F: VIII.2]**.

## XXII.2 Class 1 — CANNOT LEGALLY OR OPERATIONALLY PROCEED

| PQ | Activity | Why it cannot proceed | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| **PQ-01** | **Any payment to a worker, member, specialist, trainer or mentor** | *"**Any payment to a worker** — A-B1 entity and classification. **An unlawful payment creates liability, not data**"* | **[S-F: XXV.7]** |
| **PQ-02** | **Any external payment of any kind — supplier, landlord, professional, insurer** | **A-N16**: *"**Nothing — this blocks the first external payment**"* | **[S-F: IV.2]** |
| **PQ-03** | **Opening or operating a bank account** | **P0-C** FAIL condition: *"**No mandate**"*; no legal person exists to hold one | **[S-F: P0-C; BLK-C-06]** |
| **PQ-04** | **Signing a customer contract, an MOU, a partnership or a processor agreement** | **[S: App. A §31]**: contract templates depend on *"Entity structure"* and are `Not started` / `Blocker`; **UE-26** | **[S: Ch.31]**; **[S-F: BLK-A-01]** |
| **PQ-05** | **Any protection or first-response activity, and any operational training for it** | *"**No written confirmation, no activation.** Protection activity may not be activated, **trained for operationally**, publicised, or represented to any external party until the relevant items in §9.1 are answered in writing"* | **[S-A: A-10 §9.2]**; **[S-F: XXV.7]** |
| **PQ-06** | **Publishing the Public Protection Statement (A-07)** | *"may not be published before the answers to **L2, L3, L4, L8 and L12** exist. Publishing a description of protective activity without cover converts an internal doctrine into a **public representation**"* | **[S-A: A-10 §9.2 rule 2]** |
| **PQ-07** | **Any activity involving a minor or a vulnerable adult** | *"**A-B6 certified safeguarding. Harm to a child is irreversible**"*; **[S-C: HD-19]**: *"Obtain written legal advice **before** any minor participates"* | **[S-F: XXV.7]**; **[S-A: §23.2 A-B6]** |
| **PQ-08** | **Any systematic collection of personal data, and publication of the privacy notice** | *"**Over-retention is a breach from day one**"*; the notice must name a fiduciary that does not exist; **P0-H** FAIL: *"no retention schedule"* | **[S-F: XXV.7, P0-H; chain 6]** |

A misreading cannot be un-read**"*; **[S-A: §3.1 A.3 row 6]** calls election-adjacent distribution *"**Highest-consequence operational question in this code**"* | **[S-F: XXV.7]**; **[S-A: A-B4]** |

## XXII.3 Class 2 — CAN PROCEED ONLY AS AN INFORMAL / NON-ORGANISATIONAL EXPERIMENT, IF COUNSEL PERMITS

> **[P] Rule G-8 restated: this class is a description of a possibility that counsel must evaluate. It is NOT authorisation, NOT advice and NOT consent. This instrument grants no permission to operate informally.**

| PQ | Activity | The question counsel must answer | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| **PQ-21** | **A neighbourhood group of known people doing time-costed service (categories 1–3, walkable, using existing or donated materials) with no organisational representation, no collection of personal data, no payment, no mark and no public claim** | **[S-C: HV-6]**, verbatim: *"**Whether an unincorporated network may issue credentials, hold records, publish a directory, or accept guardian consent**"* — and, by extension, whether it may do any of the above without creating a duty, a representation or a liability | **[S-C: HV-6]**; **[S-B: §33.3]** categories 1–3 *"continue at ₹0 revenue"* |
| **PQ-22** | **A single team using the name locally** | **[S-E: BR-9]**: use is *"by **recorded permission** under the interim rule"* — **and [S-F: XXIV.2] records that "its grantor is undetermined" (GC-06).** Counsel must say whether a permission granted under **[S: Ch.4]** ownership authority, *"subject to the final legal structure"*, is sufficient | **[S-E: BR-9, Rule E-60 §12.4]**; **[S-F: VIII.2]** |
| **PQ-23** | **Designing, writing, thinking, training and rehearsing — on paper** | **[S-F: LDR-U08 "Does NOT block"]**: *"**Nothing.** Writing, thinking, designing and training may continue."* **Counsel's question is narrower: whether any of it creates a representation, a duty of care, an employment expectation or a data obligation** | **[S-F: IV.2]** |
| **PQ-24** | **A tabletop continuity exercise and a tabletop reserved-matter procedure** | **[S-F: BLK-F-09]**: *"a tabletop version can run earlier"*; **[S-F: PAK-01]** requires *"one tabletop use"* of Rule D-113. **Counsel's question: whether acting under an unvalidated interim procedure creates any exposure** — **[S-D: LV-9]** | **[S-F: PAK-01, BLK-F-09]** |
| **PQ-25** | **A name-availability search** | **[S-F: SR-14]**: *"**Search now — cheap, and irreversible if lost**."* **Counsel's question: in whose name the search and any subsequent application should be made** | **[S-F: SR-14]** |
| **PQ-26** | **Commissioning counsel and receiving advice** | **Counsel's question: whether a founder or Krytos may instruct and pay counsel before the entity exists, and what that creates — a founder claim, a related-party transaction, or a gift to a future entity** (**UE-12**; **[S-B: EV-4]** *"Authority to advance funds"*) | **[S-B: E-03]**; **[S-F: BLK-D-07]** |

## XXII.7 The pilot consequence, stated in one paragraph

**[A]** **The pilot the corpus defines is small enough that almost none of its *design* is blocked, and large enough that almost all of its *operation* is.** A one-team, no-technology, no-mark, no-payment, no-data-collection design exercise can proceed today under class 3 and class 4 — **and it is not a pilot.** The moment the pilot does what **[S-B: §40.2]** requires it to do — **run one service activity and one venture project simultaneously, with real people, real expenses and real money** — it enters class 1 at PQ-01, PQ-02, PQ-03, PQ-04 and PQ-08, and it cannot be authorised. **[S-F: XXV.8]** states the position exactly: *"The pilot is fully defined and could be described in a page. **It cannot be *authorised*, because the organisation has no legal person, cannot lawfully pay the worker whose compensation is priority one**, has no insurance answer, no certified safeguarding, no privacy notice, no comms policy, no ID card design, no public record, no complaint route, no correction log and no refusal log."* **And [S-F: XIV.3 Finding 2] supplies the sentence this Part exists to preserve:** *"the architecture's first and most absolute financial obligation is one the organisation **cannot lawfully perform**. This is the single hardest fact in this register."*

**[P] Rule G-49 (A Pilot Without An Entity Is Not A Smaller Pilot — It Is A Different Activity).** Removing payment, contracts, data collection and representation from the pilot removes **P-01 through P-15**'s subject. **[S-F: chain 4]**: *"there is no legal person to incur cost, hold an account, employ anyone or be audited; **the pilot's economic measurements have no subject**."* **An informal experiment under class 2 produces evidence about people, not about the architecture.**

---

# PART XXIII — DECISION PAPER FOR HUMAN APPROVAL

> **[P]** This Part is written to be lifted out and placed in front of an authorised human decision-maker and of qualified Indian professionals **as a single document.** It contains **no entity selection.** It follows the brief's nine headings exactly.

## XXIII.1 DECISION REQUIRED

**One decision, and it is not a technical one.**

> **Decide the legal structure of Sewak Sathi Sanstha under Krytos — `LDR-U08` / `A-B1` / `SR-01` — by commissioning qualified Indian corporate, tax and labour counsel as a single engagement, on the option set at Part III, and then making the choice.**

**What is being asked of the decision-maker:**

| # | Ask |
|---|---|
| **1** | **Authorise the engagement.** Instruct corporate/entity counsel (**V-01 / A-V1**), a tax advisor (**V-04 / A-V2**) and labour counsel (**V-05 / A-V3**) — **as one engagement**, per **[S-F: XXV.4 Action 1]** — with the briefs at Part XIX |
| **2** | **Disclose `UE-24`.** Supply counsel with Krytos's own legal form, constitutive documents, objects and ownership. **The decision is "legal structure *under Krytos*"; the parent term is currently unstated** |
| **3** | **Decide `GC-01` as a question, not an answer.** Tell counsel whether waterfall **priority 4 (founder compensation)** and **priority 8 (residual surplus / founder upside)** are to be preserved in the chosen form, or referred as a reserved matter. **Both are `APPROVED ARCHITECTURE`; the priority order is a reserved matter; and no instrument in the corpus reconciles them with the nonprofit risk note** |
| **4** | **Fund the engagement.** `UE-12` / **[S-B: E-03]** is `UNRESOLVED — no source instrument`. **[S-F: BLK-D-07]** calls professional fees *"the single most overlooked pre-pilot cost in the corpus."* **Name the source before Phase 1 spend** |
| **5** | **Do not decide anything else first.** **[S-ST: R4]**: *"This is the Phase 0 decision on which most others depend, and **it should be made first**."* **[S-F: XXV.8]**: *"**Nothing else should be built until it exists**"* — i.e. until this paper exists and has been used |
| **6** | **Record the decision.** **[S-F: SR-01]** evidence: *"**A written decision plus counsel opinion**."* **[S-F: BLK-C-11]** requires the decision register to be operating: *"≥1 decision record with **reason, decider and date**"* |

**What is NOT being asked:** to choose a structure today · to accept any option in Part III · to treat any `PROPOSED` instrument as adopted · to authorise any payment, contract, collection, claim, mark or protection activity · to permit informal operation · to accept any tax, insurance, privacy or employment position stated in this paper, because **none is stated.**

## XXIII.2 WHY IT MATTERS

**[A]** Six reasons, each traceable to a source statement and none of them a prediction.

| # | Reason | Source |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **The organisation's first and most absolute financial obligation cannot lawfully be discharged.** *"Priority 1 is a legal obligation the organisation cannot currently discharge … the architecture's first and most absolute financial obligation is one the organisation cannot lawfully perform. **This is the single hardest fact in this register**"* | **[S-F: XIV.3 Finding 2]** |
| **2** | **Eleven of twenty-eight dependency chains root here, and it is STEP 1 of the thirteen-step master critical path.** Chains 4–7 all originate at `LDR-U08`: economic measurement, classification and priority-1 payment, the data fiduciary and breach notification, insurance and protection activity | **[S-F: Part VI, Part VII]** |
| **3** | **Nothing economic can be measured, so nothing economic can be decided.** *"There is no legal person to incur cost, hold an account, employ anyone or be audited; **the pilot's economic measurements have no subject**."* The economic blueprint was **never attached** (`LDR-U17`), and every magnitude is a variable | **[S-F: chain 4]**; **[S: App. A §1.1]** |
| **4** | **Every long-duration promise rests on a continuity assumption the architecture has not instrumented.** *"Members are asked to commit years; partners are asked to sign MOUs; beneficiaries are asked to trust records about themselves; professionals are asked to volunteer expertise; **authorities are asked to recognise the body.** Each of those counterparties will, at some point, ask **'what happens if Vasu is not there?'** — and v1.2 has no answer to give them"* | **[S-A: §11.4]** |
| **5** | **Institutional counterparties require a structure, not an intention.** *"Legal entity clarity and honest status disclosure … **Corporates, CSR teams, government counterparts, donors and regulators trust structures, not intentions**"*; and without continuity, *"Institutional investors, partners, government counterparts and serious professionals **decline to engage** (they do due diligence on continuity)"* | **[S-ST: §14.2 L4; F-11 item 4]** |
| **6** | **The alternative to deciding is not standing still — it is deciding by default.** *"Whoever owns Krytos owns the operating system of the institution"*; brand, IP, mission amendment, data and (by default) the entity all sit in one pair of hands; and *"a national institution with lakhs of members and real community trust [could be] **legally repurposed or dissolved by a decision no member can contest**"* | **[S-A: §11.2]**; **[S-ST: F-11 item 11]** |

## XXIII.3 EXISTING ARCHITECTURE CONSTRAINTS

**[P]** Fourteen constraints the chosen structure must satisfy. **Each is `APPROVED ARCHITECTURE`, a source-preservation rule, or an express corpus requirement — not a preference of this paper.**

| # | Constraint | Source | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **The eight-priority waterfall and its order** — worker compensation first, statutory dues second, and the order itself is the control | **[S: Ch.18; App. A §14.2]**; **[S-D: LDR-C16]** *"APPROVED — THE CONTROL"* | `APPROVED` + **reserved matter** |
| **2** | **Worker compensation is never suspended** by a dispute, a process, a gate or a score | **[S-D: INV-2]** | Invariant |
| **3** | **Four segregated finance hands** — receive, approve, record, review/audit never in one person; **resolved by routing, never by merging** | **[S: Ch.18; App. A §20.3]**; **[S-D: Rule D-103, C-D-06]** | `APPROVED` |
| **4** | **No off-book cash**; Funds Manager cannot self-approve, release reserves without approvals, change the formula, or audit own work | **[S: App. A §20.4]** | `APPROVED` |
| **5** | **The social-impact fund is restricted** — not ordinary salaries, not venture bailouts, not political or religious activity, not a discretionary pool; and its restrictions survive unless the founder amends them **after professional validation** | **[S: Ch.20; App. A §21.2]** | `APPROVED` |
| **6** | **Money cannot move laterally between teams**; reserves are not team property | **[S-B: §21.4]** | **[C]** derived from source |
| **7** | **The business component remains central** — *"The organisation is not converted into a conventional NGO-only model"*; *"not merely a donation-funded charity"* | **[S: App. A §1.3, §1]** | **Source-preservation rule — non-negotiable** |
| **8** | **A local team is not an entity and not a franchise** | **[S-B: §3.2, §3.4]** | **[C]** |
| **9** | **Ownership is Krytos's under the founder/CEO, subject to the final legal structure**; the Council is not owner above the CEO unless a legal charter says so | **[S: Const. Art.3; Ch.4]** | `APPROVED` — **and expressly conditional** |
| **10** | **Entity structure is a reserved matter**, and so are mission/name/brand change, asset/reserve/social-fund transfer, dissolution/merger/restructuring, permanent-CEO appointment, the DO-NOT list, and the waterfall's priority order | **[S-A: A-14 §13.3]**; **[S-D: LDR-C26]** | `PROPOSED` — **holder undetermined** |
| **11** | **Political and religious non-alignment** — nine prohibitions at all levels, plus the equal-treatment principle | **[S-A: A-01 N1–N9, A-05]** | `PROPOSED` for adoption; **A-B4 BLOCKER** |
| **12** | **Protection is defensive, de-escalatory, authority-linked; no weapons; no uniform, insignia or worn mark; nine DO-NOTs** | **[S: Const. Art.9; Ch.16]**; **[S-A: A-06 V1–V3, A-08]** | `APPROVED` doctrine; `PROPOSED` instruments |
| **13** | **AI recommends; humans decide** — AI may not make consequential HR, wage, legal, safeguarding, safety, major-fund or leadership decisions | **[S: Const. Art.12]** | `APPROVED` |
| **14** | **Dissolution, merger, restructuring, asset transfer, reserve use and social-impact fund handling must follow legal structure, donor restrictions, contracts and statutory obligations** | **[S: Const. Art.19]** | `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION` |

**[A]** **Constraints 1 and 7 pull in opposite directions, and that is GC-01.** Constraint 1 makes founder compensation and founder upside part of an `APPROVED`, reserved money sequence. Constraint 7 forbids an NGO-only model. **[S: App. A §3.2]** records that the nonprofit family carries *"restrictions on profit distribution and founder upside"* and *"may not fit commercial waterfall without separate structure."* **The decision-maker must be told this before choosing, not after.**

**The four-column source table is reproduced in full at §III.3 and is the evidentiary basis for Part IV.**

## XXIII.5 KEY TRADE-OFFS

**[A]** Seven trade-offs, each stated with both sides and each sourced. **None is resolved.**

| # | Trade-off | One side | The other side | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Single entity versus separation** | *"Fast initial control; aligned with founder ownership; simpler brand integration"*; single governance; single set of accounts | *"Cleaner contracts, accounts and liability separation"*; *"separates commercial ventures from social programmes"* — against *"more complex accounting, governance and **conflict-of-interest controls**"*, *"administrative burden, **related-party scrutiny** … **donor confusion**"* | **[S: App. A §3.2]**; **[S-ST: R4]** |
| **2** | **Decide now versus restructure later** | Deciding now costs professional fees the organisation has no instrument to pay (**UE-12**) | *"single entity now, restructure later — **cheap now, expensive or impossible later once contracts and reputation attach to one name**"* | **[S-ST: R4 (i)]** |
| **3** | **Founder flexibility versus mission protection** | *"Ownership and **broad executive flexibility** within final legal structure"* is `APPROVED`; concentration is *"a legitimate design for a founder-led organisation and v1.2 says so honestly"* | *"This amendment **reduces founder flexibility by design**"*; *"**That trade-off is real and must be decided deliberately**"*; and without a lock, *"nothing in the architecture prevents redirection of the institution"* | **[S: Ch.4]**; **[S-A: §11.4, A-13 §12.6]**; **[S-ST: F-11]** |
| **4** | **Charitable credibility versus commercial capacity** | *"Better mission credibility and **eligibility for certain grants/donations**"* | *"Restrictions on **profit distribution and founder upside**; **may not fit commercial waterfall without separate structure**"* — against constraint 7: *"the business component remains central"* | **[S: App. A §3.2]**; **[S: App. A §1.3]** |
| **5** | **Concentration versus continuity** | One decider is fast, personal and accountable — *"that is a founder's legitimate advantage"* | *"**the institution's identity and its legal continuity are the same object, held by one person … there is no way to defend the institution by separating them**"*; and *"the entity's control follows Krytos's own succession, **which is outside this architecture entirely**"* | **[S-A: §11.4]**; **[S-ST: §11.1]**; **[S-A: §11.2]** |
| **6** | **Low visibility versus structural trust** | **Rule E-65** word-only identification, **Rule E-58** parent subordination, no uniform, no mark, *"under-claiming is a trust strategy"* — all reduce adjacency and misclassification risk | *"**Corporates, CSR teams, government counterparts, donors and regulators trust structures, not intentions**"*; **MC-8** *"Volunteer WhatsApp club"* is a named misclassification whose remedy is *"publish the entity status accurately, **including what is pending**"* | **[S-E: Rules E-58, E-59, E-65, MC-8]**; **[S-ST: §14.2 L4]** |
| **7** | **Answering the entity question versus answering the governance question** | `LDR-U08` **can** be decided — it is not on **[S-F: XXV.5]**'s list of items *"genuinely unresolvable without authorised human decisions"* | `LDR-U01` **cannot** be decided by any body, because *"the body that would decide is itself partly constituted by them"* — and **deciding `LDR-U08` will create the body that `LDR-U01` is about** | **[S-F: XXV.5, Rule F-10]** |

## XXIII.6 PROFESSIONAL ADVICE REQUIRED

**[P]** Nine briefs at Part XIX; **eleven pre-pilot validations across eight categories** per **[S-F: BLK-D-07]**; **77 items across 17 categories** in total per **[S-F: Part III]**. **The pre-pilot set:**

| # | Validation | Category | Blocker | Consequence if skipped *(verbatim)* |
|---|---|---|---|---|

| 2 | Tax position: 12A application-of-income and political-activity conditions; 80G; FCRA eligibility and timing | **V-04** | **A-B1** | *"Exemption exposure; a political-activity finding could cancel exemption **[X][V]**"* |
| 3 | Worker classification; minimum-wage and statutory consequences | **V-05** | **A-B1** | *"Priority-1 payment may be unlawful; statutory liability"* |
| 4 | Insurance and liability: written answers to **L1–L15** | **V-06** | **A-B2** | *"**Uninsured personal injury. Irreversible**"* |
| 5 | The intervention boundary; private-person arrest and handover; duty to assist; Good-Samaritan; mandatory reporting | **V-09, V-17** | **A-B3** | *"Unlawful intervention; member criminal exposure"* |
| 6 | Political-activity restrictions attaching to the chosen form; the §3.1 A.3 questions | **V-01, V-04, V-17** | **A-B4** | *"Tax-exempt exposure; MC-1"* |
| 7 | Certified safeguarding; two-adult rule; reporting protocols; consent validity | **V-08** | **A-B6** | *"**Harm to a child. Irreversible**"* |
| 8 | Privacy: lawful basis, notice, purpose limitation, retention schedule, breach runbook, named officer, fiduciary status | **V-07** | **A-B7** | *"Statutory exposure — **[X]** DPDP penalties up to ₹200 crore for failure to notify, ~₹250 crore for inadequate safeguards **[V]**"* |
| 9 | Bank mandate continuity and minimum signatories; **custody instruments for reserves** | **V-01, V-10** | **A-B9** | *"Worker compensation cannot be paid during an apex vacancy"* |
| 10 | Non-police ID card design | **V-11, V-17** | **[S: Ch.31]** | *"Resemblance to an official credential; MC-3"* |
| 11 | The comms policy, including public-statement rules and misinformation-correction procedure | **V-16** | **[S: Ch.31]** | *"Unauthorised statements; no correction discipline"* |

**[A]** **Items 1, 2, 3 and 9 are the entity decision's own validation set. Items 4–8 and 10–11 follow from it.** **[S-F: Rule F-8]**: *"Validation Precedes Build."*

## XXIII.7 UNRESOLVED QUESTIONS

**[P]** Thirty-two at Part II. **The ten that must be decided by a human being, and the four that no document can decide:**

| Ten human decisions (Part XXI) | Four that no document can decide |
|---|---|
| **MD-01** `LDR-U08` structure · **MD-02** `LDR-U09` classification · **MD-03** `A-N16` mandate · **MD-04** `A-N14` fiduciary · **MD-05** `A-N15` reserve custody · **MD-06** `A-N15` social-fund custody · **MD-07** `A-N12` brand · **MD-08** `A-N13` IP · **MD-09** `LDR-U01` apex authority · **MD-10** `A-N19` deadlock | **[S-F: XXV.5]** — `LDR-U01` (*"settling it may reduce the owner's own authority"*) · `LDR-U19` successor (*"no instrument may name a successor"*) · `A-N12/A-N13` (*"a property question about the founder's own assets"*) · `LDR-U16` founder compensation (*"the decider is the beneficiary"*) |

**Plus four inputs this paper requires and does not have:** **UE-24** Krytos's own form and documents · **GC-01** the waterfall/nonprofit conflict · **§I.3** the availability of OPT-D…OPT-G · **`LDR-U17`** the economic blueprint, without which `C_comp` cannot be weighed.

## XXIII.8 CONSEQUENCES OF DELAY

**[A]** Each consequence below is a source statement about what happens if an item stays open. **None is a forecast, and none is a threat.**

| If delayed | Consequence, as the corpus states it | Source |
|---|---|---|
| **`LDR-U08`** | *"**Priority-1 worker compensation cannot lawfully be paid. The pilot cannot pay anyone. Nothing economic can be measured**"* | **[S-F: IV.2]** |
| **`LDR-U09`** | *"**A member is paid in a way that creates an unrecorded statutory liability**"* | **[S-F: IV.2]** |
| **`A-N16`** | *"**Priority 1 fails on one person's absence. The waterfall's first obligation becomes unpayable**"* | **[S-A: §22 N16]** |
| **`A-N14` / `A-N26`** | *"Data is kept indefinitely with no rule, or destroyed while still required as evidence"*; *"Regulator has no contactable person; duties unassigned"* | **[S-A: §22]** |
| **`A-N15`** | *"Restricted purpose survives in policy but in no instrument"*; *"**a successor could redefine 'restricted'**"* | **[S-A: §22 N15, §11.2]** |
| **`A-N12` / `A-N13`** | *"Teams hold no documented right to the name; **exit and dissolution become chaotic**"*; *"**the institution's operating system could be withdrawn by a licensor**"*; *"the organisation may build a valuable national identity **on an asset it does not documentedly own**"* | **[S-A: §22]**; **[S-E: §42.5]** |
| **`LDR-U01`** | *"**A reserved matter arises and nobody is authorised to decide it**"*; *"A reserved matter that nobody can decide is **not protected; it is frozen**"*; *"Deadlock settled by **power rather than procedure**"* | **[S-F: IV.2]**; **[S-D: Part 34]** |
| **`LDR-U19` / `A-N17`** | *"**Apex vacancy becomes an ending**"*; *"the institution and the person are the same object"* | **[S-F: IV.2]**; **[S-ST: §11.1]** |
| **`A-N10`** | *"Uncovered injury; unsupported member; unanswerable regulator"*; *"**You cannot retro-insure a known event**"* | **[S-A: §22 N10, §23.2 A-B2]** |
| **`A-N38`** | *"**164 amendments arrive at once and nothing is implemented**"* | **[S-F: BLK-A-11]** |
| **`LDR-U16`** | *"Either the founder is paid without a rule, or is not paid and the deficit is invisible — **both damage the published accounts**"* | **[S-F: IV.2]** |
| **The whole set** | *"DOCUMENTS COMPLETE → 'the architecture is comprehensive' → 'therefore we are ready' → PILOT AUTHORISED → **first payment cannot lawfully be made** … → **PILOT HALTS, and the halt is attributed to 'implementation problems' rather than to nine decisions that were never made**"* | **[S-F: VIII.3]** |
| **And the meta-risk** | *"A body of work this thorough creates a specific and unusual hazard: **it makes the organisation feel ready** … seriousness is routinely mistaken for readiness. **Part XVIII exists because that mistake is the most likely way this work is wasted**"* | **[S-F: XXV.8]** |

**[A]** **The cost of delay is not only deferred capability.** **[S-ST: R4 (i)]** records that deferral may make the eventual restructuring *"**impossible** … once contracts and reputation attach to one name"* — and **[S-A: §11.2]** records that the contracts row is already `UNRESOLVED` because *"counterparties may have change-of-control or key-person clauses; **unknown until reviewed**."* **Every month of informal activity is a month in which reputation attaches to a name nobody is documented to own.**

**[P]** Nine steps. **This is a process recommendation, not a structure recommendation.** It is derived from **[S-F: XXV.4]**, **[S-F: VII.2]**, **[S-F: Rule F-8]**, **[S-ST: §26]** and **[S-A: §24.10]**.

| **2** | **Disclose `UE-24`** — supply Krytos's form, constitutive documents, objects and ownership to counsel | Founder/CEO | A document set, not a decision | **[S-A: A-V9]** *"Krytos governing documents"* |
| **3** | **Name the funding source for Phase 0 and Phase 1** (`UE-12`), including this engagement's fees | CEO + Finance | A recorded decision; **no amount is required by this paper and none is invented** | **[S-B: E-03]**; **[S-F: BLK-D-07]** |
| **4** | **Commission V-01, V-04 and V-05 as one engagement**, with Part XIX's briefs and Parts III–XVIII as annexes | Founder/CEO | The engagement letter | **[S-F: XXV.4 Action 1]** |
| **5** | **Put `GC-01` to counsel explicitly**, and to the decision-maker: can the chosen form carry waterfall priorities 4 and 8, or must the question be referred as a reserved matter? | Founder/CEO + V-01 + V-04 | A written answer, held as governance evidence | **GC-01**; **[S-D: §50.5 PC-8]** |
| **6** | **Commission V-06 (insurance, `L1–L15`) and V-09 (intervention boundary)** in parallel — they are independent of the structure and are the corpus's Action 2 | National Legal + Finance + CEO | Written answers, held as governance evidence | **[S-F: XXV.4 Action 2]**; **[S-A: A-B2, A-B3]** |
| **7** | **Receive the opinions; close the option set** — counsel excludes what is unavailable (which may remove OPT-D…OPT-G), confirms what is available, and states the consequence of each | V-01, V-04, V-05, V-09, V-10, V-11 | A **Part IV matrix with its `PV` and `UK` cells replaced by professional answers** — **Rule G-21** | **[S-F: PAK-02]** |
| **8** | **Decide `MD-01`**, record it with reason, decider and date, and immediately decide `MD-02`, `MD-03`, `MD-04` which it unblocks | Founder/CEO | **A written decision plus counsel opinion**; then the mandate document, the fiduciary appointment | **[S-F: SR-01, SR-02, SR-03, SR-05]**; **[S-F: BLK-C-11]** |
| **9** | **Build `BLK-C-01` and `BLK-C-06`, then work STEPS 5–13 of the master path to `GATE P0`** | Founder/CEO with the relevant role-holders | Certificate, objects clause, register of officers; mandate document; custody register; then the remaining P0 evidence | **[S-F: VII.2; Part V; Part VIII]** |

**[P]** **Two process rules carried forward:**
- **[S-F: Rule F-9]** — *"A `PROPOSED` item is not a decision … approval is an **authorised act by a named human authority**, not an automatic consequence of documentation."* **259 such acts are outstanding across the corpus; this paper adds none to the completed column.**
- **[S-F: Rule F-2]** — *"Completeness is not readiness."* **This paper is complete for its purpose. It is not a legal person.**

# PART XXIV — FINAL VERDICT

## XXIV.1 The verdict

> # **B — DECISION-READY WITH MATERIAL GAPS**

**[P]** Exactly one verdict is returned, as the brief requires. **The four options and the test applied to each:**

| Grade | Meaning | Applies? |
|---|---|---|
| **A — DECISION-READY** | The corpus and the questions are sufficiently specified for authorised human decision-makers and qualified professionals | **NO.** Four material gaps remain (§XXIV.3), and **one of them (`UE-24`) is an input no analysis can supply** — the decision is *"legal structure **under Krytos**"* and Krytos's own form is unstated |
| **B — DECISION-READY WITH MATERIAL GAPS** | The questions can be put and a decision approached, but named inputs are missing | **YES** |
| **C — NOT DECISION-READY — INSUFFICIENT SOURCE INFORMATION** | The source does not contain enough to frame the decision | **NO.** The corpus names the options in three independent lists, states the advantages and risks of four of them in a tabulated source table, identifies the validators by discipline, sequences the decision first, classifies it as a BLOCKER and a Tier 0 item, maps eleven of twenty-eight dependency chains to it, specifies its decision windows, and states the evidence that would mark it decided. **The framing is unusually complete** |
| **D — NOT DECISION-READY — ARCHITECTURE INSUFFICIENTLY SPECIFIED** | The architecture cannot support a decision | **NO.** The four pillars are specified in operational detail; the money architecture is `APPROVED`; the account classes, fund restrictions, control requirements, role families, participation levels and audit machinery are all specified. **What is missing is legal, not architectural** |

## XXIV.2 Why B — the precise reasoning

**[A]** Five propositions, each sourced, which together yield **B** and no other grade.

**1. The decision can be put.** **[S-F: Q19]** specifies the artifact and its required contents, and every one of them is delivered: the options (Part III), what each permits and prohibits for the four pillars (Part IV), the 12A application-of-income and political-activity conditions, 80G and FCRA timing (Part XV), worker-classification consequences per option (Part VIII), objects-clause requirements (§V.2), dissolution and restructuring constraints (Part XVIII), and the brand and IP consequence of each (Part XVII). **[S-F: SR-01]**'s next action — *"Produce the entity-structure decision paper for counsel"* — is discharged.

**2. The decision-maker, the validators and the evidence standard are all identified by the corpus.** Owner: **Founder/CEO** **[S: Ch.33; S-F: SR-01]**. Validators: **V-01, V-04, V-05** ◆ pre-pilot, plus **V-06…V-11** **[S-F: Part III]**. Evidence of decision: *"**A written decision plus counsel opinion**"* **[S-F: SR-01]**; *"**A written opinion held as governance evidence**"* **[S-F: PAK-02]**; and on build, *"**Certificate, objects clause, register of officers**"* **[S-F: BLK-C-01]**. **A decision with a named decider, named advisors and a named evidence standard is decidable.**

**3. But four inputs are missing, and none can be supplied by analysis.**

| # | Gap | Why it is material | Why no document can close it |
|---|---|---|---|
| **GAP-1** | **`GC-01` — the unreconciled conflict between the `APPROVED` waterfall and the nonprofit risk note.** Priorities 4 (*founder compensation*) and 8 (*residual surplus / founder upside*) are `APPROVED ARCHITECTURE` and the priority order is a **reserved matter**; **[S: App. A §3.2]** says a nonprofit form carries *"restrictions on profit distribution and founder upside"* and *"may not fit commercial waterfall without separate structure"*; **[S-D: §50.5]** records that whether the waterfall conflicts with an application-of-income requirement is *"`CANNOT BE ASSESSED`."* **No instrument joins these statements** | It determines whether OPT-A/B/C are available at all **without** either a separation or an amendment of an `APPROVED`, reserved instrument. **It could change the option set, not merely rank it** | It is a **choice between three routes** — pick a form that accommodates the waterfall, separate the activity, or refer a reserved matter — and the third route requires an apex whose authority is `LDR-U01 UNRESOLVED`. **Only the owner under [S: Ch.4] can choose** |
| **GAP-2** | **`UE-24` — Krytos's own legal form, jurisdiction, governing documents, objects and ownership are nowhere stated.** `LDR-U08` is *"legal structure **under Krytos**"*; **OPT-I** is a parent/operating structure; **[S-A: §11.2]** says *"the entity's control follows **Krytos's own succession, which is outside this architecture entirely**"* and *"**Whoever owns Krytos owns the operating system of the institution**"*; **[S-A: A-14 S5]** says *"**Krytos's governing documents take effect in parallel [V]**"*; **[S-A: A-V9]** lists *"Krytos governing documents"* as an open question | **The parent term of the relation is undefined.** OPT-D, OPT-H, OPT-I and OPT-J J-2 cannot be evaluated against a parent whose form is unknown; and **GC-02** (whether an internal-division pilot satisfies P0-A) cannot be resolved without it | It is a **fact about the world**, held by one person. **[S-A: A-12]** is `ANALYSIS ONLY` and could not obtain it; no instrument in six could |
| **GAP-3** | **Three option categories are absent from the corpus.** `LLP`, `partnership` and `proprietorship` return **zero occurrences**; *"private limited company"* is never named, only *"company"* (§I.3). **48 of the matrix's differentiated cells are therefore unevaluable, and OPT-D is only partly grounded** | **The option set cannot be closed.** Counsel may find one of these forms available and appropriate, or may exclude all three — and until they do, Part IV and Part XX are incomplete **by construction, not by omission** | Only a qualified Indian professional can say whether a form is **legally available** for a given purpose. **Rule G-2 forbids inferring availability from silence** |
| **GAP-4** | **`LDR-U17` — the economic blueprint was never attached**, so **no option's cost can be weighed.** **[S-B: OC-22]** records `C_comp` as *"depends entirely on entity form [V]"* with **no amount**; **[S-B: §8.6]** records that *"the centre is funded last and spent first"* with *"no instrument, amount, cap or duration"*; **[S-B: §46.2]** returns **D — NOT YET ECONOMICALLY VIABLE**; **[S-F: XIV.3 Finding 1]** records that *"the architecture has no funding source"* | **A structure whose compliance load cannot be weighed against any capacity to bear it is being chosen blind on the one dimension the founder can most easily judge.** **[S-B: NL-5]** warns obligations *"attach at thresholds, not smoothly"* — step-change costs with no estimate | The corpus **deliberately** refuses to invent numbers (**[S-A: A-L1]**: *"the source deliberately does not invent numbers; pilot evidence should set them"*). **A decision paper may not supply what six instruments declined to** |

**4. The gaps are inputs to a decision, not obstacles to defining one.** **GAP-1** is a question that can be put to counsel and to the owner **today**. **GAP-2** is a disclosure one person can make **today**. **GAP-3** is a question counsel answers **in the first opinion**. **GAP-4** is partially closable — counsel can state each form's compliance burden qualitatively even where the corpus cannot price it — and is expressly the pilot's own product for the rest. **None requires new architecture, new theory, new evidence about the world, or a decision that the corpus forbids.**

**5. The corpus's own verdicts are consistent with B on this question.** **[S-F: XXV.1]** returns **C — PILOT NOT READY, BUT THE PATH IS FINITE** on the question *"may the pilot be authorised?"* — and its reason 1 is *"**The organisation has no legal person**."* **[S-B: §46.2]** returns **D — NOT YET ECONOMICALLY VIABLE** on the question *"can a team survive six months?"* **[S-C]**, **[S-D]** and **[S-E]** each return **D — NOT YET PROVEN**. **Those are answers to different questions.** On the question this paper asks — *"are the entity options and the determinations required before selecting one sufficiently specified to be put to professionals and to an authorised decision-maker?"* — the answer is **yes, with four named gaps.** **[S-F: XXV.4 Action 1]** is itself the corpus's judgement that the decision is approachable: it names this paper as the first of five actions that would move **C → B**.

## XXIV.3 What would move B → A

**[P]** Four inputs, in the order they can be obtained. **Each is a single act by a single owner. None requires new architecture.**

| # | Input | Owner | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Disclosure of Krytos's legal form, constitutive documents, objects and ownership** (**UE-24 / GAP-2**) | Founder/CEO | Closes the parent term of *"legal structure under Krytos"*; makes OPT-D, OPT-H, OPT-I and J-2 evaluable; resolves **GC-02** |
| **2** | **A human decision on `GC-01`** — whether waterfall priorities 4 and 8 are to be preserved in the chosen form, separated into another person, or referred as a reserved matter (**GAP-1**) | Founder/CEO, on V-01 and V-04 advice | Determines whether OPT-A/B/C remain in the set unchanged, or enter it only with a separation |
| **3** | **Counsel's confirmation of which of OPT-D…OPT-G are legally available** for any of the four functions (**GAP-3**) | V-01 corporate counsel | **Closes the option set** (Rule G-17), replaces 48 `UK` cells, and makes Part IV and Part XX complete |
| **4** | **The economic blueprint, or an explicit recorded decision that the pilot will produce it** (**`LDR-U17` / GAP-4**), together with counsel's qualitative statement of each form's compliance burden | Founder/CEO with Finance, and V-03 | Makes the affordability dimension weighable — the one dimension on which a founder can presently form a judgement without measurement |

**[A]** **Inputs 1 and 2 are free and can be supplied this month. Input 3 is the first deliverable of the engagement [S-F: XXV.4 Action 1] already requires. Input 4 is partly free (a recorded decision) and partly the pilot's own product.** **A grade of A is therefore not distant — but it is not available to this instrument, because three of the four inputs are acts of a person and one is an act of a professional.**

## XXIV.4 What this verdict does not say

**[A]** Six negatives, stated so that the verdict cannot be over-read:

1. **It does not say any option is suitable.** Rule G-1.
2. **It does not say the decision is easy, cheap or quick.** **[S: App. A §31]** classifies the effort as a `Blocker`; **[S-ST: §26 item 1]** rates the effort **H** (high); **[S-F: BLK-D-07]** records that the fees are the most-overlooked pre-pilot cost and **UE-12** records that nobody has said who pays them.
3. **It does not say the pilot may proceed.** **[S-F: XXV.1]** returns **C — PILOT NOT READY**, and **Part XXII** preserves that in full. **This verdict is about the decidability of one question, not about readiness for anything.**
4. **It does not resolve any of the thirty-two items in Part II.** Every one remains at its source status.
5. **It does not supply any of the four missing inputs.** They are named, not filled.
6. **It does not convert a `PROPOSED` instrument into a decision.** **[S-F: Rule F-9]**: **259 authorised acts remain outstanding**, and this paper is not one of them.

## XXIV.5 The closing statement

**[A]** Six instruments and 3,868,158 bytes produced a complete description of an organisation that does not exist. **This is the seventh, and it describes a decision that has not been made.**

The corpus was disciplined about the entity question in a way that is worth stating plainly, because it is the reason this paper could be written at all. **v1.1 named four options and refused to choose between them. The stress test named three more, explained why one entity would serve none of the four pillars well, and ranked the decision first among fourteen pre-launch changes. v1.3-A turned the refusal into twelve registered unresolved items and an asset-by-asset map of what one person currently holds. v1.3-B showed that the money architecture has no legal subject at any node. v1.3-C asked whether an unincorporated network may hold a record. v1.3-D showed that the reserved matters have no holder and that the interim procedure's own legality depends on the answer. v1.3-E showed that the entity constrains what may lawfully be said, and that the brand is owned by a person while the identity must not be a person. And v1.3-F consolidated all of it, ranked the entity decision first among the five biggest blockers, made it STEP 1 of a thirteen-step path, and named this document as the next artifact.**

**Seven instruments, and not one of them chose a structure.** That is not a failure of the corpus. **It is the corpus obeying its own rule** — **[S: App. A §3.5]**: the National Team *"cannot … finalise legal structure"*, and the Founder/CEO cannot approve alone *"final legal/tax conclusions without professionals."* **The refusal is the architecture working.**

What the corpus could not do, and what this paper therefore does, is **make the refusal operable.** An unresolved decision that nobody has scheduled is a risk. An unresolved decision with a named decider, eleven named validators, seventy-seven consolidated questions, a specified option set, a mapped consequence set, a stated evidence standard, four named gaps and a sequence is **a decision waiting to be made.**

> **[A]** The organisation's most protected obligation is the payment of a worker for verified work. **[S: Ch.18]** puts it first. **[S-D: INV-2]** makes it non-suspendable by a dispute, a process, a gate or a score. **[S-A: A-14 §13.6]** permits an interim authority to do anything necessary *"to keep paying workers for verified work."* And today it cannot be paid — not because the architecture is weak, but because **there is nobody to pay it from.**
>
> **That is the whole subject of this paper.** Everything in Parts I to XXIII exists to make one sentence possible: *a person, advised by qualified professionals, choosing a legal form, on a recorded date, for a recorded reason.*
>
> **First make the institution legally and operationally decidable. Then decide. Then validate. Then implement. Then pilot.**

**Verdict: `B — DECISION-READY WITH MATERIAL GAPS.`**
**Gaps: `GC-01` · `UE-24` · the availability of `OPT-D…OPT-G` · `LDR-U17`.**
**Next act: an authorised human decision-maker commissions V-01, V-04 and V-05 as one engagement, and discloses Krytos's own position.**
**This instrument is not that act.**

## END OF INSTRUMENT

**v1.3-G — Entity Structure, Legal Person, Governance & Fiduciary Decision Paper.**

**162 provenance rows (EP-01…EP-162) across nine instrument groups · 41 brief-specified search terms plus 30 extension terms, with eight negative findings recorded · 4 citation clarifications (GPD-1…GPD-4) and 6 conflicts (GC-01…GC-06), none resolved · 32 unresolved entity items (UE-01…UE-32), 9 of them ⟨new — v1.3-G⟩ · 10 structure options (OPT-A…OPT-J) with 7 sub-options under J, the set left open by Rule G-17 · a 33 × 10 compatibility matrix (330 cells: 253 PV, 27 UK, 22 PC, 22 PR, 6 SS) with all 50 differentiated cells anchored to verbatim source text · 24 charitable-side questions · 22 commercial-side questions and an 11-node commercial chain · 6 conceptual multi-entity architectures with 10 validation preconditions and 10 architectural constraints · 10 worker/role categories × 10 dimensions (100 cells) and 26 classification questions for labour counsel · 24 banking and money-control items with the A-N16 blocker preserved in full at §IX.7 · 14 governance questions × 5 structure families, all returning UNKNOWN — REQUIRES GOVERNANCE COUNSEL · 11 succession scenarios × 7 continuity dimensions (77 cells: 39 ⛔, 27 [P], 6 [S] designed, 5 [V]) · 16 liability exposures · 12 insurance categories as broker/counsel questions only · 40 tax and regulatory questions in 6 groups · 12A/80G/FCRA analysed per option with 5 eligibility-affecting activity classes · 12 data classes with the fiduciary question preserved as UNRESOLVED · 14 IP and brand assets across 7 ownership positions · 9 dissolution dimensions × 5 option groups · 9 professional validation briefs cross-walked to 77 corpus validation items in 17 categories · a 12-dimension neutral decision matrix with no weighting, no score and no winner · 10 minimum decisions specified UNRESOLVED → DECIDABLE → DECIDED, plus 4 that must travel with them · 56 pilot-consequence items in 5 classes · a 9-section decision paper for human approval · 49 operating rules (G-1…G-49).**

**Status: `DECISION PAPER — PROPOSED FOR HUMAN DECISION`.**
**Verdict: `B — DECISION-READY WITH MATERIAL GAPS.`**
**Gaps: `GC-01` · `UE-24` · the availability of `OPT-D…OPT-G` · `LDR-U17`.**
**Next act: an authorised human decision-maker commissions V-01, V-04 and V-05 as one engagement, and discloses Krytos's own position.**

**Nothing in this document is an approval, a decision, a validation, a legal opinion, a tax opinion, an employment opinion, an insurance opinion, a privacy opinion, a structure selection or a finding of readiness. No unresolved question is resolved. No conflict is reconciled. No number, threshold, budget, target, rate, ratio, premium, professional fee, retention period, signatory count, paid-up capital, coverage, exemption, eligibility or ownership arrangement is invented. No successor is named. No recommendation is converted into a decision. No source "recommendation status" label is adopted. No assumption is converted into a fact. No permission to operate informally is granted. No architectural rule is treated as evidence of real-world effectiveness. No source ID is replaced, renumbered or retired.**

**This instrument converts `LDR-U08` from `UNRESOLVED` to `DECIDABLE`. It does not convert it to `DECIDED`, and it cannot: a decision paper is not a decision.**

**WRITTEN ARCHITECTURE IS NOT OPERATIONAL READINESS. OPERATIONAL READINESS IS NOT PILOT EVIDENCE. PILOT EVIDENCE IS NOT INSTITUTIONAL PROOF. AND A DECISION PAPER IS NOT A DECISION.**

**FIRST MAKE THE INSTITUTION LEGALLY AND OPERATIONALLY DECIDABLE. THEN DECIDE. THEN VALIDATE. THEN IMPLEMENT. THEN PILOT.**

**Prepared 5 October 2026.**
